**The skeptic's corrected fix.** On line 697, which is outside the fence so the digest does not move, write: "(the UTF-8 bytes, no BOM, of the lines between the markers, each ending LF)".

Put the same definition next to the preregistered digest in COMPLETED.md §791. Compute that digest from raw bytes, either `<:raw` with Digest::SHA or sha256sum over the LF index blob, and never through a decoding layer such as perl `-CSD` or `:encoding(UTF-8)`, or PowerShell 5.1's Get-Content or Set-Content.

Optional extra safety, only before the digest is fixed: make the fence fully ASCII by replacing "(§791)" on line 701, for example with "(section 791)". Then UTF-8, Latin-1 and cp1252 pipelines all agree. The stated encoding is still needed, because UTF-16 would still differ.

### 52. The instrument cell for 2016 rows is unspecified

- **Lens:** consistency - **reviewer:** note - **skeptic:** note
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:300

**The scenario.** The saved 115th tab also calls Minnesota's 2016 block splits cosmetic. The design names only Minnesota 2018 as 'cosmetic' and '-' where no saved page says, so MN 2016's cell is left to the implementer. Nothing decides on it.

**The fix proposed.** State the 2016 rows' instrument values.

**The skeptic's evidence.** The finding holds. The gap it names is real, but nothing depends on it.

1. The design leaves the cell open. Staged plan line 300 defines the column as "`instrument` (the redraw's instrument as `house_districts.md` reading 8 names it - a court-ordered plan, an act; Minnesota 2018's change "cosmetic"; "-" where no saved page says)".

2. The column has 2016 redraw rows to fill. ElectionsData/usa/house_maps.csv has four of them, each with lines_changed = 1:
   - 2016,FL,27,1
   - 2016,MN,8,1
   - 2016,NC,13,1
   - 2016,VA,11,1
   The tool writes map rows for 2016 to 2026 (Tools/us_house_prep.pl: `my @map_years = (2016, 2018, ...)`).

3. A saved page does describe Minnesota's 2016 change. The 115th tab (raw/maps/census_rdo_congressional_districts_115th.html, a visible <p>) says "Four states (Florida, Minnesota, North Carolina, and Virginia) delineated new boundaries for the 2016 election cycle." It also says "The state of Minnesota provided splits in their 115th and 116th Congressional District plans. None of the Minnesota block splits changed population allocations as they were cosmetic in nature." The 116th tab repeats this. Reading 8 cites both tabs: "(Minnesota's block splits, the 115th's and 116th's tabs say, "were cosmetic in nature" ...)". It also says "The 2016, 2018, 2022 and 2026 instruments were not read".

4. So MN 2016 fits none of the definition's branches:
   - Reading 8 names no 2016 instrument (no court-ordered plan, no act).
   - "cosmetic" is given only for Minnesota 2018.
   - "-" applies only where no saved page says, and the 115th tab does say.
   One implementer would write "cosmetic", reading the saved page and following the 2018 parallel. Another would write "-", taking the 2018-only list as complete. FL, NC and VA 2016 are "-" under the general clause either way.

5. The panel's spec already had this gap. Its method_a says "'cosmetic' for MN 2018; '-' where no saved page says, which covers CO and PA 2018, GA 2024 and every 2022 and 2026 state". None of the four 2016 rows is mentioned, so the plan's condensation inherited the omission and did not cause it.

6. Nothing reads the cell:
   - The fence never mentions the column, so the preregistered SHA-256 is unaffected.
   - The fence's held test ("its lines changed at no election after B up to T") makes C1 (2016->2018) read the 2018 rows, never the 2016 ones.
   - Line 300 says nothing decides on the column.
   - Every place that prints an instrument (the outputs, the per-state table, the decision table) covers a measured cycle's redrawn rows only, which are 2018 to 2024.
   - Play reads 2024 and 2026.
   Two implementations would differ in one generated CSV cell and so in the catalog digest stamped on the block. They would not differ in any outcome, decisive digest or printed figure.

This is wording outside the fence and can be fixed without an amendment, so "note" is the right grade.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Plan line 300 sits outside the fence, so changing it leaves the rule's digest as it is. In the `instrument` parenthesis, replace `Minnesota 2018's change "cosmetic"` with `Minnesota's 2016 and 2018 changes "cosmetic" (the 115th and 116th tabs say it of both plans)`. The general clause `"-" where no saved page says` then stays true and covers FL, NC and VA 2016.

The other option is to declare the 2016 rows "-" because no cycle reads them. That would conflict with the clause "where no saved page says", because the 115th tab does say. The first option is better.

### 53. The plan drops the spec's 'printed, not scored' for (a)'s NC-9 2018 call

- **Lens:** consistency - **reviewer:** note - **skeptic:** note
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:311

**The scenario.** NC is held in C1, so (a) calls NC-9 2018, but the truth has no winner there. The measure 'the districts called wrong, named' can list it as wrong in one implementation and skip it in another. The spec says the call is printed, not scored.

**The fix proposed.** Add: '(a)'s NC-9 2018 call printed, not scored'.

**The skeptic's evidence.** The finding is right that the sentence was dropped, but it cannot change anything the rule decides. It is a wording gap in a list that is only printed, and it sits outside the fence.

Premise confirmed from the catalog:
- North Carolina is held in C1. house_maps.csv has `2016,NC,13,1` and `2018,NC,13,0`, so the fence's held test (lines 80-82) holds it.
- NC-9 2016 has a share (`2016,NC,9,...,193452,139041,R`), so (a) calls NC-9 for 2018. Recomputed from the catalog, the call is REP.
- The truth row is `2018,NC,9,0,0,...,-,N`: no winner and no votes.
- The spec's truth field, under SCORING BY INTERVAL, says "(a)'s NC-9 call is printed, not scored." A grep of the staged plan finds no such sentence and no "not scored".

Why it cannot change the rule:
- "Printed" is already covered. Line 301's output is "every held district's swung share and call", and line 314 names NC-9 among the exceptions.
- Every scored figure already handles the undecided seat by interval:
  - Lines 756-764: "Z_s is a state's undecided seats (NC-9 2018)", W_s is "the distance of REPhat_s from [R_s, R_s + Z_s]", N is the distance from [R, R + Z], and K counts a class right "under some resolution".
  - On the record, (a)'s other 12 North Carolina calls are all right, so W_NC is 0 whether NC-9 is called REP or DEM.
- The only exposed spot is line 311's "the districts called wrong, named with their margins and split into 'the swing flipped it, the record did not' and the reverse".
  - Line 321: "Only the figures the rule names decide; everything else informs Elias."
  - It is not pinned (line 319) and not in the decisive digest (line 843: "Nothing printed only is in it").
  - It is in US-11's Design block, not between the R-US18 markers, so it is outside the hashed fence.

The path the finding describes does exist. A literal `call != winner` check (winner '-') lists NC-9 2018 as called wrong. The base winner is R and the call is R, so the swing did not flip it. The split therefore files it under "the record flipped it, the swing did not", with a 0-0 margin of record. That contradicts line 304 ("NC-9 2018, undecided") and the interval convention, so a careful builder would not do it. But the plan, which outlives the scratchpad spec, no longer says so outright.

Caution on the finding's own fix: bare "not scored" could be read as leaving the call out of (a)'s North Carolina count. That breaks the fence's W: with 12 seats, W = (0 + 0 - 1)/2 is negative and not an integer. It also trips line 323's data failure, "not summing to 435".

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Add this to US-11's Design block at line 311, outside the R-US18 markers, so no digest is involved. After "...and the reverse;" insert: "(a)'s NC-9 2018 call (C1) is printed with every held district's call and never listed as called wrong, since it has no winner of record; it still counts in (a)'s North Carolina total, which W, N and K score by interval;". Do not add a bare "printed, not scored" anywhere near the fence's scoring, because it reads as dropping the seat from REPhat_s.

### 54. The panel's premise reasons, rationale and attack record exist only in the scratchpad

- **Lens:** consistency - **reviewer:** note - **skeptic:** note
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:697

**The scenario.** CLAUDE.md line 74 is a standing rule: a design a later session depends on is written to the repo, never a scratchpad. The staged change carries the rules but not P1-P20's reasons, the rationale, or the accepted and rejected attacks. Two of those explain fence choices: R2 (why reading (ii)'s stops are printed only) and A18 (the no-change carry). All of this lives in final_spec.json in the scratchpad.

**The fix proposed.** Carry them into COMPLETED.md §791 or the plan in this commit.

**The skeptic's evidence.** Partly true but overstated, and it does not breach the wording of CLAUDE.md:74. Line numbers are in the staged docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md.

(1) What the rule covers. CLAUDE.md:74 (§643) asks for "premises, a plan put to Elias, the rulings it answers - into the spec". The staged plan has all three:
- the premises' statements, in the fence (699-860) and in US-11's Design (298-344);
- the six blind choices (865-870);
- the ruling record (862).
§643's own precedent (POLITICAL_SYSTEM_SPEC.md 118-148) wrote the formateur's nine premises as bare statements, with no reasons and no record of the deliberation.

(2) Much of the list is already in the plan.
- The rationale's "from the game's use" and P5's reason against re-solving are at 299.
- P9's reason for S5 is at 328 ("which is why S5 asks rather than switches") and 770.
- The consistency and noise-floor reasoning is at 331.
- The reasons for P4, P7, P8, P10, P16 and P20 are in the six choices and the cycles bullet (305).
- P19's reason is at 322.

(3) R2 is the wrong example. The fence already gives its reason at 802: "S2, S3 and S6 are printed, since each would rest on one swing cycle". That is final_spec's STEP 6 (ii) minus "which the rule forbids".

(4) What exists only in final_spec.json:
- the 22 accepted and 4 rejected attacks, with how each was checked;
- the rationale's BLINDNESS paragraph: the estimates folder was not opened, vote shares were summed to check P4, and "an illustrative rounding with an assumed, unfitted rho" was sketched. This is the evidence behind 697's "wrote it blind";
- the sentence "R-US14 supplies the sum and the tie to (a); R-US15 supplies 'not above'";
- the reasons for P6, P11 (a census redistricting resets maps by statute) and P15;
- the no-change invariant (point 5).
The stated plan for §791 covers only derived figures and "what the panel saw" (697). Everything above would be lost with the scratchpad.

(5) A18 is the right example, and it matters. spec.measures says the carry is made "so every W_s stays an integer and the benchmark meets the apportionment assertion". The fence (754-755) keeps only "Where a state's seats changed", and its TERMS read a state's seats from house_maps.csv. Take North Carolina in C2:
- its seats do not change: house_maps.csv has 2018 NC 13 and 2020 NC 13;
- B has only 12 decided seats: house_districts.csv row 2018,NC,9 has winner '-' and flag N, leaving 9 R and 3 D, against 2020's 8 R and 5 D.
Read literally, no change gives NC 9-3. Then W_NC = (|9-8| + |3-5|)/2 = 1.5 and the national total is 434. Line 323 ("a method's output ... not summing to 435") fails the instrument if no change counts as a method. Read by the dropped invariant, P(13, 0.75) = 10, W = 2 and the total is 435.

No change decides only in S3. On the record S3 is dormant, because C2 is the only forward swing cycle. Once C5 is read, C2 and C5 make it live, and a one-seat difference in N could flip S3's third condition.

Verdict: real, as a provenance note. The NC reading is a separate, minor ambiguity in the fence.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Do not paste the reasons into the plan. Several of them carry transcribed figures: P2's seven winners, P4's swings, the district counts in the rationale, and the attacks' checks. CLAUDE.md:41 keeps those out of docs/specs, and CLAUDE.md:67's short form keeps bulk out of §791.

(1) Commit the panel's output verbatim, the way §790 committed its review ("every report below is verbatim", Reviews/2026-10-06_s790_us11_house_districts.md). For example, write a file from final_spec.json holding the spec, the accepted attacks and the rejected attacks. §791 points to that file and quotes the BLINDNESS paragraph next to its account of what the panel saw.

(2) Before the digest is fixed at this commit, put the invariant back into the fence. For example: "No change: B's seats and delegations. Where B's decided seats in a state differ from n_T - its seats changed, or B left a seat undecided (NC-9 2018) - B's REP share of its decided seats is carried onto n_T by P and the half rule, so every W_s is an integer and the row sums to 435."

§791 should say that naming NC-9 is a reading of the panel's invariant. The panel's own attack (A18) was about C3's apportionment changes.

### 55. Ask 1's stated reason is false: a 2024-start game's first House election is 5 Nov 2024, not a midterm

- **Lens:** style-claims - **reviewer:** defect - **skeptic:** defect
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:865

**The scenario.** Ask 1 defends 'D with C1' as 'the kind of election a 2024-start game meets first'. The plan's own text contradicts it. In play (line 324): 'B is the previous House election - the record's 2022 for 5 Nov 2024'. The game's range (line 318) covers 5 Nov 2024 and 3 Nov 2026. So the first House election is a presidential-year one, with five states redrawn. C1's kind (a midterm on mostly held lines) is what the game meets on 3 Nov 2026, as line 305 says and as the spec's cycles field says ('meets next: 3 Nov 2026'). 'First' comes from P16's reason. Elias, answering blind, would confirm the default on a false premise about the game's first election.

**The fix proposed.** Use line 305's wording: '... the kind of election a 2024-start game meets on 3 Nov 2026 ...'.

**The skeptic's evidence.** The finding holds. Read naturally, the sentence is false, and the plan's own text contradicts it.

Line 865 of the staged plan, ask 1, reads: "D with C1 (2016→2018) - the record's only large swing on mostly held lines and its only midterm on which (a) runs, the kind of election a 2024-start game meets first - by default".

The plan shows the first House election of a 2024-start game is a presidential-year one, not a midterm:
- Line 382 (US-16): "On the presidential polling day the game also elects the House, by R-US18's method on US-11's districts ... so under §618's ruling 4 a 2024-start game elects its own 119th Congress." Its Done-when (line 384): "a fresh world on the eve of 5 Nov 2024 seats a 119th".
- Line 324 (In play): "(US-16 on the presidential day; US-27 every even year) ... B is the previous House election - the record's 2022 for 5 Nov 2024, then the game's own".
- The start is the run-up before 5 Nov 2024 (line 85; 12 Mar 2024 at lines 240, 261, 462). Midterms join only at US-27 (line 459: "adds every even year"), whose diagnostic "holds 3 Nov 2026" (line 462).
- So the first House count the method runs is 5 Nov 2024. It uses 2022's record as its base, on 2024's lines, in a presidential year. That is C4's setup (2022→2024), not C1's. The spec's method_a lists "5 Nov 2024: AL, GA, LA, NY and NC" as redrawn. The plan's reference chain also has a world that follows history count 2024 "at 2024's V" (line 318), which is C4's near-zero swing.

The same commit says it correctly elsewhere. Line 305 reads: "C1 joins D as the record's only large swing on mostly held lines and its only midterm on which (a) runs - the kind of election a 2024-start game meets on 3 Nov 2026 -". So the plan contradicts itself.

The panel's spec has the same split. Its cycles field says "That is the kind of election a 2024-start game meets next: 3 Nov 2026." P16's reason says "C1 holds the record's largest swing and is a midterm, the kind of election a 2024-start game meets first." Line 305 copied the cycles field. Ask 1 copied P16's slip, so copying the spec faithfully does not make the claim true.

Scope:
- The sentence is outside the fence. The markers are at lines 699 and 860 of the staged blob, and the fence holds no such wording. The digest and what the rule decides are therefore unaffected.
- It is still a false claim, and it sits in the stated reason for the default of a choice Elias answers blind.
- The scale counts "is false" as a defect, so the severity stays at defect.

The other reading, "C1's kind (mid-decade lines) comes before any census redistricting", is equally true of C2 and C4. It cannot be what this sentence means as an argument for C1.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** At line 865, replace "the kind of election a 2024-start game meets first" with "the kind of election a 2024-start game meets on 3 Nov 2026". This is line 305's wording and the spec cycles field's "meets next: 3 Nov 2026". Optionally add "(its first, 5 Nov 2024, is a presidential year on C4's base and lines)" so Elias sees the contrast. The edit is outside the R-US18 fence, so the digest does not move. When COMPLETED.md §791 is written, it should not quote P16's reason ("meets first") unchanged; it should note the correction.

### 56. Ask 2 calls '213 of 435' the Clerk's figure, but the Clerk's own table prints 212 and a vacancy; it is also a derived seat count

- **Lens:** style-claims - **reviewer:** defect - **skeptic:** defect
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:866

**The scenario.** The Clerk's 2020 volume (raw/returns/clerk_statistics2020.pdf, party-division table, read with pdftotext -raw) prints the 117th House as 435: Democrats 222, Republicans 212, Vacant 1. That is the same as [HH-DIV]. The 213 is the catalog's count of district pluralities on the Clerk's figures (house_districts.md reading 5: 'the districts count 213 Republicans where the division prints 212 of 434'). So the blind ask sets 'the Clerk's 213' against '[HH-DIV]'s 212' when both publications print 212. The real choice is the district plurality against the published division. 213 is also a derived seat count typed into the plan (the claim convention; this commit's condensation keeps seat counts out), it appears nowhere else in the plan, and it goes stale if R-K9's re-read corrects a 2020 row.

**The fix proposed.** '*2020's truth.* The district plurality on the Clerk's counts, NY-22 to the winner its count names (house_districts.md reading 5) ... by default; or the division as published - [HH-DIV]'s and the Clerk's own table's - 212 of 434, NY-22 vacant.' On line 304, 'the Clerk's winner' reads better as 'the winner on the Clerk's count'.

**The skeptic's evidence.** I could not refute it. Every factual part of the finding checks out against the saved pages.

1. The staged text matches the working copy (no unstaged diff). docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:866 reads: "2. *2020's truth.* The Clerk's 213 of 435, NY-22 to the winner its count names - ... by default; or `[HH-DIV]`'s 212 of 434, NY-22 undecided." It sits after the RULE END marker (line 860), so the digest is unaffected.

2. The Clerk's own volume prints 212. ElectionsData/usa/raw/returns/clerk_statistics2020.pdf, read with /mingw64/bin/pdftotext -raw (around text lines 5270-5363, volume p. 79), has the table "Political Divisions of the U.S. Senate and House of Representatives from the 40th Congress to the 117th Congress [ALL FIGURES REFLECT IMMEDIATE RESULTS OF ELECTIONS]". Its row is "117th .... 2021-2023 100 46 51 2 1 435 222 212 ...... 1": House 435 seats, D 222, R 212, other none, Vacant 1. The volume is "Published on FEBRUARY 26, 2021", after Tenney's certification on 8 Feb, and it still prints 212 plus a vacancy. No 2020 seat figure of 213 appears anywhere in it; grep -w 213 finds only vote figures.

3. [HH-DIV] (raw/executive/history_house_party_divisions.html) prints "117th (2021–2023) 435 [fn 6] 222 212 0". Footnote 6 says New York sent no certificate for the Twenty-Second District before the opening day. Both publications therefore print the same division: 212 R and one seat unassigned.

4. 213 is the catalog's count of district winners, not a published figure. house_districts.md reading 5 says "the districts count 213 Republicans where the division prints 212 of 434". The run line reads "House 2020: the districts' winners R 213 D 222 ... [HH-DIV] R 212 D 222, NY-22 vacant".

5. The project's own usage makes the label read as a published seat figure. ElectionsData/usa/returns_2024.md:31 cites "seats per the Clerk's 'Political Divisions… 119th Congress' table". Read that way, "the Clerk's 213" contradicts the Clerk's table for 2020. Set beside "[HH-DIV]'s 212", the ask presents the choice as a disagreement between two publications. The real choice is the plurality on the Clerk's counts against the published division, which both sources print. Ask 2 is the text Elias rules on blind, and a ruling either way is written into the fenced block by amendment, so the misframing can steer what the rule becomes.

6. The plan follows the panel faithfully, so this is inherited, not a transcription slip. The spec's P7 says "The 2020 truth is the Clerk's 213 of 435"; its truth field says "213 REP / 222 DEM of 435, by the Clerk"; facts.md:73 says "the Clerk's 213 of 435 or [HH-DIV]'s 212 of 434". The brief never mentions the Clerk's own division table. The fence is correct: line 756 says "The truth is the record's own district plurality (house_districts.csv's winner)" and line 799 says "(i) 2020's truth at [HH-DIV]: REP 212 of 434".

7. The 213 also breaks the claim convention. It is a derived count typed into the plan. CLAUDE.md allows a derived claim only as GENERATED, REFERENCED or DELETED ("Nobody transcribes"), and this commit's stated condensation keeps seat counts out. "213 of 435" appears only at line 866; line 296's "DEM 213" is 2022's [HH-DIV] figure. DocumentClaimCheck only checks `Type.Member` references, so no bar catches it.

Grading: defect, because the claim is false as a statement of where the figure comes from, in the text of a blind ruling. It changes nothing the fence computes and needs no amendment to fix. It must be fixed before the ask goes to Elias. Read loosely as "on the Clerk's counts", it would only be minor, but the publication-against-publication framing still misleads.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Line 866: "2. *2020's truth.* The district plurality on the Clerk's counts, NY-22 to the winner that count names (`house_districts.md` reading 5) - the game counts all 435 seats by plurality and models no certification - by default; or the division as published, NY-22 undecided: `[HH-DIV]`'s 212 of 434 (its footnote 6), which the Clerk's own Political Divisions table in the same 2020 volume also prints (212, one seat vacant)." This drops the derived 213 and points to the reading for it. It keeps the sourced 212 that reading (i) needs, and frames the choice as plurality against certification instead of one publication against another. The text is outside the markers, so the digest is unchanged and no amendment is needed. Optional wording note for line 304: "the Clerk's winner" could read "the winner on the Clerk's count".

### 57. Provenance overstated: the brief did not hold 'facts of record only', and the panel's two disclosed computations go unrecorded

- **Lens:** style-claims - **reviewer:** minor - **skeptic:** minor
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:697

**The scenario.** facts.md held more than facts of record. Besides them it held:
- the plan's own wording of R-US18, US-11 and US-16, R-US3's ruling and the R-US14, R-US15 and grain precedents (its §1);
- the game's constraints and a forecast ('a board by state on the election night, D-US, is likely', §2);
- approximations ('about 181 districts');
- the deliverables (§4);
- one 'fact' on no saved page: 'Pennsylvania's was a court-ordered new map'. The 116th tab says only that three states 'delineated new boundaries'; house_districts.md reading 8 says the 2018 instruments were not read; the spec's method_a flags it itself.

The brief also opened the whole repo to the panel (facts.md line 3). The rationale's BLINDNESS paragraph discloses two computations made from it:
- the national two-party shares on the deciding-count basis, summed from the catalog;
- 'an illustrative rounding with an assumed, unfitted rho' while weighing C4. That is a (c) seat computation, and the plan's 'told not to ... count any method's seats' leaves it unsaid.

US-11's line 298 says 'R-US18's entry and COMPLETED.md §791 say what the panel saw', but R-US18's entry says only what the panel was told and what its brief held.

**The fix proposed.** Reword to something like: 'its brief held the plan's question, the rulings it answers and facts of the record - one, Pennsylvania 2018's "court-ordered", on no saved page - and no estimate; it could read the repository, and COMPLETED.md §791 records the two computations it disclosed'. Then make §791 carry both of the BLINDNESS paragraph's disclosures.

**The skeptic's evidence.** CONFIRMED: the sentence is literally false.
- Staged plan line 697 sits outside the fence (BEGIN marker at 699, END at 860). It says: "The panel was told not to open them and not to count any method's seats; its brief held facts of record only (`COMPLETED.md` §791 says what it saw ...)".
- The brief holds at least two factual assertions that are on no saved page:
  1. facts.md line 61: "Pennsylvania's was a court-ordered new map". The saved 116th tab says only "Three states (Colorado, Minnesota, and Pennsylvania) delineated new boundaries for the 2018 election cycle". house_districts.md reading 8 says "The 2016, 2018, 2022 and 2026 instruments were not read". The spec's own method_a says "The facts sheet's 'court-ordered' for PA 2018 is on no saved page".
  2. facts.md line 64, which the reviewer missed: "Georgia renumbered: its 2024 6th comes mostly from the 2022 13th". No saved page says this: the 119th tab only lists Georgia among five states, the block equivalency files were not saved (reading 8), and grep finds it nowhere in ElectionsData/usa. The spec's method_a uses it anyway: "(Georgia's 2024 6th is mostly its 2022 13th)". The plan's fence rightly drops it ("A district number is never matched across a redraw.").
- The brief also holds material that is not facts at all: the plan's question and rulings (§1), the game's constraints and a forecast (line 30, "D-US, is likely"), and the deliverables (§4). Line 3 also opens the repo to the panel.
- So "facts of record only" overstates the brief, and the plan itself keeps both claims out.

REFUTED or unshown:
- "Told not to ... count any method's seats" is true as a statement of the instruction (facts.md line 3: "Do not compute any method's seat counts yourself either"). Line 697 claims what the panel was told, not that it complied.
- "Blind to rough scratch estimates" holds. The rationale says "the estimates folder was not opened", and the repo at 8f7b87fa held no seat-method estimate (COMPLETED §790 has none).
- Of the two BLINDNESS disclosures, the vote-share sum ("vote shares only") is inside the instruction. The other is "an illustrative rounding with an assumed, unfitted rho ... It decided nothing".
- "Go unrecorded" cannot be shown. §791 is not yet written, and both line 697 and line 298 defer "what it saw" to it.
- Line 298's "(R-US18's entry and `COMPLETED.md` §791 say what the panel saw)" is not false. R-US18's entry does say what the brief held and defers the rest to §791. That makes it a wording point.

SEVERITY: minor, not defect. By the rubric's letter a false sentence could count as a defect. But this one sits outside the digest-held fence, so it can be edited without amendment. It changes nothing the rule decides, and the property it vouches for (no estimate in the brief or the repo) still holds. It is a provenance overstatement.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Reword the clause on line 697, keeping the true "told not to" part. For example: "The panel was told not to open them and not to count any method's seats. Its brief held the plan's question, the rulings it answers, the game's constraints and facts of the record, and no estimate. Two of its phrases are on no saved page: Pennsylvania 2018's map 'court-ordered', and Georgia's 2024 6th 'mostly from the 2022 13th'. It could read the repository, which held no estimate (`COMPLETED.md` §791 says what it saw and did, and holds the preregistered digest)." When §791 is written it owes:
- the rationale's two BLINDNESS disclosures: the national two-party shares on the deciding-count basis, summed from the catalog; and the illustrative rounding with an assumed, unfitted rho, made while weighing C4 against S3, which decided nothing;
- the two unsourced phrases in the brief.

Optionally, reword line 298's parenthetical to "(R-US18's entry says what the panel was told and what its brief held; `COMPLETED.md` §791 says what it saw and did)". Nothing inside the fence changes.

### 58. 'Declared before any measurement' contradicts the next sentence's 'estimates ... made first'

- **Lens:** style-claims - **reviewer:** minor - **skeptic:** minor
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:697

**The scenario.** The heading says 'declared before any measurement'. The next sentence says the session had already made rough scratch estimates of the three methods. Anyone auditing the preregistration meets a contradiction in its first two sentences.

**The fix proposed.** '**The rule, declared before the instrument's first run (§791).**' or 'before US-11's measurement'.

**The skeptic's evidence.** Staged docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:697: "**The rule, declared before any measurement (§791).** A design panel wrote it blind to rough scratch estimates of the three methods, which the session had made first while mapping the item." The estimates were measurements of exactly what the rule decides. scratchpad/us11map/critic.md:77 records the methods reader's est2.pl: "method (a) covers 368 districts with R 185 against 185 and the same 8 districts wrong", which is (a)'s held-state seat error for C4, the quantity step 2 compares. critic.md:92 also records a "comparison of total errors" across (a), (b) and (c). The panel measured something too: final_spec.json spec.rationale BLINDNESS says "the national two-party vote shares on the deciding-count basis were summed from the catalog", which is reading (vi)'s V(E). So "before any measurement" is false on its plain reading, and its own next sentence contradicts it. Every other source words the timing precisely: the panel's spec.rule says "A digest of that block is committed before the first run (P17)"; critic.md:98 (G2) says "the rule must be written before the instrument runs, and the record should say the estimates came first"; the same commit's sibling headings say "*Design, DECLARED before the build (§791).*" (line 298) and "**Asked blind, before the first run (§791).**" (line 864). The defence that "measurement" means only US-11's instrument run ("The measurement decides", line 695) fails, because the heading says "any", not "the". It is not a defect: line 697 is outside the hashed fence (BEGIN marker at line 699, END at 860), so nothing the rule decides changes, the fix needs no amendment and the preregistered digest does not move. The paragraph discloses the truth, so it contradicts itself rather than hiding anything. It is still more than wording, because it is the preregistration's own timing claim, which an auditor or a quotation of the heading alone would take as stated.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Change line 697's heading to "**The rule, declared before the instrument's first run (§791).**" This matches the panel's own "committed before the first run (P17)" and the "Asked blind, before the first run" heading at line 864. Keep the next sentence, which says the estimates came first. Avoid "before US-11's measurement": the scratch estimates already measured US-11's quantities, so that wording is still loose. The line is outside the R-US18 markers, so the edit does not move the fence's SHA-256.

### 59. 'All ten' is a typed count of house_maps.csv's 2026 rows

- **Lens:** style-claims - **reviewer:** minor - **skeptic:** minor
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:324

**The scenario.** 'all ten if a later read restores the plan' types the count of 2026 rows with lines_changed = 1 (ten today: AL CA FL LA MO NC OH TN TX UT). The plan's own risk 9 (line 973) points to the list instead ('the tool prints them'), because 'the 2026 page is revised in place'. A re-read that adds or drops a 2026 state makes 'ten' false while every check stays green.

**The fix proposed.** '... every state the 2026 row names, if a later read restores the plan ...'.

**The skeptic's evidence.** The finding holds. The commit adds one typed count, it is true today, and it sits outside the R-US18 fence, so it is a style breach and not a defect.

1. The new text, line 324 of docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md (staged, the 'In play' bullet): "The redrawn states are `house_maps.csv`'s `in_force` reading - Missouri held on 3 Nov 2026 by its saved note, all ten if a later read restores the plan".

2. "Ten" is a count of generated catalog rows. In ElectionsData/usa/house_maps.csv, the 2026 rows with lines_changed = 1 are lines 257 AL, 261 CA, 265 FL, 274 LA, 281 MO, 289 NC, 291 OH, 298 TN, 299 TX and 300 UT. That is ten, so the claim is correct today. The CSV's header says the 2026 rows are "the plans as the Bureau collected them".

3. The claim convention, CLAUDE.md lines 39-41: DERIVED covers "a count, a figure ... a 'there are N of X'". Its only allowed places are GENERATED, REFERENCED or DELETED, and "Nobody transcribes, anywhere, in any file". Only COMPLETED.md is exempt.

4. The plan's own risk 9 (line 973) refers to the list instead of counting it: "the tool prints them; the 2026 page is revised in place, Missouri's plan in doubt". So a later re-read can change the 2026 list.

5. This is a leftover of the condensation. final_spec.json, spec.cycles, says: "3 Nov 2026: the nine states other than Missouri (173 districts), Missouri held by its saved note (all ten and 181 districts if a later read restores the plan)". spec.undecidable[1] has "173 districts (181 if Missouri's plan stands)". The plan drops "nine", "173" and "181", in line with its stated aim of keeping the redrawn-state lists and seat counts out. "All ten" is the only one kept. The rest of the plan types no catalog counts; the other spelled-out numbers are legal constants such as the veto's "ten days".

6. Nothing would catch it going stale. Assets/Editor/DocumentClaimCheck.cs:124 scans only `Directory.GetFiles(root, "*.md", SearchOption.TopDirectoryOnly)`, so not docs/specs. Its pattern at line 64 matches only backticked `Type.Member` references, never a spelled-out count. No other Editor check reads USA_STAGE_PLAN.md for counts. If a re-read adds or drops a 2026 state, the text goes false with every check green, as the finding says.

7. Why it is minor: the fence runs from line 699 to line 860 and never mentions Missouri, in_force or ten, so the rule's SHA-256 is unaffected. The line's actual instruction is "the in_force reading", and the plan says nothing decides on in_force. The rule's result is unchanged and the figure is true today, so by the rubric this is a style breach.

8. One correction to the finding's fix: "every state the 2026 row names" is imprecise. house_maps.csv has one row per state per election, 50 rows for 2026, and no single "2026 row" names states.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Replace the count with a reference to the column rule the Inputs bullet already declares. On line 324, write: "The redrawn states are `house_maps.csv`'s `in_force` reading - Missouri held on 3 Nov 2026 by its saved note, `in_force` equal to `lines_changed` again if a later read restores the plan - and past the rows read the last map read stays in force." Another option is "... every state `lines_changed` marks for 2026, if a later read restores the plan ...". Either way, no count stays in the plan; the list belongs to the tool's output and to COMPLETED.md §791.

### 60. '(none in the record)' is a derived claim, and the rule it qualifies is (a)'s arithmetic sitting outside the fence

- **Lens:** style-claims - **reviewer:** minor - **skeptic:** minor
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:301

**The scenario.** 'A held district with no base share (none in the record)' is a 'there are 0 of X' claim about the catalog. It is true today: the only N row is NC-9 2018, and NC is redrawn for 2020. Lines 298 and 697 say the methods' arithmetic is the block's, yet this edge rule of (a) lives only here, outside the digest. If R-K9's re-read or a later cycle adds an N row in a held state, the claim turns false with no check, and that state's count follows text no digest holds.

**The fix proposed.** Drop '(none in the record)'. Then either make it an asserted data failure ('a held district without a base share') or move the rule into the block before the digest is fixed.

**The skeptic's evidence.** Both halves of the finding hold. Neither changes the outcome on the record, so this is minor, not a defect.

1. The parenthetical is a DERIVED claim, and it is true today.
- CLAUDE.md:39 lists "a count ... a 'there are N of X'" as DERIVED.
- CLAUDE.md:40-41 allow a DERIVED claim only as GENERATED, REFERENCED or DELETED, and say "Nobody transcribes, anywhere, in any file". COMPLETED.md is the only exempt file.
- Staged plan line 301 says "A held district with no base share (none in the record) would be counted with its state by F-old's quantile form over the rest". That is a "there are 0 of X" claim about the catalog, carried over from the panel's method_a step 5: "(none in the record, impossible in play)".
- It is true now. The only row in house_districts.csv that has a winner of '-', flag N, or a zero deciding count without U is `2018,NC,9,...,-,N`. house_maps.csv has `2020,NC,13,1,...`, so NC-9 2018 is a base only in C2, where North Carolina counts as redrawn. The 2016, 2020, 2022 and 2024 bases (C1, C3, C4 and C5) have no N rows.
- DocumentClaimCheck.cs only matches backticked `Type.Member` references (the regex at line 66), so nothing in the bar holds this claim.
- R-K9's re-verification is still pending (plan line 957). If it changes a row so that a held state's base gains an N district, the document becomes wrong. That is exactly what the convention's test forbids.

2. A piece of (a)'s measured arithmetic sits outside the digest.
- Line 298 says: "The methods' arithmetic, the scoring and the decision are R-US18's preregistered block; this list holds everything else."
- Line 697 says: "the arithmetic, the scoring and the decision are the block's".
- The fence defines the N share (line 716: "none if it has no winner and no votes (N)"). Its held-state rule (lines 720-721: "each district is REP if dhat > 0.5, DEM if dhat < 0.5, and its base winner's party at exactly 0.5") has no branch for a district with no share. Only redrawn states take the shares as a set (line 722).
- The panel put this rule inside (a)'s held-state arithmetic, in method_a step 5. The plan moved it to line 301, which is not covered by the SHA-256 this commit fixes. A later edit to it would need no ruling.
- None of the data failures at line 308 catches the case. "a row outside N and U with no deciding count", "North Carolina 2018's base not its decided seats" and "M = 0" all let a held N district through.
- If the case ever occurs, an implementation that follows only the fence, as lines 298 and 697 direct, makes no call for that district. Line 309 sets DEM to "435 less" REP, so the seat would quietly go to DEM. The design block instead sends it through F-old's quantile form, so the two would disagree.

Why minor and not a defect:
- No held state's base has an N district in any measured cycle (C1-C4) or under any S4 reading. North Carolina is redrawn in C2 under all of them, and the readings only change Minnesota, Colorado, the truth, D or V.
- C5's base (2024) has no N rows either.
- In play, B is the record's 2022 or the game's own count, and neither has N rows.
- So nothing the rule decides changes today, and the parenthetical is not false.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Do both before the digest is fixed.

1. Drop "(none in the record)". It is decoration, so the convention's destination is DELETED. If the fact is wanted, make it REFERENCED instead: add "every held district counted by the quantile form" to the measures' "the exceptions, named" list, so the instrument prints any such district rather than the plan asserting there are none.

2. Move the rule into the fence, where the panel had it in method_a step 5, by extending the held-state bullet: "- A held state: each district is REP if dhat > 0.5, DEM if dhat < 0.5, and its base winner's party at exactly 0.5; a held district with no share (N) is counted with its state by F-old's quantile form over the state's other shares." Then cut that sentence from line 301.

The finding's other option, making it a data failure, would depart from the panel: the panel counts such a district, it does not fail on it. If that route is taken, record it as a departure from the panel's spec.

### 61. 'The record's only large swing ... and its only midterm on which (a) runs' is derived, would go false under ask 6, and contradicts C3's own line

- **Lens:** style-claims - **reviewer:** minor - **skeptic:** note
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:305

**The scenario.** These are claims about the record on disk (also on line 865). Ask 6's alternative, sourcing the Clerk's statistics before 2016, adds other midterms (2010, 2014) to the record. 'Only' would then need re-reading, while ask 1, asked alongside ask 6, still rests on it. 'Its only midterm on which (a) runs' also contradicts line 305's own 'C3 ... printed ..., (a) running through F-old in every multi-seat state': (a) does run on 2022.

**The fix proposed.** 'on the record on disk, its largest swing on mostly held lines and the one midterm on which (a) counts held districts' (or point to §791 for the swings), on both line 305 and line 865.

**The skeptic's evidence.** Only one of the three legs holds, and it is wording outside the fence.

CONFIRMED (the contradiction):
- Line 305 (added): "C3, the census redistricting, is printed in R-US14's stress-case form, (a) running through F-old in every multi-seat state ... C1 joins D as the record's only large swing on mostly held lines and its only midterm on which (a) runs". Line 865 (added) repeats the clause.
- Fence line 715: "(a) is district uniform swing with F-old". On that definition, (a) runs in C3, whose later election is the 2022 midterm.
- house_maps.csv: AK, DE, ND, SD, VT and WY have lines_changed 0 and unchanged seats in 2022. They are held for C3 under line 710's rule, so (a)'s own district swing runs there. My counts from house_maps.csv: 402 seats held into 2018, 6 into 2022.
- The two midterms the record reaches as T are 2018 (C1) and 2022 (C3). So the literal claim is false; the meaning intended, the only midterm on mostly held lines, is true.
- The panel's spec has the same tension word for word ("C1 is the record's only large swing on mostly held lines, and its only midterm on which (a) runs", beside "PRINTED ONLY: C3 for every method, with (a) running through F-old"). The plan inherited it.

WHY IT DECIDES NOTHING:
- The fence fixes the cycles (line 704) and D = {C1, C2, C4} (line 705), and says "D itself changes only by amendment" (line 858).
- Ask 1's alternative is the item's {C2, C4}. The argument for C1 (held lines and a large swing) stands under either reading of "(a) runs".
- No implementer reads D from this sentence, so it cannot be resolved two ways in code.

REFUTED ("derived"):
- The sentence is a qualitative statement about the 2016–2024 election record, not a figure, count, name or path. CLAUDE.md's test ("the code can change freely and no document becomes wrong") still passes.
- The same bullet keeps statements of the same kind ("C4's swing is close to zero"). The session deliberately moved only the figures to §791.
- The finding's own fix ("its largest swing") is just as derived.

REFUTED ("would go false under ask 6"):
- Swings recomputed from house_by_state.csv: C1 -4.99, C2 +2.89, C3 +2.95, C4 -0.08 pp. These match the panel's figures, so the claim is true of the record on disk.
- Ask 6 (line 870) needs only 2014 to give (c) a C1 window (2014->2016, whose later election precedes 2018). 2014 enters as a base, not as a midterm T, so "only midterm" stays true. Only sourcing 2012 or earlier adds a midterm transition (2012->2014).
- Ask 6 already says "(a change to D is an amendment)", and its default line ("The record before 2016. Not sourced") already scopes "the record". The future case is a TRACKING update, not a present falsehood.

THE FINDING'S FIX IS ITSELF FALSE: "the one midterm on which (a) counts held districts" fails for 2022, where (a) counts the six held at-large districts.

ADJACENT, NOT IN THE FINDING, also on line 865: "the kind of election a 2024-start game meets first" is false. A 2024-start game's first House count is 5 Nov 2024, a presidential-year count on the 2022 base (line 324: "the record's 2022 for 5 Nov 2024"; US-16). It also departs from the panel's "meets next: 3 Nov 2026" and from line 305's "meets on 3 Nov 2026".

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** On line 305 and line 865, replace "its only midterm on which (a) runs" with "its only midterm on mostly held lines" (or "its only midterm whose seats (a) counts by district swing rather than through F-old"). Keep "the record's only large swing on mostly held lines": it is true of the record on disk, and ask 6 already says a change to D is an amendment. If wanted, scope it "the record on disk's". Do not use the finding's wording "(a) counts held districts": the six held at-large states make it false for 2022. In the same edit, on line 865 replace "the kind of election a 2024-start game meets first" with "the kind of election a 2024-start game meets on 3 Nov 2026", matching line 305 and the panel's "next: 3 Nov 2026". A 2024-start game first counts the House on 5 Nov 2024. Both lines are outside the R-US18 markers, so the fence's digest is unaffected.

### 62. The list of which redraw instruments are on disk is a derived claim and conflicts with reading 8

- **Lens:** style-claims - **reviewer:** minor - **skeptic:** note
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:328

**The scenario.** 'Alabama 2024's court-ordered plan and the enacted acts are on disk, Pennsylvania 2018's and Georgia 2024's instruments are not, and 2026's were not read' is a claim about the disk. Reading 8 contradicts it in three places:
- Colorado's and Minnesota's 2018 instruments were not read either ('The 2016, 2018, 2022 and 2026 instruments were not read'). The spec's undecidable [3] has 'Colorado 2018 is undescribed', which is dropped here.
- North Carolina 2024's act is only named on a file list; 'its text and date are BILLED'.
- Alabama's plan is only named on a page that 'says no more'.
This line is S5's stated reason, so a reader would take most of D's redraws as documented.

**The fix proposed.** 'the kind of the record's redraws against play's - mixed and partly unread (house_districts.md reading 8), 2026's not read; which is why S5 asks rather than switches'.

**The skeptic's evidence.** The finding is real only in a narrow sense. Its main claims fall apart when checked against the files.

Staged plan line 328: "the kind of the record's redraws against play's - Alabama 2024's court-ordered plan and the enacted acts are on disk, Pennsylvania 2018's and Georgia 2024's instruments are not, and 2026's were not read; which is why S5 asks rather than switches;"

The panel's text (final_spec.json spec.undecidable[2], the third item): "...Alabama 2024's court-ordered plan and the enacted acts (NC 2020; LA, NY and NC 2024) are on disk. Pennsylvania 2018's 'court-ordered' (the facts sheet's phrase) and Georgia 2024's instrument are not; Colorado 2018 is undescribed; 2026's instruments were not read." Two things were dropped: the scope of "the enacted acts" and "Colorado 2018 is undescribed".

The three claimed conflicts with reading 8:

1. **Colorado and Minnesota 2018: an omission, not a conflict.** Line 328 says nothing about either state. The plan carries both elsewhere:
   - line 334: "Minnesota's and Colorado's 2018 changes (asked blind);"
   - line 867 (choice 3): "(Minnesota's change "cosmetic" on the Bureau's page, Colorado's undescribed)"
   - line 300 defines `instrument` "as house_districts.md reading 8 names it ... "-" where no saved page says".
   So the dropped Colorado clause is not lost from the plan.

2. **Alabama: not a conflict.** Reading 8 lists Alabama under "The instruments read for the changes this item's cycles meet". Two saved pages name the court-ordered plan: the Legislature's page and Supreme Court docket 23A231, which denied the stay. The plan's own sourcing row (line 945, unchanged since 8f7b87fa) treats it as saved, with only "Alabama's order day on PACER: BILLED". The bullet is about the kind of redraw, and that is documented.

3. **North Carolina 2024: the one real wrinkle.** Reading 8 counts S.L. 2023-145 as read, because the State Board of Elections' file list names it, but says "its text and date are BILLED". Line 945 says "Georgia's 2024 act and North Carolina's S.L. 2023-145 refused (403/401, the Internet Archive 429)". With the spec's scope dropped, "the enacted acts are on disk" still naturally covers S.L. 2023-145. So two lines of the plan give that act two different statuses. The wording comes from the spec, so this is not a departure from it.

**The claimed harm is not a misreading.** In house_maps.csv, `lines_changed`=1 in D's target years gives nine redrawn state-cycles:
- 2018: CO, MN, PA
- 2020: NC
- 2024: AL, GA, LA, NY, NC

Five of the nine have their instrument named on a saved page (NC 2020, AL, LA, NY and NC 2024), and the Bureau calls Minnesota's change "cosmetic". So "most of D's redraws documented" is correct by reading 8's standard.

**Nothing depends on line 328.**
- It sits outside the `<!-- R-US18 RULE BEGIN/END -->` markers, so it is not in the hashed digest.
- Nothing decides on `instrument` (line 300), so no implementer can read the bullet two ways.

**"Derived claim" is not a distinct breach.**
- DocumentClaimCheck.cs:64 only checks backticked `Type.Member`.
- The same plan uses "on disk" for sourcing status at lines 80, 102, 174, 184, 192, 360, 465, 546, 605, 938, 941, 949 and 951.

What remains is wording: one unscoped phrase that line 945 contradicts, and one clause dropped that line 867 already carries. That is a note, not minor.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Point line 328 at the record instead of listing part of it, for example: "the kind of the record's redraws against play's - mixed (a court-ordered plan, enacted acts, some named only, a change the Bureau calls cosmetic) and partly unread (`house_districts.md` reading 8), 2026's not read; which is why S5 asks rather than switches;". If the named list stays, restore the spec's scope and Colorado clause: "...and the enacted acts (North Carolina 2020; Louisiana and New York 2024; North Carolina 2024's named only, its text BILLED) are on disk; Pennsylvania 2018's and Georgia 2024's instruments are not, Colorado 2018 is undescribed, and 2026's were not read". Either version makes line 328 agree with line 945. The rule's digest is not affected.

### 63. 'Run on the defaults' and 'the defaults confirmed' could mean ruled or unruled, which decides whether S4 can fire

- **Lens:** style-claims - **reviewer:** minor - **skeptic:** minor
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:864

**The scenario.** The Needs line (295) accepts 'the defaults confirmed' and line 864 accepts 'said to run on the defaults'. Line 864 then says a choice 'ruled before the run - either way' makes its S4 reading printed-only, and one 'not ruled' keeps it. If Elias says 'run on the defaults', one implementation treats all six choices as ruled: readings (i)-(vi) become printed-only and S4 can never fire. Another treats them as unruled and keeps S4 live. The rule's possible outcomes differ. Choices 5 and 6 also have no S4 reading, though the paragraph gives each choice one.

**The fix proposed.** State it outright: 'Run on the defaults rules each default (or: leaves each unruled)'. Add 'choices 1-4 carry S4's readings (ii), (i), (iii)-(v) and (vi); 5 and 6 carry none'.

**The skeptic's evidence.** Staged docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md (read via git show :path).

Line 295: "*Needs:* US-0; for its instrument, R-US18's six choices answered blind or the defaults confirmed (§791)."
Line 864: "The instrument is built once Elias has answered them or said to run on the defaults (US-11's *Needs*) ... A choice ruled before the run - either way - is written into the block by amendment, and its S4 reading becomes printed only; one not ruled keeps its default and its S4 reading."

The fence leaves S4 live unless the block is amended. Lines 797-807 (STEP 6) re-run readings (i)-(vi) and "The rule STOPS (S4) if any reading changes the outcome". So whether a "defaults" reply triggers an amendment decides whether S4 can fire.

Both readings are tenable:
(1) NOT RULED. Line 864 lists "answered them" and "said to run on the defaults" as separate routes. "One not ruled keeps its default" only has work to do if the defaults route leaves choices unruled.
(2) RULED.
 - "Either way" directly follows a sentence naming two routes, so it can be read as "by either route".
 - The plan records Elias's acceptance of a default as a ruling: lines 531-648, e.g. "**RULED** (F8): (a), as recommended". So "the defaults confirmed" (line 295) reads as a ruling.
 - The spec's P20 reason agrees: "A blind ruling before the run removes the forking path wherever Elias gives one".

Effect: given the same reply, one session writes "AMENDED (Fn)" and makes the S4 readings for choices 1-4 printed only, so S4 can never fire. Another leaves the block alone and S4 stays live. Line 305's "C1 can turn a verdict into an ask but never decide against the item's list" holds only in the second case.

Second point, verified: step 6's readings map only to choice 2 (i), choice 1 (ii), choice 3 (iii)-(v) and choice 4 (vi). Choices 5 ((b)'s seat rule) and 6 (the record before 2016) have none, yet line 864 gives each choice "its S4 reading". Choice 3 has three readings, not one.

Grade: minor, not a defect. The fence text is unambiguous; the fork is in the amendment process outside it.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Use one phrase in both places and state its effect.

Line 295: "...R-US18's six choices answered blind, or Elias's word to run on the defaults (§791)". Avoid "confirmed": under the F8 "as recommended" = RULED convention it reads as a ruling.

Line 864, replace the last sentence with (or state the opposite outright, if that was intended): "A choice Elias answers before the run, default or alternative, is written into the block by amendment and its S4 readings become printed only. 'Run on the defaults' rules none: each choice keeps its default and its S4 readings. Choices 1-4 carry S4's readings (ii), (i), (iii)-(v) and (vi); choices 5 and 6 carry none, and a ruling on either is written in by amendment only."

### 64. US-11's guarantee that C1 'never decides against the item's list' lapses once ask 1 is ruled

- **Lens:** style-claims - **reviewer:** minor - **skeptic:** note
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:305

**The scenario.** Line 305: 'because the item's own cycles must agree with D (S4's reading (ii)), C1 can turn a verdict into an ask but never decide against the item's list'. Line 864 makes reading (ii) printed-only once ask 1 is ruled either way. After that, C1 can decide against {C2, C4}. The design states without condition a guarantee its own ask removes.

**The fix proposed.** '... never decide against the item's list while S4's reading (ii) stands (ask 1)'.

**The skeptic's evidence.** The scenario can happen. Line 305 (new in this commit) says: "because the item's own cycles must agree with D (S4's reading (ii)), C1 can turn a verdict into an ask but never decide against the item's list. Asked blind (R-US18's entry)." Line 864 (also new) says: "A choice ruled before the run - either way - is written into the block by amendment, and its S4 reading becomes printed only; one not ruled keeps its default and its S4 reading."

Traced in the fence:
- Lines 801-807: reading (ii) re-runs on D = {C2, C4} and compares the step-2 verdict and the fallback. S4 fires "if any reading changes the outcome".
- While (ii) is a stop, the guarantee holds. A decided (a) or (b) on {C1, C2, C4} needs (ii)'s step-2 verdict and fallback to match; otherwise S4 turns it into an ask.
- Now suppose Elias rules ask 1 for its default, "D with C1" (line 865). Line 864 then makes (ii) printed only. Say {C1, C2, C4} picks (b) and {C2, C4} would pick (a): the rule decides (b) with no stop.
- In that reachable state, line 305's "never" is false, while the rest of the line (D = {C1, C2, C4}) stays true. That is what makes this more than the generic "any amendment can change the design".

Why it is only a note and not minor:
- The fence decides, and the ruling is written into it by amendment. Line 864 states the demotion explicitly, and line 305 is outside the digest. No two faithful implementations of the fence can differ over this.
- The claim is true at this commit and on any run where ask 1 stays unruled. Its "because" names the condition it rests on, and "Asked blind (R-US18's entry)" points straight to line 864.
- It is a faithful transcription of the panel's spec. spec.cycles has the same unconditional sentence: "So C1 can turn a verdict into an ask, but never decide against the item's own list". spec.stop_and_ask has the demotion: "A convention he rules before the run replaces its default under the digest, and its S4 reading becomes printed only".
- It does not breach the claim convention. It holds no derived figure, and it depends on an open ask: when ask 1 is answered, the sentence's "This departs from the item's text" and "Asked blind" wording has to be revised anyway.

A separate point the fix brushes against, which can change what the rule decides: line 295 says "the defaults confirmed", while line 864 says "said to run on the defaults" and "ruled before the run - either way". That leaves open whether Elias confirming the defaults counts as a ruling, which demotes all six S4 readings including (ii), or not, which keeps them all. That choice decides whether line 305's guarantee holds on the default path, so it should be settled in its own right.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Line 305: "...and because the item's own cycles must agree with D (S4's reading (ii)), C1 can turn a verdict into an ask but, while ask 1 is unruled, never decide against the item's list (a ruling either way makes reading (ii) printed only). Asked blind (R-US18's entry)." The reviewer's shorter clause, "while S4's reading (ii) stands (ask 1)", is equally correct. Separately, make line 295 match line 864's buckets, for example "...answered blind, or Elias's word to run on the defaults unruled (§791)", so that confirming the defaults is not mistaken for ruling them.

### 65. The ruling-record mirror line could put a typed SHA-256 in the plan, and nothing says whether the instrument checks it

- **Lens:** style-claims - **reviewer:** minor - **skeptic:** minor
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:838

**The scenario.** The block says the record is 'mirrored outside this block', and its ASKED and RULED states carry 'the decisive digest'. If line 862's mirror copies the state as defined, the plan will hold a hand-typed hash. CLAUDE.md classes a hash as DERIVED, and only COMPLETED.md is exempt. Nothing says whether the instrument compares the mirror: one implementation fails on a stale mirror, another never reads it.

**The fix proposed.** Mirror only the state and its section ('ASKED (§79x)', 'RULED (Fn, §79x)'). Keep the digests in COMPLETED.md and the card's stamp, and say whether the instrument checks the mirror line.

**The skeptic's evidence.** The staged plan (`docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md`), lines 838-840, inside the fence: "The ruling record, held by the instrument and mirrored outside this block, is NONE; ASKED, with the ask's section and the decisive digest; or RULED, with the ruling, its option and the decisive digest it was made on." So two of the three states the mirror would copy include a SHA-256.

The digest changes over time. Lines 845-849: "ASKED: the bars pass while the decisive digest equals the registered one. A moved digest fails until the ask is registered again..." and "RULED: ... A moved digest with the same outcome fails as moved pins until a commit re-pins it, naming the old digest, the new one". The spec's `stop_and_ask` is plainer: "until the session re-pins and updates the record's digest in that commit". The record's digest is a live pin, not a fixed identifier.

The mirror is a hand-written line, outside every GENERATED block. Line 862: "**Ruling record:** NONE." Line 322 has `WriteReadings` write only "the card's third GENERATED block, stamped with the catalog's digest, the rule's, the decisive table's and the ruling record's state. A stale block fails both bars." That is the card's own copy, not the plan's line.

Line 323's list of instrument failures has no mirror check: "...a rule digest not on the instrument's list; a pin moved, a measured row unpinned, or a stale block; `GeneratedCatalogCheck`'s re-read failing." "mirror" appears only at line 838, and "Ruling record" only at line 862. Nothing says whether `UsHouseCountCheck` reads line 862. The instrument already reads this file to hash the fence (line 697), so checking the mirror would be trivial for one implementation and absent from another. A stale NONE next to an ASKED record then fails the bar in one and passes in the other.

The spec has the same choice ("mirrored by a line in the plan's R-US18 entry outside the fence"; ASKED/RULED "with ... the decisive digest"), so the problem is inherited, not a condensation error.

CLAUDE.md:39 lists "a hash" as DERIVED. Line 40 allows only GENERATED, REFERENCED or DELETED. Line 41: "Nobody transcribes, anywhere, in any file ... `COMPLETED.md` is exempt". A literal copy of ASKED or RULED into line 862 breaks this.

`DocumentClaimCheck.cs:64,124` only matches backticked `Type.Member` names in root `*.md` files, so no bar check would catch it.

The plan's own style references figures instead of typing them. Line 666: "**Asked §789, with the measurement** (...; the figures are the card's, the record's `COMPLETED.md` §789)". The RULED lines (531-648) carry no digests.

ERRANDS.md:26/29 also type SHA-256s, but they name fixed archives that cannot go stale, unlike a re-pinnable table digest.

Grading: the rule's verdict, stops and digest-gated passes never read the mirror, so this is not a defect. It is a minor issue: an ambiguity that changes bar behaviour, plus a latent breach of the claim convention.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Fix it now, before the fence's digest is fixed. Replace fence lines 838-840 with:

"- The ruling record is held by the instrument: NONE; ASKED, with the ask's section and the decisive digest; or RULED, with the ruling, its option and the decisive digest it was made on. The line after this block mirrors its state, section and ruling, never a digest (the digests are the card's GENERATED stamp and `COMPLETED.md`'s). The instrument fails when that line's state differs from its own."

The checked form fits the repo's rule that checks, not discipline, prevent transcription, and the instrument already reads this file.

If Elias prefers an unread mirror, say so explicitly ("...is TRACKING, never read by the instrument") instead of leaving "mirrored" undefined.

Keep line 862 in the referenced form, e.g. "**Ruling record:** ASKED (§79x) - its decisive digest is the card's stamp, the record's `COMPLETED.md` §79x". If the check is added, also add "the ruling record's mirror differing from the record held" to line 323's failure list. Line 323 is outside the fence, so it can change without an amendment.

### 66. Steps 2, 3 and 5 name C1 and C2, which leaves reading (ii) and the C5 re-run underspecified

- **Lens:** style-claims - **reviewer:** minor - **skeptic:** minor
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:775

**The scenario.** Under reading (ii) (line 801, D = {C2, C4}), step 2's 'H(b) < H(a) in C1 and in C2' names a cycle outside D. An implementation may:
- test C1 anyway, so its evidence leaks into the reading meant to exclude it;
- drop the C1 condition;
- treat the condition as failed, so (b) becomes unreachable.
That gives three possible S4 outcomes. Separately, after C5 is read (line 856: 'a swing cycle by declaration; the rule is run again over D plus C5'), nothing says whether (b) must also win C5 (steps 2 and 5) or whether S2 needs C5 too (step 3: 'in both C1 and C2', line 782). The spec has the same gap.

**The fix proposed.** Phrase the conditions over 'each swing cycle of D' and 'the level cycle', and say C5 joins the swing cycles in the re-run.

**The skeptic's evidence.** Staged docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md, inside the fence:
- L705: "D = {C1, C2, C4}: C1 and C2 are the swing cycles, C4 the level cycle."
- L775-776: "H(b) < H(a) in C1 and in C2;" / "H(b) <= H(a) in C4;"
- L781-782: S2 fires if the method's |N| is "strictly larger than the other's in both C1 and C2".
- L793-794: S6 uses "H strictly lower in C1 and in C2, H not higher in C4".
- L801-802: "(ii) the item's own cycles, D = {C2, C4}: the step-2 verdict and the fallback are compared; S2, S3 and S6 are printed, since each would rest on one swing cycle".
- L752-753: "A forward swing cycle is a swing cycle of D whose window is not empty."
- L855-858: C5 is "a swing cycle by declaration; the rule is run again over D plus C5 ... D itself changes only by amendment."

No line maps the names C1 and C2 onto "the swing cycles of the run". The design block (L305) says "C1 to C4 are measured", so under (ii) the figures for C1 exist and a literal reading of L775 can still test them.

Reading (ii), traced. Take H(a) = 5, 6, 1 and H(b) = 6, 4, 1 over C1, C2, C4, with G and K favouring (b).
- Declared run: the sums are 12 and 11, so (a) is not picked. The C1 condition fails, so the rule stops at S1.
- Under (ii) the sums over {C2, C4} are 7 and 5. A literal implementation still tests C1, gets S1 again, the outcome is unchanged and S4 does not fire.
- A role-based implementation tests only C2 (4 < 6) and C4 (1 <= 1), picks (b), the outcome changes and S4 (ii) fires. S5, which a (b) pick suppresses, can also differ.
- Both runs end in an ask, but the verdict line and the options put to Elias differ.
- A third reading, that C1 lies outside D so its condition fails, would make (b) unreachable under (ii) and turn a decided (b) into an ask. It is less plausible because C1 is measured.

C5, traced. Suppose the ruled outcome is (b), and in C5 H(a) = 3, H(b) = 4, while the sums, G and K over D plus C5 still favour (b).
- If C5 enters only the sums and K, as the named text allows, the outcome stays (b) and there is no re-ask.
- If C5 joins the strict swing-cycle condition, as its "a swing cycle by declaration" label suggests, the rule stops at S1, R-US18 is asked again and the bars fail.
- Step 4 has a further fork. C5's window (2016-18, 2018-20, 2022-24) is not empty, but C5 is not "of D" (L858). So whether C2 and C5 together make S3 live is also open.

The finding's "The spec has the same gap" is only half right. The spec's rule field names C1 and C2 the same way. But its premise P15 says "A switch to (b) needs all of: strictly fewer in each swing cycle; no more in the level cycle", and its cycles field says reading (ii) "re-runs steps 1 and 2 on {C2, C4}". Both point to the role-based reading. The plan carries neither: searching it for "each swing" and "on {C2, C4}" finds nothing. The condensation therefore dropped the text that settled the question, which strengthens the finding.

Severity stays minor. The fence matches the spec's rule field and the outcome depends on which faithful reading is chosen, so this is an ambiguity rather than a definite change. It matters only while reading (ii) stays live (choice 1 not ruled before the run) and when C5 is run. Line references 775, 782, 801 and 856 are accurate.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Restore the spec's P15 wording in the fence. Phrase each condition by cycle role, not by name:
- Step 2: "H(b) < H(a) in each swing cycle of D; H(b) <= H(a) in the level cycle".
- Step 3: "in every swing cycle of D".
- Step 5: "H strictly lower in each swing cycle, not higher in the level cycle".
- Reading (ii): add "C2 is then the only swing cycle".
- C5 paragraph: "the rule is run again with C5 added to D's swing cycles, every step reading it as one (a forward swing cycle where its window is not empty); D itself changes only by amendment".
All of this changes the fence's text, so it must happen before the preregistered digest is fixed.

### 67. For C5, 'its lines changed' could mean lines_changed or in_force; Missouri 2026 differs

- **Lens:** style-claims - **reviewer:** minor - **skeptic:** minor
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:710

**The scenario.** The block defines held as 'its lines changed ... (house_maps.csv)'. That file will carry both lines_changed and in_force, and line 300 says nothing decides on in_force. For C5, Missouri has lines_changed = 1, but the 120th tab says its plan 'cannot be used'. Reading lines_changed, Missouri is redrawn in C5's step 1 although its election ran on the old lines. Reading in_force, it is held. The step-1 sums at the C5 re-ask differ between the two.

**The fix proposed.** Name the column in the block ('lines_changed' or 'in_force') before the digest is fixed.

**The skeptic's evidence.** I could not refute it. The fence never names a column for held or redrawn, and the two columns the file will carry disagree on the one state-cycle the rule measures later.

1. The fence (staged USA_STAGE_PLAN.md, fence lines 699-860) cites the maps file twice, both times in prose only:
   - line 710-711: "A state is held for B->T if its lines changed at no election after B up to T and its seats did not change (house_maps.csv); otherwise it is redrawn."
   - line 751, (c)'s window: "(the seats of the states whose lines or seats changed, house_maps.csv)".
   A grep inside the markers finds neither `lines_changed` nor `in_force`. Elsewhere the fence does name its columns, e.g. "final_r and final_d (house_districts.csv)".

2. The panel's spec names the column outright. method_a section 4 says: "A state is redrawn for B->T if lines_changed = 1 at any election after B up to T, or if seats(T) != seats(B)." method_c says: "the seats of states with lines_changed = 1 or a change of seats." The condensation dropped the column name.

3. The catalog has the column today and gains a second one:
   - `house_maps.csv`'s header is `year,state,seats,lines_changed,source`, and its row 281 is `2026,MO,8,1,raw/maps/census_rdo_congressional_districts_120th.html`.
   - Reading 8 of `house_districts.md` and `us_house_prep.pl` line 266 keep the 120th tab's note that Missouri's plan "cannot be used".
   - Plan line 300 says the tool gains `in_force` (`lines_changed`, but Missouri 2026 held) "on which nothing decides".
   - For 2016-2024 the two columns are equal by construction, so the D verdict (C1, C2, C4) is the same under either reading. Only 2026 differs: 181 changed seats with Missouri, 173 without, summed from the CSV.

4. The plan points both ways for C5. The fence says at lines 855-857: "the rule is run again over D plus C5, and step 1 over C5's redrawn states. Either giving an outcome other than the ruled one asks R-US18 again."
   - Toward `lines_changed`: line 300's "nothing decides" on `in_force`.
   - Toward `in_force`:
     - line 324 defines "The redrawn states are house_maps.csv's in_force reading - Missouri held on 3 Nov 2026";
     - line 338 says "Missouri's 2026 plan - read as held by the saved note";
     - the fence's own words "its lines changed at [an] election" are false for an election that ran on the old lines.
   - The spec has the same split. Its rule says `lines_changed`, but its C5 line reads "step 1 over C5's redrawn states (the first of play's kind: 173 or 181 districts)", which are `in_force`'s two states.

5. The failing path: C5 is measured once the Clerk's 2026 statistics are saved. If `lines_changed(MO,2026)` is still 1 then:
   - The `lines_changed` reading puts Missouri in step 1's C5 sums. Its F-old W equals the held count, because M = n = 8; its F-prop W is (b0)'s.
   - The `in_force` reading leaves Missouri out of step 1 and puts its 8 seats in C5's H instead.
   - So step 1's sums, H, and G under F-prop all differ between the readings, and with them whether R-US18 is asked again.

Why minor: the ambiguity cannot reach D's verdict, and C5 is BILLED until 2027. But the fence's digest is fixed at this commit, so any later fix needs an amendment.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Before the digest is fixed, name the spec's column in both places inside the fence:
- Line 710-711: "A state is redrawn for B->T if lines_changed = 1 (house_maps.csv) at any election after B up to T, or if its seats changed; otherwise it is held."
- Line 751: "(the seats of the states with lines_changed = 1 or a change of seats, house_maps.csv)".

Then settle Missouri at C5 outside the fence, so line 338's "read as held" does not contradict the rule. Two options:
- Say the rule reads `lines_changed` in every cycle, C5 included. Add that the 120th tab is re-read before C5 is measured, and that Missouri at C5 is counted as redrawn while the Bureau's list still names it, flagged to Elias.
- If C5 should follow the lines 2026 actually ran on, use `in_force`. That departs from the spec's "on which nothing decides", so it needs an explicit choice (an ask), not a silent change.

### 68. The decisive digest's contents are underspecified

- **Lens:** style-claims - **reviewer:** minor - **skeptic:** minor
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:841

**The scenario.** The spec covers 'every deciding and guarding row in D'. The block says 'every figure steps 1-7 compare ... H, G, N and K for every row a step compares in D'. On the record, step 4's first condition fails (one forward swing cycle), so whether (c)'s and no change's N count as 'compared', and so are in the digest, depends on whether an implementation short-circuits. 'H, G, N and K for every row' also leaves open whether (b')'s N, which no step compares, is in it. Two faithful instruments would disagree on which moved figure forces a re-ask or a re-pin.

**The fix proposed.** List the digest's rows and figures explicitly: W by redrawn state-cycle under both fallbacks; H, G, K of (a), (b) and (b'); N of (a) and (b) in C1 and C2; N of (c) and no change in each forward swing cycle.

**The skeptic's evidence.** The finding holds. I could not refute it.

The fence (staged USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:841-843) says: "The decisive digest is SHA-256 over every figure steps 1-7 compare, under every reading: W per redrawn state-cycle under F-old and F-prop, and H, G, N and K for every row a step compares in D. Nothing printed only is in it."

The panel's spec (stop_and_ask) says: "every figure steps 1-7 read under every reading: W per redrawn state-cycle, and H, G, N and K for every deciding and guarding row in D. It excludes the printed-only parts (C3, the game range, (b0), the U-blind line, and C5 until it is measured)."

The condensation made two changes:
- It replaced a fixed, role-based row set ("deciding and guarding") with "a row a step compares", which can be read as "what this run compared".
- It replaced the spec's list of exclusions with the undefined phrase "printed only".

**The short-circuit path happens on the record.**
- Lines 752-753: "A cycle with an empty window has no (c). A forward swing cycle is a swing cycle of D whose window is not empty."
- house_years.csv and house_by_state.csv hold only 2016-2024. So C1's window is empty and C2 is the only forward swing cycle. The spec's cycles field agrees: "On the record on disk that is C2 alone ... so S3 cannot fire."
- Step 4 (785-790) reads "STOPS (S3) only if all of these hold: D has at least two forward swing cycles; ...", then "Its comparisons print in the verdict line."
- An instrument that stops evaluating after that first condition never compares (c)'s or no change's |N|, and can treat them as "printed only". An instrument that follows the colon list hashes (c)'s N and all of no change's H, G, N and K.
- Step 5's gate, "If (b') is fitted in every cycle of D" (792), splits all of (b')'s figures the same way.

**Head clause and colon list disagree.**
- (b')'s N: step 5 compares only H, G and K. The colon list puts N in; the head clause and "Nothing printed only" leave it out.
- The same split covers (a)'s and (b)'s N in C4 (step 3 reads only C1 and C2), (c)'s N in C4, and whether "(a) with F-prop" counts as a row (its N and K). F-prop and (b') are both rulings Elias can choose (851).
- C5: the spec brings it into the digest once measured. The plan's "in D", with "D itself changes only by amendment" (856-858), leaves C5's H, G, N and K out, while "the rule is run again over D plus C5" puts them in.

**What two instruments would do differently.**
- In the ASKED state (845-846), "A moved digest fails until the ask is registered again with the new table, and Elias is told."
- A figure outside the digest still fails as a pin (319: pins cover every measured row), but it does not trigger a new ask.
- So the same moved figure leads to a new ask and Elias being told under one instrument, and only to a failed pin under the other. Examples: a fix to (c)'s window code, or a small V(2016) correction that only flips (c)'s rounding.
- The fence is locked by SHA-256, so implementers cannot settle this later without an amendment ruling.

**Why minor, not a defect.**
- The digest never changes the verdict; steps 1-7 decide the outcome alone. It only decides when an ask is registered again, or when a re-pin must name the old and new digests.
- Read as a fixed set, the plan's rows match the spec's. This is two-way ambiguity, not a changed rule.

**The finding's own fix is narrower than the spec.**
- It drops (b')'s N, F-prop's N and K, (a)'s and (b)'s N in C4, no change's H, G and K, (c)'s N in C4, and C5 once measured.
- (b') and F-prop are rulings Elias can choose. After such a ruling, a moved N of the ruled method would not move the digest.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Name the rows outright, keep the spec's breadth, and make the set fixed rather than dependent on what a run compared. Restore the spec's exclusion list. Suggested fence text:
