- house_by_state.csv is votes only: year, state, votes_r, votes_d, votes_other, votes_total.
- GeneratedCatalogCheck.CheckUsHouseDistricts (Assets/Editor/GeneratedCatalogCheck.cs:743-788) checks national winners against the House Historian's division and each state's numbering against its seats. It has no per-state party figure.
- The pins bullet (line 319) covers method rows only: "every measured cycle and row - REP seats, N, G, H, K and the contingent outcome". The record's class counts and contingent call are not pinned.
- The fence (lines 699-860) does not mention this gate. Its only use of a truth gate is STEP 7's "(In 2016-2024 such a winner fails the truth gate, a data defect.)"

So from this text alone, one build makes "the delegations of record" an empty structural check that cannot fail beyond "districts or seats against the apportionment" and the truth gate. Another pins the record's classes in code, following UsStateSwingCheck's Pins pattern (Assets/Editor/UsStateSwingCheck.cs:48, 243-253).

The reviewer's single-correction case is overstated. One corrected winner changes the national count, which GeneratedCatalogCheck currently holds equal to [HH-DIV], so it fails the truth gate in every build. The builds part only on offsetting corrections in two states in the same year. That correction is possible under the BILLED R-K9 re-verification (plan line 957). In that case the national gate holds, the catalog digest only makes the block stale (a regeneration clears it), and K, G and H can cancel or stay unchanged. The pin build then fails as a data defect while the structural build regenerates and passes.

Why minor and not a defect: the rule's decision does not depend on the gate, and the text is undefined rather than false. Line 323 is outside the R-US18 fence, so it is not digest-held and can be fixed by an ordinary edit before the build. The build waits on the six blind choices anyway.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Restore the spec's gate as a pin, keeping the figures out of the plan (claim convention):
- Line 304: change "are derived by the instrument." to "are derived by the instrument, and their class counts (REP majority / DEM majority / tied) and contingent call per year are pinned in it like the rows - the panel's data gate, its figures in the code, never in this plan."
- Line 323: change "the truth gate or the delegations of record;" to "the truth gate; the record's delegation class counts or contingent call, in any year, not their pins (a moved one is a data defect, re-pinned only by the commit that corrects the catalog, naming the correction);"
- Line 319: add "the record's class counts and contingent call per year" to the pins bullet.

The alternative is to drop the gate: delete "or the delegations of record" from line 323 and record in COMPLETED.md §791 that the plan departs from the panel's typed delegation gate. Do not use the reviewer's "summing state by state to the truth's decided seats": that is the empty structural check that the apportionment and truth-gate items already cover.

### 86. The C5 paragraph drops two of the spec's conditions

- **Lens:** implementability - **reviewer:** minor - **skeptic:** minor
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:857

**The scenario.** The spec says a C5 outcome other than the ruled one 're-asks R-US18, and the bars fail until the ask is registered', and it excludes C5 from the decisive digest only 'until it is measured'. The fence keeps neither. Line 857 says only 'asks R-US18 again', and the digest covers rows 'in D' (842-843), which C5 never joins without an amendment (858). A build from the fence alone therefore prints the re-ask but stays green whenever the D-only outcome still equals the ruled one. And C5's figures never move the decisive digest, so the ruled record is never re-pinned or re-registered because of C5.

**The fix proposed.** Restore both: '... asks R-US18 again, and the bars fail until the ask is registered'; and add to the digest 'and, once C5 is measured, the rows of D plus C5'.

**The skeptic's evidence.** SPEC (final_spec.json, field stop_and_ask) states the C5 conditions in three places:
(1) "C5 RE-ASK. ... Either giving an outcome other than the ruled one re-asks R-US18, and the bars fail until the ask is registered."
(2) The RULED clause: "A changed outcome fails until the session re-asks ... Causes include a correction to the [PROVISIONAL] catalog after R-K9's re-read, and C5."
(3) The digest: "H, G, N and K for every deciding and guarding row in D. It excludes the printed-only parts (C3, the game range, (b0), the U-blind line, and C5 until it is measured)."

FENCE (staged docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md):
- 855-858: "...the rule is run again over D plus C5, and step 1 over C5's redrawn states. Either giving an outcome other than the ruled one asks R-US18 again. D itself changes only by amendment." This condenses spec.cycles, the version that has no bar clause. The C5 RE-ASK version's "and the bars fail until the ask is registered" is dropped.
- 847-850 (RULED): "the bars pass while the generated outcome equals the one ruled on ... A changed outcome fails until R-US18 is asked again". The cause list naming C5 is dropped. THE OUTCOME (815-818) is defined over D only, so a build can take "the generated outcome" to mean the D-only outcome.
- 822 and 844 show that "asked" does not mean a red bar in the fence's own words: "R-US18 is asked with the full table whatever the outcome" sits beside "NONE: a decided verdict passes". So "asks R-US18 again" alone does not make the bars fail.
- Two faithful builds therefore part. Build A prints the C5 re-ask and stays green while the D-only outcome equals the ruled one. Build B reads line 849's "changed outcome" as covering C5 and fails. The spec requires B. This is the finding's scenario, and it can happen once C5 is measured (due 2027).
- 841-843 (digest): "every figure steps 1-7 compare, under every reading: W per redrawn state-cycle under F-old and F-prop, and H, G, N and K for every row a step compares in D. Nothing printed only is in it." Line 858 keeps C5 out of D, so the spec's "C5 until it is measured" (include once measured) is gone for H, G, N and K.
- Where this bites: if the record is ASKED when C5 lands, the C5 clause cannot fire (there is no ruled outcome), and the spec's re-registration ("A moved digest fails until the ask is registered again with the new table, and Elias is told") would not trigger. Likewise the RULED re-pin naming "outcome unchanged" would not be required.

NOTHING OUTSIDE THE FENCE RESTORES EITHER CONDITION:
- Line 697 says text outside the markers is not part of the rule.
- Line 322's "an unregistered stop still fails" covers stops only.
- Lines 305 and 343 say only that C5 is BILLED and that adding it to D is an amendment.

ONE CORRECTION TO THE FINDING: "C5's figures never move the decisive digest" is too strong. The W clause on line 842 is not limited to D, and line 856 runs step 1 over C5's redrawn states. So under one reading C5's W values do enter the digest, while its H, G, N and K do not. That makes the digest a three-way ambiguity (none, W only, or all of C5) rather than a definite exclusion. It is still a real departure from the spec.

GRADE: minor, not defect. Line 849 lets a careful build reach the spec's RULED behaviour, and the digest's opening clause can be read to include C5. Each prong can be resolved two ways by faithful implementers, which is the rubric's definition of minor. But the fence is hashed at this commit, so the fix must go in before the digest is fixed.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Inside the fence, before the digest is fixed:
(1) Line 857: "Either giving an outcome other than the ruled one asks R-US18 again, and the bars fail until the ask is registered."
(2) Lines 842-843: "W per redrawn state-cycle under F-old and F-prop, and H, G, N and K for every row a step compares in D; once C5 is measured, in D plus C5, C5's redrawn state-cycles included. Nothing printed only is in it."
This covers both W and the rows. The finding's wording covered only the rows, and it should not suggest C5's W is excluded today.
(3) Optional, line 849: "A changed outcome, C5's re-run included, fails until R-US18 is asked again". This puts back the spec's link between C5 and the RULED clause.

### 87. The design block drops the spec's 'or a rounding', so it says only districts can move in C4

- **Lens:** implementability - **reviewer:** minor - **skeptic:** minor
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:305

**The scenario.** Line 305 says 'every anchored method returns its base but for a district within that swing of a tie'. But (b) and (b') move a seat when c·u lies within c·|Delta| of a rounding threshold, and (c) moves when 435·Shat lies near a half, with no district near a tie. The spec reads 'except for a district within 0.08 pp of a tie or a rounding'. As written, the sentence is false for (b), (b') and (c).

**The fix proposed.** '... but for a district or a rounding within that swing of a boundary'.

**The skeptic's evidence.** The staged docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md line 305 says: "C4's swing is close to zero, so every anchored method returns its base but for a district within that swing of a tie: C4 tests structure and step 1 ..., and can only veto a switch." The spec's cycles field (final_spec.json) says: "At Delta -0.08 pp every anchored method ((a), (b), (b') and (c)) returns its base, except for a district within 0.08 pp of a tie or a rounding." So the plan drops "or a rounding". The claim convention does not explain the cut: the convention removes derived figures, and this is a mechanism clause, not a figure.

The failing path, traced through the fence:
- **(b), fence 733-735.** `P(c, x)` = nearest integer to `c*min(1, max(0, x))`, and for n_T >= 2, `REP = min(n_T - U_D, max(U_R, R_B + P(c_T, uhat) - P(c_B, u)))`. A held state at C4 keeps its seats, and 2022 has no undecided seat, so m_B = n_B and c_T = c_B = c. That gives `REP = R_B + [P(c, u+Delta) - P(c, u)]`. The state moves one seat whenever c*u and c*(u+Delta) fall on either side of a half-integer. That is a state-level rounding; no district share is read. The clamps block it only where R_B = U_R or R_B = n_T - U_D. Only at n_T = 1 (fence 734) is (b)'s exception a district at a tie.
- **(b U-blind), fence 737.** The same, with c = m_B, n_T.
- **(b'), fence 738-744.** The same, with c*p(x); p is monotone for k > 0.
- **(c), fence 745-753.** `REP` = nearest integer to `435*Shat`. C4's window is {C1, C2}, which is not empty, so (c) is computed at C4. 435*S(2022) is an integer (435 decided seats). Any nonzero Delta moves Shat, by roughly rho times the swing. So whether (c) leaves its base depends on rho, never on a district.

The spec treats roundings as a source of movement in its own words. premises[3].reason: "A district or a rounding inside that gap could move a strict integer comparison". The rationale (BLINDNESS): "While weighing the C4 attack on S3, I sketched an illustrative rounding with an assumed, unfitted rho".

Why minor and not defect:
- Line 305 is outside the R-US18 markers, so the digest does not hold it.
- Nothing in the fence depends on it. Step 4 runs only on forward swing cycles, which excludes C4 (fence 752-753). Steps 2 and 5 test C4 only with "<=" ("not higher").
- Neither the design's asserted items nor its data-failure list checks C4 against the base, so no build would turn the sentence into a check.
- The sentence's conclusion still holds: C4 tests structure and step 1 and can only veto a switch.
- The spec's own premise P16 reason uses an even looser form ("leaves every anchored method at its base"). Line 305, though, gives an explicit exception list, so it reads as complete. As a complete list it is wrong for (b), (b U-blind), (b') and (c).

The result is a dropped spec condition in a rationale sentence, fixable by a plain edit with no amendment.

Two small corrections to the finding's scenario, neither changing the verdict:
- For (b'), the band is c*p(u) against c*p(u+Delta), not c*u within c*|Delta|.
- For (c), the base 435*S(B) is a whole number, not "near a half". (c) moves when rho times Delta carries 435*Shat half a seat from it.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** At line 305 (in US-11's Design block, outside the R-US18 markers, so no digest change and no amendment), replace "returns its base but for a district within that swing of a tie" with "returns its base except where the swing carries a district across a tie or a seat count across a rounding threshold (a state's under (b), (b U-blind) and (b'), the nation's under (c))". A minimal alternative is to restore the spec's words: "returns its base but for a district within that swing of a tie or a rounding".

### 88. Choice 2 types the derived count '213 of 435'

- **Lens:** implementability - **reviewer:** minor - **skeptic:** minor
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:866

**The scenario.** The count is the tool's district plurality (house_districts.md's pasted run: 'the districts' winners R 213'). It is a derived figure that the deliberate condensation and the claim convention keep out of the plan. The design block states the same fact without it (304: 'NY-22 2020, which goes to the Clerk's winner'). By contrast, '212 of 434' (799-800, 866) is [HH-DIV]'s own row in house_years.csv, a source figure of the kind the plan types elsewhere.

**The fix proposed.** 'The Clerk's count, NY-22 to the winner its count names ... by default; or [HH-DIV]'s 2020 row, NY-22 undecided.'

**The skeptic's evidence.** I could not refute this finding. It is a real style-rule breach of the claim convention. It sits outside the fence, so the digest and what the rule decides are unaffected, and the count itself is correct.

1. **213 is a count the tool computed. No saved source prints it.**
   - `ElectionsData/usa/house_districts.md`:35 is the pasted tool run: "House 2020: the districts' winners R 213 D 222 (none 0) - [HH-DIV] R 212 D 222, NY-22 vacant".
   - Reading 5 (line 55) draws the same line: "so the districts count 213 Republicans where the division prints 212 of 434". The count is derived; the 212 is printed.
   - `house_years.csv` has "2020,117,212,222,0,NY-22".
   - The saved [HH-DIV] page (`raw/records/history_house_party_divisions.html`) prints "117th (2021–2023) 435 6 222 212 0" with footnote 6 for NY-22.

2. **"The Clerk's 213" also gets the source wrong.** The Clerk's own 2020 *Statistics* prints 212, not 213. Its divisions table (`PoliSim-captures/sources/usa_us5/clerk/2020.raw.txt`:5363) reads "117th .. 2021-2023 .. 435 222 212 ...... 1": DEM 222, REP 212, one seat vacant, the same as [HH-DIV]. The 213 comes only from counting the winners in that publication's district tables. So the choice's "the Clerk vs [HH-DIV]" framing does not hold: both publications print 212. Elias, who gets this choice without the design block beside it, would find 212 if he checked the Clerk.

3. **The rules.**
   - `CLAUDE.md`:39-41 classes "a count, a figure ... 'there are N of X'" as DERIVED. Such a figure may only be GENERATED, REFERENCED or DELETED, and "Nobody transcribes, anywhere, in any file". `COMPLETED.md` is exempt; `docs/specs` is not.
   - The commit's own declared condensation keeps derived seat counts out of the plan.

4. **The plan states the same fact elsewhere without the number.**
   - Line 304 (the new design block): "it must give `[HH-DIV]` every year but NY-22 2020, which goes to the Clerk's winner".
   - Line 292 (already there): "the districts give `[HH-DIV]` in every year but its two footnoted vacancies".
   - Line 866 is the only place the 213 appears. It is choice 2, after `<!-- R-US18 RULE END -->` (line 860), so the fix changes no digest.

5. **Where the number came from.** The spec types it in its 'truth' field ("2020, C2's T: 213 REP / 222 DEM of 435, by the Clerk"). But its blind-conventions list says only "2020 by the Clerk", so the plan carried the number over from the truth field.

6. **212 of 434 can stay.** It is [HH-DIV]'s row as printed, and the fence's reading (i) at lines 799-800 already uses it in its definition.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Line 866: "2. *2020's truth.* The district plurality on the Clerk's counts, NY-22 to the winner that count names - the game counts all 435 seats by plurality and models no certification - by default; or `[HH-DIV]`'s 2020 row, 212 of 434, NY-22 undecided (the Clerk's own divisions table prints the same row)." This drops the transcribed 213 and the claim that the Clerk prints it. If the 213 is wanted on record, it goes to COMPLETED.md §791 or the card's GENERATED block. It is outside the fence, so the preregistered digest is unchanged.

### 89. The C5 re-run's inputs are not settled

- **Lens:** implementability - **reviewer:** note - **skeptic:** minor
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:300

**The scenario.** (1) Line 300 says 'Alaska 2024 ... is never a base in a deciding cycle', but 2024 is C5's base, and the C5 re-run (855-857) decides a re-ask; Alaska's deciding count there stays at first choices. (2) Step 1 over C5's redrawn states reads 'its lines changed' (710), i.e. lines_changed, which marks Missouri 2026 as redrawn although its saved note says the plan 'cannot be used'. in_force holds Missouri, but it is a column 'on which nothing decides' (300). (3) S7 prints 'that measurement' (809-811), but W's formula (759) assumes R_s + D_s + Z_s = n_s. A third-party seat therefore has no score until S7 is ruled, and the C5 re-run's other figures and stops differ by build.

**The fix proposed.** Say how C5's base reads Alaska 2024, which column decides held or redrawn for C5, and how a third-party seat is scored while S7 is being asked.

**The skeptic's evidence.** Only part (3) holds up. Parts (1) and (2) are settled by the text, and in both the plan matches the panel's spec.

(1) REFUTED. Fence 709 says "A district's deciding count is its final_r and final_d (house_districts.csv)". The catalog row is house_districts.csv:1767 `2024,AK,0,159550,156245,13010,329555,159550,156245,1,2,159550,156245,R,A`. Its final_r/final_d are the first choices, and they name the winner (R leads), so the identity holds. C5's base therefore reads Alaska at first choices, or at whatever final_r/final_d hold if its BILLED rounds are read first. Only one reading is possible.
- "never a base in a deciding cycle" (300) copies the spec ("never a base in a decisive cycle"). It is true in the plan's own terms: D = {C1, C2, C4} (705), "D itself changes only by amendment" (857-858), and "C5's role - whether 2026 joins D is an amendment" (343).
- At most a wording point: the C5 re-run can force a re-ask using this base.

(2) SETTLED, not ambiguous. Fence 710 ("its lines changed at no election after B up to T ... (house_maps.csv)") is the spec's method_a step 4: "redrawn for B->T if lines_changed = 1 at any election after B up to T". Design 300 calls in_force a column "on which nothing decides". So C5 reads lines_changed, and house_maps.csv:281 `2026,MO,8,1,...` makes Missouri redrawn in C5.
- The reviewer's consequence is accurate. This conflicts with 338 ("Missouri's 2026 plan - read as held by the saved note") and with play's in_force reading (324).
- The effect is small. With M = n = 8, F-old returns the held count, so only the H / step-1 split and F-prop's Missouri figure change.
- It also only applies if the 120th tab is not re-read before the 2026 statistics are saved (2027). Note-level.

(3) REAL. Fence 756-760 says "R_s and D_s are the record's decided seats by party" and "W_s = (|REPhat_s - R_s| + |DEMhat_s - D_s| - Z_s) / 2 ... the distance of REPhat_s from [R_s, R_s + Z_s]".
- The two forms agree only when REPhat_s + DEMhat_s = R_s + D_s + Z_s. Every method gives REPhat_s + DEMhat_s = n_s, so a seat won outside REP and DEM breaks the identity.
- Checked with a perl script. In a one-seat state an independent wins:
  - a REP call scores 0.5 by the formula, 0 under C# integer division, and 1 by the distance;
  - a DEM call scores 0.5, 0 and 0.
- K's three classes (763-764) have no place for such a member.
- STEP 7 (809-811) only stops with "printing that measurement", and the scoring is one of S7's options (834-835). Yet 856 runs steps 1-6 over D plus C5 before any ruling. And 806-807 make "which of S1, S2, S3, S5 and S6 fire" part of the outcome.
- So two faithful builds can list different stops, figures and decisive digests in the S7 ask.

Why this is not a defect:
- S7 can never be part of a ruled outcome: in 2016-2024 such a winner fails the truth gate (810-811, 323).
- So the re-ask and the bars behave the same in every build. Only the ask's table differs.
- It depends on a third-party House winner in 2026, which the plan foresees (S7 exists for it).

Adjacent, not graded: the C5 re-run also does not say how "a swing cycle by declaration" enters conditions that name C1 and C2 outright (775, 781-782, 793-794), or step 4's "a swing cycle of D" (752-753).

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Make (3) the only required change, inside the fence before its digest is fixed. Extend STEP 7 to say how a seat won outside REP and DEM is scored until S7 is ruled. For example: "until S7 is ruled, that cycle's figures are computed, and the stops listed, under each of S7's first two scorings - the seat misplaced for every method alike, and the seat undecided (added to Z_s)". Also:
- define W_s as the distance of REPhat_s from [R_s, R_s + Z_s], and say the halved formula holds only when R_s + D_s + Z_s = n_s;
- say which class K gives a delegation that has such a member.

Drop the Alaska clause: line 709 and the catalog already settle it. Missouri is optional and note-level. Either name lines_changed in line 710 and say C5 reads house_maps.csv as re-read with the 2026 statistics, or have C5 read in_force and drop "on which nothing decides" at line 300.

### 90. n_B is never defined

- **Lens:** implementability - **reviewer:** note - **skeptic:** note
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:741

**The scenario.** The fence defines n, n_T and m_B, but (b')'s fit filters on n_B >= 2 without defining it. It could mean apportioned seats or decided seats. For NC 2018 those are 13 and 12, and both pass, so nothing moves on the record. A one-seat state with an undecided base seat would make the builds differ.

**The fix proposed.** 'n_B the state's seats at B (house_maps.csv)'.

**The skeptic's evidence.** CLAIM HOLDS AS A WORDING GAP. `n_B` appears once in the whole plan, at docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:741: "over the base's states with n_B >= 2, c_B >= 1 and 0 < u < 1". A grep finds no definition anywhere. The fence defines only n (723, "the state's seats at T"), m_B (730, "the base's decided seats") and n_T (732, "n_T the state's seats at T"). This is not a transcription slip. The panel's final_spec.json method_b has the same undefined symbol ("binomial maximum likelihood over the base's states with n_B >= 2, c_B >= 1 and 0 < u_s < 1"). Its only seats-at-B notation is method_a's "seats(T) != seats(B)".

THE FINDING'S EXAMPLE IS WRONG. Take a one-seat state whose only base seat is undecided:
- Read as seats at B, n_B = 1. Read as decided seats, n_B = m_B = 0. Both fail >= 2.
- c_B = m_B - U_R - U_D = 0, which fails c_B >= 1 under either reading.
- Such a base is also a data failure in both builds: the design block says "M never zero" (line 301), lists "M = 0" among data failures (line 323), and counts a held district with no share "by F-old's quantile form over the rest", which is empty here.

So both builds treat it the same way. The only input that separates the readings is a two-seat state with one undecided and one contested base seat (n_B = 2, m_B = 1, c_B = 1, 0 < u < 1). It enters the fit as one trial under "seats at B" and is left out under "decided seats".

THAT INPUT NEVER OCCURS. Checked against the catalog:
- Across all 2,175 rows of house_districts.csv (435 a year for 2016-2024), the only row without an R/D winner is "2018,NC,9,...,-,N".
- Rows per state-year equal house_maps.csv's seats in all 300 map rows. The only state-year where decided seats fall below seats is NC 2018 (13 vs 12), and both values pass >= 2.
- No two-seat state at any base (HI ID ME NH RI, plus MT WV from 2022) has an N row.
- Seats change between B and T in no cycle except C3 (13 states), which is printed only. So even a "seats at T" misreading picks the same states in C1, C2, C4 and C5.
- In play the game decides every seat, so it creates no N rows.

Every plausible reading therefore picks the same states for (b')'s fit in every deciding cycle, including under all six S4 readings and in C5. The fit, S6 and the outcome come out the same.

The notation also points one way: n is seats and m is decided seats (n, n_T, m_B in the fence; seats(B) in the spec), and (b')'s >= 2 mirrors (b)'s "n_T >= 2" (plurality at one seat). A careful implementer would read "the state's seats at B".

It is still worth fixing now. Once the digest is fixed, even this wording change needs an "AMENDED (Fn)" ruling from Elias.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Define n_B where n_T is defined, inside the fence, before the digest is fixed. At USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:731-732, change "c_T = n_T - U_R - U_D, n_T the state's seats at T;" to "c_T = n_T - U_R - U_D, n_T and n_B the state's seats at T and at B (house_maps.csv);" and re-wrap the fence lines as needed.

Do not substitute m_B. That would change the meaning for the hypothetical input below, against the parallel with (b)'s n_T >= 2.

Correct the finding's example as well. A one-seat state with an undecided base seat is left out, or fails as M = 0, under every reading. The case that would separate the readings is a two-seat state with exactly one undecided and one contested base seat (n_B = 2, m_B = 1, c_B = 1). Neither the 2016-2024 record nor play can produce it. The only undecided seat is NC-9 2018, in a 13-seat state.

### 91. The bytes the rule digest is taken over are not named

- **Lens:** implementability - **reviewer:** note - **skeptic:** minor
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:697

**The scenario.** The lines between the markers include the ```text and ``` lines and one non-ASCII character: '§' on line 701, stored as UTF-8 C2 A7, in a file with LF endings and no BOM. A C# build that hashes a string as UTF-16 or ASCII never matches the preregistered digest, and fails 'a rule digest not on the instrument's list' with the block untouched.

**The fix proposed.** 'SHA-256 of the UTF-8 bytes of the lines between the markers, each ending LF.'

**The skeptic's evidence.** Staged docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:697 says: "The instrument holds the list of the block's SHA-256 digests (the lines between the markers, each ending LF)". This names the line endings but not the byte encoding, and nothing else in the plan defines the rule digest's bytes. The fence (line 841) defines only the decisive digest, and US-11's data failures only say "a rule digest not on the instrument's list".

The finding's facts check out, with one exception:
- The lines between the markers are 700-859. They include the ```text and ``` lines.
- The only non-ASCII byte is the § on line 701, stored as C2 A7.
- The index blob has LF endings and no BOM. The working copy, however, is CRLF (core.autocrlf=true, 1012 CRs), so "LF endings" holds for the blob only. The text's "each ending LF" already covers this.

The finding's own scenario (a C# build hashing UTF-16 or ASCII) is unlikely:
- Every text digest in Assets/Editor hashes either UTF-8 or the raw file bytes. The only Encoding.Unicode use is a save-migration registry marker. The only Encoding.ASCII use parses CSV lines after a digest taken on raw bytes (GeneratedCatalogCheck.cs:829-833).
- The pattern the plan names, UsStateSwingCheck.cs:92/98/322, reads with Encoding.UTF8, replaces CRLF with LF, and hashes `Encoding.UTF8.GetBytes(text)`.
- I compiled a probe on that pattern and ran it on the working copy. It gives f2120d17... Encoding.Unicode would give 2999a73a... and Encoding.ASCII 2ee7f611..., so such a build would fail as described. But nothing points an implementer that way.

A real failing path exists elsewhere, at this commit's preregistration step, which has no code yet (no rule-digest script exists and COMPLETED.md §791 is unwritten):
- Perl run under -CSD, a flag this session uses regularly, decodes § to U+00A7. That code point is below U+0100, so Digest::SHA hashes it as the single byte A7 and raises no wide-character croak. The result is 7fdfefb4..., the same as hashing Latin-1 bytes.
- Both computations fit the text: SHA-256 over "the lines between the markers, each ending LF".
- If §791 records 7fdfefb4..., an instrument built on the named pattern fails with "a rule digest not on the instrument's list" even though the block is untouched. The only cures are then a Latin-1 instrument or a re-recorded "preregistered" digest.

It is not a defect: the digest only guards the text, every mismatch fails loudly, and no path decides differently. Line 697 is outside the markers, so fixing it now leaves the fence's digest unchanged.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** At line 697, replace "(the lines between the markers, each ending LF)" with "(the UTF-8 bytes, no BOM, of the lines between the markers - the ```text and closing ``` lines included - each ending LF)". Compute the preregistered digest from raw bytes: perl with '<:raw', CR stripped, never under -CSD, where § hashes as the single byte A7. Before §791 records the digest, check that it equals what the instrument's pattern gives: File.ReadAllText(path, Encoding.UTF8), CRLF -> LF, SHA-256 of Encoding.UTF8.GetBytes over the same lines. This sentence is outside the markers, so the edit leaves the fenced block's digest unchanged.

### 92. 'Every comparison is strict' sits beside non-strict comparisons

- **Lens:** implementability - **reviewer:** note - **skeptic:** note
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:765

**The scenario.** Steps 1, 2 and 5 use 'not above', '<=', '>=' and 'not higher'. Here 'strict' means exact (no noise band), not strict inequality. A reader who applied it to the C4 condition (776) would require H(b) < H(a) there.

**The fix proposed.** 'Every comparison is exact, on integer seats, with no noise band.'

**The skeptic's evidence.** I checked the staged copy (`git show :docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md`, which matches the working tree). Line 765 inside the fence reads: "- Every comparison is strict, on integer seats, with no noise band. Equal is never better."

The same digest-frozen block uses the word "strictly" to mean strict inequality in five places:
- 769 "If F-prop's sum is strictly lower"
- 782 "strictly larger than the other's in both C1 and C2"
- 787 "strictly below the step-2 method's"
- 789 "strictly below no change's"
- 793 "H strictly lower in C1 and in C2"

It also states non-strict comparisons:
- 768 "not above F-prop's, ties included"
- 773 "not above"
- 776 "H(b) <= H(a) in C4"
- 778 "K(b) >= K(a)"
- 794 "H not higher in C4 ... K not lower in any cycle"

So "strict" carries two meanings in one block, and in the block's own usage line 765 is literally contradicted. The wording clash the finding describes is real.

It cannot change what the rule decides, so it is not minor:
- Every comparison that decides something has its own operator written out. No decisive comparison relies on line 765 to choose between < and <=.
- The C4 guard named in the scenario is non-strict twice: at 776 ("<=") and again at 794 ("H not higher in C4").
- Line 813 ("TIE-BREAKS: equal sums in step 2 go to (a), in step 1 to F-old") settles ties in steps 1 and 2 on its own.
- Line 818 says "C4 can only veto".
- A reader who put "<" at C4 would have to override two explicit lines, so two faithful implementations do not diverge.
- "Equal is never better" holds everywhere. Ties are "not worse" in the veto conditions (C4, K) but never count as an improvement.

It is not a departure from the panel either. In final_spec.json, spec.rule line 3 reads: "Every comparison is strict, on integer seats, with no noise band: neither precedent uses one, and any band would be an invented number." The panel uses the word the same way in premises[3].reason ("could move a strict integer comparison"). The plan dropped only the justification clause, and "with no noise band" still shows which meaning is intended.

It is worth fixing only because the block's SHA-256 is fixed at this commit. After that, the same one-word change would need an "AMENDED (Fn)" ruling.

The finding's scenario overstates the risk. The text already handles C4. Grade it as wording, not as ambiguity.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Change line 765 before the digest is fixed, re-wrapped to the block's width:
"- Every comparison is made exactly as its operator is written, on integer counts, with no noise band. Equal is never better."

Why this wording:
- It takes the word "strict" out of a block that uses "strictly" only for strict inequality.
- It says outright that each written operator governs, which closes off the misreading.
- "Counts" also covers K, which counts delegations, not seats.

The reviewer's shorter version is also acceptable: "Every comparison is exact, on integer seats, with no noise band."

Either way, the meaning stays the panel's (spec.rule line 3). No other line changes.

### 93. Printing is narrowed: 'every half rule that fired' replaces the spec's 'every tie-break that fired'

- **Lens:** implementability - **reviewer:** note - **skeptic:** note
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:314

**The scenario.** The spec prints every tie-break that fired: step 1's and step 2's equal sums, a district at exactly 0.5 going to its base winner, and (c)'s half going to B's majority. The half rule covers only the state-then-national cascade. This affects printing only; no outcome changes.

**The fix proposed.** '... and every tie-break and half rule that fired'.

**The skeptic's evidence.** The finding holds. The word change narrows what the instrument prints, but nothing decides on it.

What the spec says (final_spec.json):
- measures item 9: "EXCEPTIONS, named: ... the labels of WA-8 2018 and VT 2024, and every tie-break that fired."
- stop_and_ask, "PRINTED, NEVER A STOP": "the tie-breaks that fired;"
- rule: "TIE-BREAK: equality on step 2's sum goes to (a), and on step 1's to F-old."
- P12, "Ties stay with the base", has three cases:
  - a district at exactly 0.5 goes to its base winner;
  - a rounding half goes to the state's base majority, then to the national base majority;
  - a half in (c) goes to T-1's majority.

What the staged plan says (docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md):
- Line 314 has "... and every half rule that fired;". A grep for "fired" finds no other line, so nothing else in the plan carries the spec's two print items.
- The fence (lines 699-860) defines the term narrowly at lines 713-714: "The half rule: a half goes to the party that held more of the state's base seats, then to the national base majority." The term is used only at:
  - 726: F-old's q(k) at exactly 0.5;
  - 733: P;
  - 739: (b');
  - 755: no change.
- These tie rules are not called the half rule:
  - 720-721: a held district goes to "its base winner's party at exactly 0.5";
  - 723-724: F-old with M = n counts "#{x(j) = 0.5 whose base winner is REP}";
  - 734: (b) with n_T = 1 at exactly 0.5;
  - 747: (c) has "a half going to B's majority";
  - 813: "TIE-BREAKS: equal sums in step 2 go to (a), in step 1 to F-old."

So an implementer who follows the plan's defined term prints a subset of what the spec asks for. The (c) half could be argued in loosely, since line 301 already uses "half" loosely ("each element's half"). The equal-sum tie-breaks of steps 1 and 2 are not halves under any reading.

Why "note" and not more:
- Line 314 is outside the fence, so the fix changes no digest and needs no amendment.
- The exceptions list is not pinned (line 319 pins REP seats, N, G, H, K and the contingent outcome).
- The list is not in the decisive digest ("Nothing printed only is in it").
- The halves that actually fire on the record are firings of the defined half rule, which the plan prints. Examples are no change in C3 for re-apportioned states with an even delegation, such as 7 of 14 carried onto 13.
- A share or q(k) at exactly 0.5, or 435*Shat at exactly k+0.5, needs an exact floating-point coincidence. Bases have no tie for first (GeneratedCatalogCheck). At zero swing, 435*R/434 is a half only at R = 217, and no base has that.
- The one omitted case that can plausibly fire is an equal integer sum in step 1 or 2. It is still visible: line 315's decision table marks each figure "better, equal or worse" and shows the outcome. It is just not named as a tie-break that fired.

On the proposed fix: "every tie-break and half rule that fired" is still incomplete. In the plan's vocabulary "tie-break" means the fence's TIE-BREAKS heading (steps 1 and 2), so a share at exactly 0.5 going to its base winner and (c)'s half going to B's majority would still be left out.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Line 314, outside the fence, so no digest changes: replace "and every half rule that fired;" with "and every tie that fired, named by its rule - a share or q(k) at exactly 0.5 (to its base winner, or by the half rule), a rounding half (the half rule), (c)'s half (to B's majority), and equal sums in steps 1 and 2 (the TIE-BREAKS);". This restores the spec's "every tie-break that fired" (measures 9; stop_and_ask's printed list) and keeps the plan's terms.

### 94. 'Every method is anchored' does not hold for F-prop, which is a buildable option

- **Lens:** implementability - **reviewer:** note - **skeptic:** note
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:299

**The scenario.** F-prop is (b0), the unanchored rule (727, 736), and '(a) with F-prop' is a buildable ruling (851). Under an F-prop ruling, a redrawn state's level moves to proportionality's at the first game election, which the sentence says never happens.

**The fix proposed.** 'Every deciding method is anchored ...; F-prop, if ruled, is not (S5's options say so).'

**The skeptic's evidence.** I could not refute it. A buildable ruling leads to exactly the case the sentence says never happens.

Line 299 (the staged text matches the working tree): "Every method is anchored on the previous House - in play the game's own (R-US11 (a)) - so a method's level never jumps at the first game election, and the measurement compares responses, not proportionality's level."

How it fails:
1. The ruling is open to Elias. Line 831 offers "S5: F-old; F-prop, persisting in every state it counts", and line 851 lists "(a) with F-old or with F-prop" as an option that can be built.
2. The first game election is 5 Nov 2024 with the record's 2022 as its base. Line 324: "the record's 2022 for 5 Nov 2024, then the game's own". Line 382: "a 2024-start game elects its own 119th Congress".
3. Five states are redrawn for that election. In `house_maps.csv`, the 2024 rows with lines_changed=1 are AL, GA, LA, NY and NC, all multi-seat (lines 207, 216, 224, 238, 239).
4. F-prop does not return the base. Line 727: "F-prop ... a redrawn state counted as (b0) counts it". Line 736: "(b0), unanchored: REP = U_R + P(c_T, uhat)". At Delta = 0 that gives U_R + P(c_T, u), not R_B. The plan's own line 302 says "(b0) - also F-prop - which at zero swing against the base delegations prints proportionality's level error".

So under an F-prop ruling those five states move to proportionality's level at the first game election.

The plan's own wording agrees with the finding. Line 305 says "every anchored method returns its base" and line 307 says "for an anchored method its base"; both assume some methods are not anchored. The sentence's second half is too broad as well. Step 1 compares F-old with F-prop's proportional level, which is its stated purpose at line 305: "show whether a new map looked like the old one or like proportional".

This is not a departure from the panel's spec. The spec's rationale says almost the same: "Every method is also anchored on the previous House ... A method's level therefore never jumps at the first game election, and the measurement compares responses rather than proportionality's level." The plan carried that over-broad claim across.

Why the severity stays at note:
- Line 299 is outside the R-US18 fence, so it changes neither the digest nor what the rule decides.
- The fence defines "(a) is district uniform swing with F-old". For the methods as defined ((a), (b), (c), and (b') too), the sentence is true.
- Lines 302, 318, 324, 727, 736 and 831 state F-prop's unanchored level plainly. A faithful implementer would add no zero-swing identity for F-prop, so the instrument cannot be built two ways from this.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Replace the sentence with: "(a) with F-old, (b), (b') and (c) are anchored on the previous House - in play the game's own (R-US11 (a)) - so their level never jumps at the first game election, and step 2 compares responses, not proportionality's level. F-prop is not anchored: a redrawn state it counts moves to proportionality's level at its first count and stays there (S5's option), and step 1 is the comparison that measures that level."

The reviewer's wording, "Every deciding method", would drop (c), which the plan treats as non-deciding (line 311 tilt; "never recommended alone") even though (c) is anchored. It would also leave the over-broad clause "the measurement compares responses" unchanged.

## The first pass - refuted by the skeptics

- [rule-fidelity] Decisive digest's 'in D' drops the spec's 'C5 until it is measured', so C5 never enters it - *The fence (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md) keeps the spec's own scope for H, G, N and K. Spec stop_and_ask: "SHA-256 over every figure steps 1-7 read under every reading: W per redrawn state-cycle, and H, G, N and K for every deciding and guarding row in D." Spec cycles field: "D itself changes only by an amendment." Fence 841-843: "every figure steps 1-7 compare, under every reading: W per redrawn state-cycle under F-old and F-prop, and H, G, N and K for every row a step compares in D." Fence 857-858: "D itself changes only by amendment." So the "in D" restriction and the line-857 sentence are faithful copies of the spec, not departures. The only thing dropped is the spec's parenthetical "(... and C5 until it is measured)", which is empty before measurement because C5 is BILLED and has no figures.

The scenario fails at "under the fence's list the digest does not move":
(1) The W clause has no "in D" on it. Fence 856-857 runs "step 1 over C5's redrawn states", and the lead clause covers "every figure steps 1-7 compare". The catalog has ten 2026 rows with lines_changed=1 (house_maps.csv: AL, CA, FL, LA, MO, NC, OH, TN, TX, UT). So once C5 is measured, C5's W under F-old and F-prop enter the digest and it moves. In ASKED, fence 845-846 then applies: "A moved digest fails until the ask is registered again with the new table, and Elias is told." That is the spec's behaviour.
(2) Even under the strained reading where "in D" also scopes W, the bars do not stay green. Plan line 319 (US-11 Design) says "pins: every measured cycle and row ... A moved pin or an unpinned measured row fails by name", so C5's new rows fail the bars.
(3) "Elias is never told" is false under every reading. The first ruling records the outcome it was made on, and fence 857 ("Either giving an outcome other than the ruled one asks R-US18 again") then asks again if the run over D plus C5 differs. US-16's guard (853) needs RULED with a current digest, so nothing goes live on a stale ruling. The spec's C5 clause has the same "the ruled one" wording, so it is equally inert in ASKED. Both texts rely on the digest there, and the fence's digest does move.

The second ambiguity (all four of H, G, N and K for each compared row, or only the figures actually compared) comes from the spec's own "every figure ... read: ... H, G, N and K for every deciding and guarding row". It decides nothing: per line 319, any change to a row's REP seats, N, G, H or K already moves a pin and fails by name under both readings.

What remains is wording only. Without the spec's "until it is measured", the lead clause includes C5's H, G, N and K once measured, while the list ("in D") leaves them out. The two readings differ only if R-US18 is still ASKED when a later correction changes C5's held-state figures but not its redrawn states' W. The pins fail in both cases, so the only difference is whether the step is re-registering the ask or re-pinning. No outcome changes. The finding's own fix would also narrow the spec's per-row set ("every H, G, N and K a step compares"), which is a new departure from the spec.*
- [rule-fidelity] 'Every comparison is strict' contradicts the rule's own non-strict conditions - *1. Line 765 does not depart from the spec. It copies spec.rule line 3 word for word ("Every comparison is strict, on integer seats, with no noise band: neither precedent uses one, and any band would be an invented number." / "Equal is never better."). Only the reason after the colon was dropped, and the meaning is unchanged.

2. "Strict" here means exact, not a strict inequality. The same sentence explains it: "with no noise band". The spec uses the word the same way elsewhere: premises[3].reason ("A district or a rounding inside that gap could move a strict integer comparison") and accepted[0].attack ("one strict sum over nine state-cycles"). The spec also states the C4 intent outright. accepted[10].change: "(b) must not be worse but is never required to be strictly better". spec.cycles: "C4 can only veto a switch (not worse)".

3. Every comparison in the fence has its own operator or qualifier, checked in the index copy (`git show :docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md`, lines 699-860; the unstaged diff is empty). No comparison is decided by line 765 alone:
   - Step 1: L768 "not above ..., ties included"; L769 "strictly lower".
   - Step 2: L773 "not above"; L775 "H(b) < H(a) in C1 and in C2"; L776 "H(b) <= H(a) in C4"; L777 "<"; L778 "K(b) >= K(a)".
   - Step 3: L782 "strictly larger".
   - Step 4: L786 "at least two"; L787 and L789 "strictly below".
   - Step 5: L793-794 "H strictly lower ... H not higher in C4 ... G strictly lower ... K not lower".
   - Share thresholds: L720, L723, L726 and L734 each say what happens "at exactly 0.5".
   - (b') fit: L741-744 "n_B >= 2, c_B >= 1 and 0 < u < 1", "below 1e-12", "at most 100", "k <= 0".
   - (c) window: L750 "more than half".

4. Reading every comparison as strict is not a consistent reading of the block. It contradicts L768 "ties included", L776 "<=", L778 ">=", and L794 "not higher" and "not lower". It also contradicts L813 "TIE-BREAKS: equal sums in step 2 go to (a), in step 1 to F-old": with a strict step-2 test, a tie could not go to (a). The finding's scenario, a C4 tie turning "not worse" into "strictly better", only happens if an implementer replaces the explicit "<=" on L776 (and "not higher" on L794) with the general sentence. That is not a faithful implementation, so two faithful implementations reach the same outcome.

5. What remains is wording only. "Strict" on L765 means exact, while the same hashed block uses "strictly" seven times to mean a strict inequality. "On integer seats" is also loose: K counts delegations, not seats, and the 0.5 threshold tests are on shares. Neither changes what the rule decides.*
- [rule-fidelity] Seat counts transcribed, against the claim convention and the commit's own condensation - *REFUTED. There is no breach of the convention and no failing path.

1) Every figure the finding cites is in the panel's own spec. In final_spec.json:
   - rule, step 6: "(i) 2020 at [HH-DIV]: 212 of 434, with NY-22 undecided and scored by interval"
   - method_c: "2020: 213/435 (212/434 under S4's reading (i))"
   - premises: "The 2020 truth is the Clerk's 213 of 435. [HH-DIV]'s 212 of 434 is S4's reading (i)."
   - truth: "(PartySystems Usa2020, ChamberSizeAt 434)"
   So the fence at lines 799-800, choice 2 at line 866 and line 304 are faithful to the spec.

2) The convention does not reach sourced figures about the world.
   - CLAUDE.md:39 defines DERIVED as "a fact about the code or the environment".
   - The founding ruling's own review, COMPLETED.md:17845, says: "KEEP | Its DERIVED claims are about the world, not the code - sourced figures with source, vintage and basis ... It is already correct practice."
   - The banned list at COMPLETED.md:17908-17913 is: counts of code things, line numbers, a measured figure presented as current, build status, environment facts.
   - The House Historian's 117th division, cited by its key [HH-DIV], and the Clerk-count plurality are historical election figures with their sources. They are on neither list.
   - Precedent: the plan already carried them at 8f7b87fa, line 296: "`[HH-DIV]` (REP 222 / DEM 213; REP 220 / DEM 215)". The record house_districts.md:55 says: "the districts count 213 Republicans where the division prints 212 of 434".
   - DocumentClaimCheck.cs:64 checks only backticked Type.Member references, so no bar changes.

3) The failure path is blocked.
   - house_years.csv's 2020 row is "2020,117,212,222,0,NY-22".
   - us_house_prep.pl:613-618 requires NY-22 2020's winner to be R, then does `$r--`, then asserts `problem(...) unless $r == $h->{R} ...`.
   - R-K9 (house_districts.md:3) is "a re-run of the tool over the saved pages". So [HH-DIV]'s 212 cannot move unless the districts move with it.
   - If both ever moved, reading (i) itself would have changed. An amendment is then the right response for a preregistered block, and the card is digest-stamped anyway.

4) The figures remove ambiguity rather than create it. Line 746 says "S = REP seats / decided seats", and with NY-22 undecided that gives 212/434. Line 800's explicit "(c)'s S(2020) = 212/434" makes reading (i) reach (c)'s rho fit, not only the scoring.

5) "The Clerk's 213 of 435" is not false. It is shorthand for line 304's "district plurality on the Clerk's counts". It parallels "[HH-DIV]'s 212 of 434", where "of 434" is itself a sum: the page prints 435 seats with footnote 6.

6) Line 304 is true as written. PartySystem.cs:776 gives `("DEM", 222), ("REP", 212)`, and :793 gives `case ElectionVintage.Usa2020: return 434;`. The 434 is also the record's own figure, and the sentence's claim is only that the item leaves that chamber untouched.

7) "The commit's own condensation" is the session's description to reviewers, not a claim written in the plan. The plan does drop the spec's measured figures: the truth table (241/194, 199/235), the delegations of record, step 1's state list, "173 to 181 districts" and "REP held exactly 26". Only the two conventions' defining figures remain.*
- [rule-fidelity] Dropped spec clause: a state with one seat at both elections is held - *The sentence is missing from the fence, but the scenario can never happen, and nothing the rule decides would change if it did.

1. On every row the rule reads, the clause adds nothing. In ElectionsData/usa/house_maps.csv, every one-seat state from 2016 to 2026 is 'seats=1, lines_changed=0': AK, DE, ND, SD, VT, WY, and MT through 2020. MT's 2022 change from 1 to 2 seats is not 'one seat at both elections', so the clause does not cover it. On this data, the fence's test at lines 710-711 ('held if its lines changed at no election after B up to T and its seats did not change') already calls every at-large state held. The spec agrees: its cycles field says 'The six at-large states are held'.

2. No input can trigger the scenario.
   - Tools/us_house_prep.pl line 224 reads each mid-decade Census tab's list of states that 'delineated new boundaries'. A one-district state has no inner boundaries to redraw.
   - Lines 245-252 read the 2022 geography page and assert that its exceptions are exactly the states with one seat in 2022 (seats(2022, $_) == 1).
   - The fence fixes the cycles: 'D itself changes only by amendment' (line 858). C5 is measured on the 2026 rows already read, where all six at-large states are 0. A later Census list can only reach the rule through an amendment, and an amendment re-hashes the fence anyway.

3. Even if a one-seat state were marked redrawn, no step's verdict would change. With M = n = 1, every method makes the same call for that state:
   - held (a), lines 720-721: plurality on dhat, the base winner's party at 0.5;
   - F-old with M = n, lines 723-724: '#{x(j) > 0.5} plus #{x(j) = 0.5 whose base winner is REP}', the same call;
   - F-prop: 'as (b0) counts it' (line 727), and (b0) is 'as (b) at one seat' (line 736);
   - (b) at n_T = 1 (line 734) uses uhat = u + Delta. Here u = d exactly, because u = sum final_r / sum(final_r + final_d) over the one district (line 729), and 'A state with c_B = 0 takes u = R_B / m_B' (line 732);
   - (b') replaces only P (line 738), which the one-seat branch does not use.
   The at-large base rows in house_districts.csv confirm that d = u in every case. There are no U rows. The one-party finals (VT 2016 at final_r = 0; SD 2020, ND 2022 and SD 2022 at final_d = 0) give d = u = 0 or 1. So the state's W would drop out of H(a), H(b) and H(b') by the same amount and enter both step-1 sums equally. The comparisons in steps 1, 2 and 5 are differences or strict comparisons, so they are unchanged. G, N and K do not depend on the held/redrawn split. The (c) window counts raw changed seats in both the spec ('the seats of states with lines_changed = 1 or a change of seats') and the fence (lines 750-751). Only the printed H totals and the decisive digest would differ.

The design block's line 302 ('at one seat it is identical to (a) in a held state') is consistent with this. The spec's sentence restates what its binary rule already gives on the catalog. Dropping it changes no classification on any row read and no outcome. The reviewer's 'note' rating is too high: it is not a departure in meaning.*
- [design-fidelity] "The item's own cycles {C2, C4}" against "the item's text, which lists 2018→2020, 2020→2022 and 2022→2024" - *The failure path cannot happen, and the finding's key premise is false.

1. The plan does say why 2020→2022 drops out. Line 305: "C3, the census redistricting, is printed in R-US14's stress-case form ...: play meets no census redistricting before the 2030 apportionment is sourced (R-US13 (a)), and its first redraws are mid-decade ones, the kind C1, C2 and C4 hold." This is the spec's own "WHY C3 DOES NOT DECIDE", nearly word for word. The other reason is in the item's own text in the same entry, line 294: "2020→2022 (the redraw: (b) and (c) only)". So nothing has to be inferred to see why the item has no (a)-against-(b) comparison at C3.

2. Inside the fence, C3's exclusion is a term, not a reading. Lines 705-706: "C3 is printed, never deciding." Every step-6 reading, (ii) included, runs under that term, so no reading can put C3 into D.

3. Wherever the label is used, the set is spelled out. Fence line 801: "(ii) the item's own cycles, D = {C2, C4}". Choice 1, line 865: "or the item's own cycles {C2, C4}". Line 305 only points to "S4's reading (ii)", which spells out the set. Two faithful implementers, or Elias choosing between the two options, cannot read this as three cycles.

4. The amendment path writes in the option as it was offered. Line 864: "A choice ruled before the run - either way - is written into the block by amendment". The second option is reading (ii)'s D = {C2, C4}, and no text maps it to C3 in D. C3 could enter D only if Elias ruled it explicitly, outside both options, against the reason line 305 gives. That would be a ruling, not a misreading.

5. This is a faithful transcription, not a departure. The spec's cycles field uses the same label ("The item's own cycles: S4's reading (ii) re-runs steps 1 and 2 on {C2, C4}"), and so do its rule ("(ii) the item's own cycles, D = {C2, C4}") and its rationale ("Every declared convention ([HH-DIV]'s 2020, the item's own cycles, ..."). Line 305 replaces the spec's other remark ("the item's '(b) and (c) only' is honoured for deciding") with the spec's "WHY C3 DOES NOT DECIDE". The rationale is condensed, but the meaning is unchanged: C3 never decides. No CLAUDE.md rule requires both reasons.*
- [design-fidelity] The reconciliation arithmetic for a buildable option sits outside the digest, though the intro says the arithmetic is the block's - *The location is true but it is by design, and nothing the rule decides depends on it.

1. The intro does not contradict the placement. Line 697 reads: "US-11's *Design* holds the methods' inputs, assertions, outputs and use in play; the arithmetic, the scoring and the decision are the block's." The reconciliation is use in play. It applies only "If Elias rules its total in" (line 303), right after "In play, if ever ruled, its window is C1, C2 and C4". Other play arithmetic also lives in the Design without anyone calling it a breach, for example line 301: "in play a held state whose prior is an F-old set is counted as the set, each element's half going to its stored call". So "the arithmetic" in the intro means the arithmetic that is measured.

2. This is not a departure from the spec. The panel's spec keeps the reconciliation in `method_c` ("DELEGATIONS: ... reconciled to it: Dlt = Rhat_c - Rhat_m ..."), not in its `rule` field. Its rule cites it from outside: "(c)'s total with that method's delegations reconciled (method_c)". P17 fences only "The rule's text". Its stated reason is "a red bar cannot be turned green by retuning the rule". The plan's fence already holds more than that, because it adds the measured methods' arithmetic.

3. The failing path does not exist.
   - No row of the instrument computes a reconciled (c). The measures list has none.
   - Line 319 pins only "for (c) REP seats and N".
   - Line 299 says "(c) gives control only".
   - Step 4 (lines 785-790) reads only (c)'s |N|.
   - The decisive digest (lines 841-843) covers "every figure steps 1-7 compare".
   - RULED (line 847) checks the generated outcome, not how an option is built.
   - So a later edit to the reconciliation cannot move a step, a stop, the outcome, a pin, either digest or a bar. The finding's "and so K" is false: K is never defined for (c).
   - The contingent call under a hypothetical "(c) reconciled" ruling would be play behaviour. That is governed like every other play rule in the Design, by the DECLARED-design rule, not by the digest.

4. "The next rounding threshold" resolves one way.
   - In the fence, (b) rounds only through P(c_T, uhat) for n_T >= 2 (lines 733-735) and compares with 0.5 at n_T = 1 (line 734).
   - The same sentence says "by vote share".
   - So the next threshold toward the favoured party is (j+0.5)/c_T or (j-0.5)/c_T with j = P(c_T, uhat), which is the spec's formula. (b) never rounds on n_T, so there is nothing "measured on n_T" to choose instead.

Leftovers outside this finding's claim:
   - The fence's S3 option (line 827) dropped the spec's "(method_c)" pointer, so the fence alone does not say where "reconciled" is defined.
   - The Design's "the result asserted to every state's seats and to 435" drops the spec's "and to every bound". This is a dropped assertion; "a state at its clamp excluded" still keeps (b) inside its bounds.*
- [design-fidelity] "Without a deciding count" replaces the spec's final_r = final_d = 0 - *I could not make the failing path happen, and on every row the instrument can read, the plan's wording and the spec's give the same result.

1. The division only runs on base rows. Fence, staged line 711: "A base district's share d: none if it has no winner and no votes (N); ... otherwise final_r / (final_r + final_d)". C5 is 2024->2026, so a 2026 row is only ever T and never reaches the 0/0 division. In play, B is the record's 2022 and then the game's own stored shares, never a record row. The bases of C1-C5 are therefore the fixed 2016-2024 rows.

2. The catalog check already rejects the scenario row. Assets/Editor/GeneratedCatalogCheck.cs:762-766:
   `string lead = r.FinalR > r.FinalD ? "R" : r.FinalD > r.FinalR ? "D" : "-"; bool unopposed = r.Flags.IndexOf('U') >= 0; if (unopposed ? r.FinalR + r.FinalD != 0 || r.VotesTotal != 0 : lead != r.Winner) wrong.Add(...)`
   A non-U row with final_r = final_d = 0 and winner O gets lead "-", which is not "O", so the re-read fails. Plan line 323 lists "`GeneratedCatalogCheck`'s re-read failing" as a data failure. A 0/0 row with an R or D winner fails the same way. Line 785 (`vacancyRight`) limits a row with no winner to the footnoted vacancy.

3. On the record, every reading of the phrase picks the same rows. In house_districts.csv the three predicates "votes_total = 0", "flag N or U" and "final_r = final_d = 0" coincide exactly: 12 rows have all three (the U rows in FL, OK and LA, plus NC-9 2018, which is N), and the other 2,163 rows have none. The one reading that differs, "the columns parse", makes the assertion vacuous and contradicts its own "outside N and U" exclusion, since N and U rows parse too. A faithful implementation would not read it that way.

4. No fact is lost by the Inputs line. The CSV's own header says "final_r / final_d: the deciding count's ... 0 where no votes were printed", and the fence (line 709) defines the deciding count as final_r and final_d.

5. Lines 301 and 323 sit outside the `<!-- R-US18 RULE BEGIN/END -->` markers. Tightening them later does not move the preregistered digest and needs no amendment, so this does not have to be fixed at this commit.

What is left is wording: "without a deciding count" is less exact than the spec's predicate.

Aside, separate from this finding: line 764 fails every non-U row with an O winner, because lead is never "O". S7 ("a measured cycle read after 2024 has a winner outside REP and DEM") therefore cannot be reached until this check is extended for the 2026 rows.*
- [design-fidelity] (b)'s assertion that c_T >= 0 is dropped - *The text claim is accurate, but the failure path cannot happen on any input the plan admits.

1. The omission is real as transcription. The spec's method_b says "c_T = n_T - U_R - U_D, asserted >= 0." The staged plan states c_T only in the fence (lines 731-732: "c_B = m_B - U_R - U_D; c_T = n_T - U_R - U_D, n_T the state's seats at T"), uses it at lines 735-737 and names it at line 302 ("At zero swing with c_T = c_B"). None of these lines asserts ">= 0". The data-failure list at line 323 does not have it either. Its "a method's output outside its bounds" follows the spec's own list, where (b)'s bounds come from (a)'s "each Rhat_s in [0, n_s]". The finding's arithmetic is also right: with n_T=2, U_R=2 and U_D=1, the formula min(1, max(2, ...)) gives 1, which lies inside [0, 2].

2. The scenario needs U_R + U_D > n_T, and no data the plan admits can produce that:
- **The record.** From house_districts.csv (flag U) and house_maps.csv (seats), the smallest c_T is 1 in every cycle B=2016, 2018, 2020 and 2022. The record's U seats are: 2016 FL-24, OK-1; 2018 FL-10, 14, 21, 24; 2020 FL-25; 2022 FL-5, LA-4; 2024 FL-20, OK-3. C3's seat-losing states (CA, IL, MI, NY, OH, PA, WV) have no U seat in 2020.
- **C5.** Its base is 2024 (FL-20, OK-3) at the same apportionment.
- **Play.** The ruled R-US13 (a) at line 643 says "Carry forward the 2020-census allocation ... until the 2030 apportionment is sourced". Line 324 repeats it ("R-US13 (a) carries 2020's forward"). So n_T = n_B, and since U <= m_B <= n_B, c_T >= c_B >= 0.
- **The rolled-forward U seats.** These are 2022's FL-5 and LA-4, one per state. Every state keeps at least one seat, so c_T >= 0 even after a sourced 2030 apportionment. At n_T = 1, (b) uses plurality and never reads c_T.
- **The finding's example.** It needs three unopposed seats in one state that drops to two seats. No such state exists in the record or in play.

3. The plan already carries an equivalent check. Line 302 heads (b)'s list "*(b), asserted.*" and includes "U seats stay their party's". Checked on the output, that means U_R <= REP_s <= n_T - U_D, which can hold only when c_T >= 0. In the finding's own example, REP=1 < U_R=2 would fail it.

4. Nothing in the fence is at stake. The plan puts assertions in the design block, outside the hashed markers (line 298: "this list holds everything else"; line 667: "US-11's *Design* holds the methods' inputs, assertions, outputs and use in play"). Restoring the spec's clause therefore changes no digest and needs no amendment.*
- [design-fidelity] Missouri in C5: the measurement reads lines_changed, play reads in_force - *The scenario's second implementation (C5's held test reads in_force) breaks the very line the finding cites, so no faithful implementation reaches it.

1. docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:300 (the design block, which the fence's intro at line 697 makes the home of the inputs: "US-11's *Design* holds the methods' inputs, assertions, outputs and use in play") says: "`house_maps.csv` for held and redrawn. ... The tool gains two generated columns on which nothing decides: `in_force` (`lines_changed`, but Missouri 2026 held ...)". Classing a state for step 1 and H decides, so the measurement cannot read in_force.

2. The fence (710-711) says "held for B->T if its lines changed at no election after B up to T and its seats did not change (house_maps.csv)". Those words are the column's own name and meaning:
   - house_maps.csv's header: "whether its district lines changed since the previous House election (1) or not (0)", column `lines_changed`.
   - UsHouseDistricts.cs:2263: `(int Year, string State, int Seats, bool LinesChanged, string Source)[] HouseMaps`.

3. in_force appears only where play is described:
   - line 324: "In play ... The redrawn states are `house_maps.csv`'s `in_force` reading - Missouri held on 3 Nov 2026".
   - line 318: the printed-only game range, "Missouri under both readings".

4. The spec's operative text says the same in so many words.
   - method_a step 4: "A state is redrawn for B->T if lines_changed = 1 at any election after B up to T, or if seats(T) != seats(B)."
   - (c)'s window: "the seats of states with lines_changed = 1 or a change of seats".
   - in_force appears only under IN PLAY and the game's range.
   So the plan transcribes the spec faithfully here: the measurement reads lines_changed and play reads in_force. That asymmetry is deliberate and stated in both places, not an ambiguity.

5. The spec's "(the first of play's kind: 173 or 181 districts)" is the one hook for an in_force reading of C5. It is a derived figure the plan does not carry. The fence's C5 paragraph (855-858) says only "step 1 over C5's redrawn states", which THE TERMS define.

6. The re-read half of the fix is about keeping the data current, not about the fence's meaning. The rule reads house_maps.csv as it stands when C5 is measured, and the same catalog gives the same outcome under any faithful implementation. On today's catalog that is 2026,MO,8,1, so Missouri is redrawn.

7. The stakes are smaller than claimed. Missouri's 2024 base is eight contested rows (no N or U flags), and its seats are 8 in 2024 and 8 in 2026. So M = n, and the fence's F-old (the M = n branch) returns the plain labelled count: (a)'s Missouri call is identical under either class. Only the bookkeeping would move (H against step 1's redrawn sum), and only under the reading line 300 rules out.*
- [design-fidelity] COMPLETED.md §791 is not staged, yet the plan relies on it for the preregistered digest - *The facts are right, but they describe the normal state of a change under review. Nothing in the staged plan is wrong, and nothing in it causes a commit without the record.

Confirmed:
- `git diff --cached --stat` stages only docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md.
- COMPLETED.md is clean in the index and the working tree, and its last heading is "## 790." at line 37567.
- The index blob points at §791 on lines 295, 298 ("`COMPLETED.md` §791 say what the panel saw"), 342 ("§791 says how near 2018 and 2022 came"), 697 ("holds the preregistered digest"), 701 and 864.
- No check resolves § pointers:
  - MetaTextCheck.cs:65 only bans "§" on player surfaces;
  - DocumentClaimCheck.cs:84 only exempts COMPLETED.md;
  - Tools/bar_tier.ps1 never reads the record;
  - .git/hooks holds samples only, and core.hooksPath is unset.

Why it is not a defect:
1. **The record follows the review on purpose.** CLAUDE.md:67 says "RECORDS: ONE COMMIT PER PASS". Each of 8f7b87fa (s790), 14ee39b6 (s789) and 40583b01 (s788) added its own COMPLETED.md section in the same commit as its plan pointers and its Reviews/ file. A section holds its review and bars paragraphs, so it cannot exist before them.
2. **Here the order is forced.** The digest covers the fence as finally committed, so it cannot be fixed while this review can still change the fence.
3. **§791 is already drafted.** The scratchpad file rec791.md (07:39) contains everything the finding's fix asks for:
   - Line 8: "**Its digest** - SHA-256 over the lines between the markers, each ending LF - is **`DIGEST791`**, preregistered here; the instrument will hold it as its list's first entry".
   - Line 5: the panel's provenance and its own disclosures ("summed the national two-party vote on the deciding-count basis (vote shares only) ... an illustrative rounding with an assumed, unfitted rho that decided nothing"). These match the BLINDNESS paragraph in final_spec.json's rationale.
   - Lines 13-16: the deferred figures, including "In 2018 and 2022 REP held exactly the 26 a contingent election needs", which is what plan line 342 points to.
   - The placeholders REVIEW791 and BARS791.
4. **"The only place the digest can live" is overstated.** The digest can be recomputed from the commit itself. Run over the index blob, the fence gives 160 lines and 10718 bytes, with no BOM and no CR.
5. **Earlier reviews refuted the same finding:**
   - Reviews/2026-10-06_s790_us11_house_districts.md:1724-1740;
   - the s789 review at :1197-1210;
   - the s788 review at :1135-1142;
   - the s787 review at :836-842.

What remains is older than this change: only the commit step guards § pointers.

Outside the finding: rec791.md line 8 calls the fence "plain ASCII", which is false. Fence line 2, "...Preregistered (§791).", holds "§" as UTF-8 C2 A7. These are the only non-ASCII bytes in the 160 lines.*
- [design-fidelity] The plan departs from P3's "wherever seats are unchanged" (correctly) without saying so - *The finding's facts are right, but the departure it claims does not exist. The plan copies the spec's own operative form, and the spec names North Carolina 2018 as the exception.

1. **The facts check out.**
   - house_maps.csv has `2018,NC,13,0` and `2020,NC,13,1`.
   - house_districts.csv has `2018,NC,9,...,-,N`, so NC-9 is undecided and m_B = 12.
   - NC-3 is one-party with votes, not flag U, so U_R = U_D = 0.
   - That gives c_B = 12 and c_T = 13. On the catalog's NC 2018 share, the roundings of c_T*u and c_B*u differ.
   - So a reading of P3 as apportioned seats plus exactly R_B would trip there.

2. **The spec states (b)'s identity in exactly the plan's form, twice, and names this case.**
   - method_b: "IDENTITY, asserted: at Delta = 0 with c_T = c_B, Rhat_s = R_B for every state."
   - method_b also says: "North Carolina's undecided base seat (2018) and C3's seat changes need no special case: c_T != c_B carries the extra or lost seats at the state's share."
   - measures: "the zero-swing identities of (a), of F-old with M = N, of (b) and (b') where c_T = c_B, and of (c), each asserted".
   - Plan line 302: "At zero swing with c_T = c_B every state returns its base delegation".
   - So the spec itself puts NC in C2 outside the identity. Read as a whole, P3's "wherever seats are unchanged" means counted seats, which is (b)'s own vocabulary. P6 has "the state's response or proportion is taken on its counted seats" and P10 has "the proportional change in the state's counted seats". Under that reading P3 is exactly c_T = c_B, so there is nothing for the plan to depart from or record.

3. **Dropping "P3's included" changes nothing.**
   - stop_and_ask has "a zero-swing identity that fails, P3's included".
   - Plan line 323 has "a zero-swing identity failing".
   - The plan carries no numbered premises: a grep of the staged plan for P1 to P20 finds none, so "P3's" would have no referent.
   - "a zero-swing identity" already covers P3's identities in the forms the plan asserts: (a)'s at line 301, (b)'s with c_T = c_B at line 302, and (c)'s "Shat = S(B) exactly" (method_c's CHECK).

4. **Nothing in the plan needs changing.**
   - Two faithful implementations of the plan cannot differ here, because the plan never uses P3's looser wording.
   - The proposed fix targets COMPLETED.md §791, which is not yet written, so "unrecorded" cannot be judged at this commit.

Outside this finding: the spec's measures lists (b')'s identity "where c_T = c_B" alongside (b)'s, and US-11's design block names only (b)'s. (b')'s holds by construction, since round(c_T p(u)) - round(c_B p(u)) = 0 when c_T = c_B, so this is at most a note.*
- [consistency] in_force is described as a column 'on which nothing decides', yet play reads it, and the fence's held rule leaves Missouri 2026 ambiguous in C5 - *The finding makes three claims. None shows a way the rule could go wrong.

1. "Nothing decides" is not contradicted by play. In this plan, "decide" means the rule's verdict:
- Staged line 309: "Only the figures the rule names decide; everything else informs Elias."
- The fence's decisive digest: "Nothing printed only is in it."
- The game range (line 318) is printed only and shows Missouri "under both readings", so it decides nothing.
- Play (line 324) uses the method once it is ruled; play reading `in_force` is not the rule deciding.
- The plan copies the panel exactly. Panel spec, method_a INPUTS: "The tool gains two generated columns, on which nothing decides: in_force (equal to lines_changed, except Missouri 2026 = 0 ...)". Panel method_a §5: "The redrawn states come from house_maps.csv's in_force reading: ... 3 Nov 2026: the nine states other than Missouri". So the panel has the same pair of statements, and there is no contradiction.

2. The held rule's column is not ambiguous.
- The fence (line 710) says "its lines changed at no election after B up to T ... (house_maps.csv)".
- The file's column is literally named `lines_changed`. Its header defines it as "whether its district lines changed since the previous House election (1) or not (0), as the Census Bureau's pages say".
- Premise 9 (line 973): "the states whose House lines changed for each election, 2016-2026, are house_maps.csv's, by the Census Bureau's pages".
- Line 300 rules out `in_force` for anything that decides.
- The panel's method_a §4 says the same in full: "redrawn for B->T if lines_changed = 1 at any election after B up to T, or if seats(T) != seats(B)".
- So any implementation that follows the plan reads `lines_changed`, and there is no second faithful reading.
- On top of that, `in_force` equals `lines_changed` on every 2016-2024 row by its own definition. C1-C4 under all six S4 readings, and every (c) window, therefore give the same result whichever column is read. Only C5 ever touches the Missouri 2026 row.

3. Counting Missouri "by F-old while play held it" moves no seat.
- house_maps.csv has `2026,MO,8,1`.
- house_districts.csv has eight contested Missouri 2024 rows: no N row, no U row. So M = 8 = n.
- With M = n, the fence's F-old count is "REP = #{x(j) > 0.5} plus #{x(j) = 0.5 whose base winner is REP}". That is exactly the held count: REP above 0.5, the base winner's party at 0.5.
- The design block asserts the same: "with M = n, F-old's quantile form returning the plain count".
- The only effect is which pile Missouri's W lands in during C5: step 1's sum instead of H. That is the behaviour the panel declared, not something the plan changed.
- C5 is BILLED until 2027, and the plan bills the read that would settle Missouri: "the referendum's result and the record's 2026 returns BILLED" and "C5's role - ... an amendment, once it is read".

The only thing left is wording. The fence turned the panel's "lines_changed = 1" into prose, so the frozen text relies on line 300 and premise 9 to say which column, and both sit outside the digest. Note level at most.*
- [consistency] The design dates redraws by the Census tab and counts them by F-old, contradicting R-US18 (a)'s asked text and the BILLED list, with no departure stated - *I could not find a failing path. The finding treats "the enacted map is read" as "the instrument (act or court order) or the plan's geography is read". Neither the plan nor the catalog uses the words that way, and the design states its rule for play explicitly.

1. **The plan already reads the 2026 maps from the Census tabs.** This was set at §790 (8f7b87fa, the base of this commit), not at §791:
   - The sources table (line 945): "The maps in force each cycle — the Census Bureau's Redistricting Data Program tabs (115th–120th Congresses) ... | saved (§790; ... `house_districts.md` reading 8)".
   - Risk 9 (line 973): "Read at US-11 (§790): the states whose House lines changed for each election, 2016-2026, are `house_maps.csv`'s, by the Census Bureau's pages".
   - Reading 8 is headed "The maps — which states' lines changed for each election, by the Census Bureau's pages". The thing it says was not read is different: "The 2016, 2018, 2022 and 2026 **instruments** were not read: the Census pages date each change by election, which is what the measurement uses."
   - So in the catalog's own words the maps are read and only the instruments are not. The finding's "no enacted map is read" mixes the two up.

2. **The 120th tab is the record of the enacted plans.** Its text says it "reflects the boundaries as submitted by the state assigned nonpartisan liaisons ... those in effect for the 2026 election cycle. Ten states (...) delineated new boundaries for the 2026 election cycle." F-old uses only the old shares and the seat count, so the map's geography would add nothing except its date. Risk 9 also says "no vote data in the game can carry a House result onto new lines".

3. **The design's own wording treats a `house_maps.csv` row as "a map read".** Line 324 says "The redrawn states are `house_maps.csv`'s `in_force` reading - Missouri held on 3 Nov 2026 by its saved note, all ten if a later read restores the plan - and past the rows read the last map read stays in force." This is what R-US18 (a) asks for: redraws enter as dated variants once read.

4. **The BILLED line does not support implementer B.** Line 957 is unchanged by this commit. Its §790 parenthetical already points at the reading the design uses: "(the Census Bureau's 120th tab names the states that redrew for 2026, Missouri's plan in doubt ... - `house_maps.csv`, §790)". The item stays open for what is still unread: Missouri's referendum, any later redraw, and a tab that is revised in place.

5. **The two implementations cannot diverge.**
   - The design names the redrawn states in play outright (line 324). "Missouri held on 3 Nov 2026" means the other nine count as redrawn.
   - The fence classifies only by `house_maps.csv` (lines 710-712): "A state is held for B->T if its lines changed at no election after B up to T and its seats did not change (`house_maps.csv`); otherwise it is redrawn. A district number is never matched across a redraw."
   - Counting the nine states on their old district lines would break both, and also risk 9's "a district swing has no prior where the map moved".
