6. **The plan transcribes the panel's spec faithfully.** The spec's `method_a` IN PLAY says: "The redrawn states come from `house_maps.csv`'s `in_force` reading: ... 3 Nov 2026: the nine states other than Missouri ..., Missouri held by its saved note". The plan has the same rule, with the figures moved out under the claim convention.

7. **What is not read is already stated and asked.**
   - Line 328: "... and 2026's were not read; which is why S5 asks rather than switches".
   - Line 327: "a prior on new lines - published results by new district for each enacted plan would retire the blind fallback (asked with R-US18, S5's third option)".
   - The adequacy line is "printed with the sourcing question".

   Nothing here departs from the item's text, so there is no counterpart to C1's stated departure (line 305).*
- [consistency] Reading (ii) calls {C2, C4} 'the item's own cycles', silently dropping C3 from US-11's list; printing (a) on C3 is also an unflagged departure - *Refuted. The finding's failure path does not occur.

1. **The label is the panel's own wording, not a departure from the spec.**
   - Panel spec, rule field: "(ii) the item's own cycles, D = {C2, C4}. Only the method and the fallback are compared..."
   - Plan line 801: "(ii) the item's own cycles, D = {C2, C4}: the step-2 verdict and the fallback are compared;"
   - The panel uses the same label in its premises ("The item's own cycles {C2, C4} must agree with D on the method and the fallback (S4 (ii))") and in its accepted change ("(ii) the item's own cycles {C2, C4}").

2. **The label is correct for what reading (ii) compares.**
   - Reading (ii) compares only step 1, (a)'s fallback, and step 2, (a) against (b). Both need (a).
   - The item, at line 294, excludes (a) from C3: "2020→2022 (the redraw: (b) and (c) only)".
   - So the item's own text can never put C3 into either comparison, and {C2, C4} is exactly the item's list as it bears on deciding. The panel says this itself in its cycles field: "the item's '(b) and (c) only' is honoured for deciding."

3. **C3 is not dropped silently.**
   - The fence's terms, line 705: "C3 is printed, never deciding."
   - Line 305 says the item "lists 2018→2020, 2020→2022 and 2022→2024", which includes C3, in the same sentence that then uses "the item's own cycles". It also gives C3's reason: play meets no census redistricting before 2030.
   - Line 340 repeats it: "a census redistricting in play after 2030 - C3's case, printed".

4. **Printing (a) on C3 is stated openly and decides nothing.**
   - Line 305: "printed in R-US14's stress-case form, (a) running through F-old in every multi-seat state".
   - That form is the plan's own pattern for a cycle that is reported and never decides. Line 657: "2012→2016 is reported as the stress case."
   - The decisive digest leaves it out: "Nothing printed only is in it."
   - No rule in CLAUDE.md requires every printed-only addition to be labelled with the word "departs".

5. **No two faithful implementations can differ.** D under (ii) is an explicit set, and C3's role is explicit.

6. **Elias's choice cannot be misled.** Neither option in blind ask 1 (line 865) puts C3 in D, so C3 is not on offer and the label cannot bias the choice.

The only thing left is wording: the plan does not carry the panel's gloss "honoured for deciding". That gloss would make the {C2, C4} label explain itself at line 305, but it is not needed to read the rule correctly.*
- [consistency] 'Certified in February 2021' is on no saved page, and the panel's brief held more than 'facts of record only' - *The finding's main claim is false. The certification date is on a saved page, and the US-11 tool reads that page.

1. **The date is on a saved page.** The page is `ElectionsData/usa/raw/returns/fec_federalelections2020.xlsx`.
   - It is tracked: committed in 947921ab (s786) and listed in `raw/returns/SHA256SUMS.txt` (line 23, 5073b6d2..., which matches the file).
   - `president_returns.md` line 125 lists the file as read "by us_house_prep.pl, the US House Results by State sheet (US-11 ...)".
   - On that sheet ('13. US House Results by State'), rows 2469 and 2473 are NY-22, Tenney's R and CRV lines. Their FOOTNOTES column (X) reads: "The election was certified on February 8, 2021, and Claudia Tenney took the oath of office on February 11, 2021."
   - The tool reads exactly this sheet and column. `Tools/us_house_prep.pl` line 646 lists `[2020, '13. US House Results by State']`. Line 659 has `$col{fn} = $k if $h eq 'FOOTNOTES';`, and every full-term district row's `$fn` is read (lines 668-671).
   - facts.md's "Tenney was certified 8 Feb 2021" is that footnote, word for word in substance. Plan line 304's "certified in February 2021" agrees with it.
   - The reviewer checked three places and missed this sheet. Those three do lack the date:
     - [HH-DIV] footnote 6: "did not submit an election certificate ... prior to the opening day of the 117th Congress".
     - Reading 5.
     - The Clerk's 2020 volume ("Published on FEBRUARY 26, 2021"): NY-22's figures with no note.

2. **The plan's head is not breached.** It says "Every date, rule and figure is sourced by Code before use ... a fact marked *from memory* is unverified". This date is sourced. The proposed fix, "drop the date or mark it from memory", would put a false statement into the plan.

3. **The second half does not hold.**
   - The brief's Pennsylvania 2018 "court-ordered" phrase is indeed on no saved page. The spec's method_a and undecidable[2] say so. The saved 116th tab says only "Three states (Colorado, Minnesota, and Pennsylvania) delineated new boundaries for the 2018 election cycle."
   - The plan does not repeat that phrase. Line 328 says "Pennsylvania 2018's and Georgia 2024's instruments are not" on disk.
   - Line 697's "its brief held facts of record only" uses "of record" as the plan always does: the real record as against the game's or an estimate. Compare "the delegations of record", "the 117th of record", "the winner of record", and the brief's own heading "The record (facts ...; not method estimates)".
   - The sentence's job is to claim blindness: no method estimates were in the brief. A true historical fact without a saved page does not break that.
   - The suggested wording "two statements on no saved page" miscounts. Only the Pennsylvania phrase lacks one, and line 697 already points to COMPLETED.md §791 for what the panel saw.

4. **Neither line touches the hashed rule.** Line 304 is in US-11's Design block and line 697 is the intro paragraph. Both sit outside `<!-- R-US18 RULE BEGIN -->`/`END`, so nothing the rule decides or its SHA-256 depends on them. The date also decides nothing: NY-22 goes to the Clerk's winner because "the game counts all 435 seats by plurality and models no certification".*
- [consistency] The fence drops the spec's 'a state with one seat at both elections is held' - *The omission is real as text, but it cannot change anything the rule decides.

The spec's method_a step 4 has two sentences. The first: "A state is redrawn for B->T if lines_changed = 1 at any election after B up to T, or if seats(T) != seats(B). Otherwise it is held." The second, the one the finding names: "A state with one seat at both elections is held." The fence at USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:710-712 keeps only the first: "A state is held for B->T if its lines changed at no election after B up to T and its seats did not change (house_maps.csv); otherwise it is redrawn."

1. The sentence adds nothing on the record.
   - In house_maps.csv, every state with one seat has lines_changed 0 in every year from 2016 to 2026: AK, DE, ND, SD, VT and WY throughout, and MT from 2016 to 2020.
   - MT 2022 reads 2,1. Its seats went from 1 to 2, so it is not a state with one seat at both elections.
   - Tools/us_house_prep.pl:249-252 fails unless 2022's exceptions are exactly the states with one seat, and marks only the other states as changed.
   - Every other year's list comes from saved pages checked against their SHA-256 (page(), :71-80).
   - The only later row the rule ever uses is 2026 (for C5), and it is already read: 0 for every state with one seat. So the finding's "any later row" case cannot occur inside the rule's scope.

2. The fence's wording is the spec's own premise. P8 says: "Held or redrawn is read from house_maps.csv, which is binary: lines changed, or seats changed", and its reason is "The catalog's own source decides." The finding's second fix, "state that the list alone decides", is therefore already what the fence says. The spec's (c) window also reads house_maps.csv directly ("states with lines_changed = 1 or a change of seats"), and the fence copies that; the one-seat sentence never applied there.

3. The rule's result would not change even if a state with one seat were classed redrawn. With one seat at both elections, every method gives the same seat:
   - The held call compares dhat with 0.5 and gives an exact 0.5 to the base winner's party.
   - F-old with M = n = 1 gives #{x(1) > 0.5}, plus x(1) = 0.5 when the base winner is REP. Here x(1) = d + Delta = dhat, so this is the held call.
   - F-prop is "as (b0) counts it", and (b0) is "as (b) at one seat".
   - (b) at n_T = 1 compares uhat with 0.5. For a single district, u = final_r/(final_r+final_d) = d. In the one-zero case u is 1 or 0, which is d. For a U seat, c_B = 0 gives u = R_B/m_B, which is again d.
   - (b') puts its fitted curve only where (b) uses P, and the n_T = 1 branch has no P, so it is (b) there.

   Moving such a state to redrawn therefore puts the same W into both sides of step 1. It removes the same W from H(a) and H(b) in steps 2 and 5, and every comparison there is a strict or non-strict inequality, so equal terms cancel. G, K and N stay the same, because (a) counts the state identically through F-old. Steps 1 to 7 and THE OUTCOME are unchanged. A "flip to F-old" moves no seat; only the per-state table's label and what goes into the decisive digest would differ, and the digest-checked pages rule that case out.*
- [consistency] Whether (c) and no change are in the decisive digest depends on whether step 4 short-circuits - *The finding's premise about the data is right. Its failing path is not a faithful implementation.

Premise checked against the catalog:
- house_by_state.csv and house_years.csv start at 2016, so C1 has an empty window.
- house_maps.csv shows 33 seats redrawn in 2018 (CO, MN, PA), so C2's window {2016->2018} is not empty.
- C4 is the level cycle, not a swing cycle.
- So D has exactly one forward swing cycle (C2), and step 4's first condition fails, as the finding says.

Why the "short-circuit" implementation is not faithful. The fence makes step 4's comparisons an output in every run, whatever condition 1 gives:
- Staged USA_STAGE_PLAN.md l.785-790: "STEP 4 - (c). The rule STOPS (S3) only if all of these hold: - D has at least two forward swing cycles; - in every one, (c)'s |N| is strictly below the step-2 method's ...; - in every one, (c)'s |N| is strictly below no change's. (c) is never recommended alone. Its comparisons print in the verdict line."
- l.818-819: the decided verdict line must carry "(c)'s comparison".

An implementation that "compares no (c) or no-change figure" cannot print that line, so it breaks the fence. Any faithful build compares (c)'s |N| against the step-2 method's and no change's in C2. That makes (c) and no change "rows a step compares", and l.841-843 then puts them in the digest: "every figure steps 1-7 compare ... H, G, N and K for every row a step compares in D".

"Nothing printed only is in it" does not exclude them:
- The fence names its printed-only parts explicitly: C3 is "printed, never deciding" (l.705), and under reading (ii) "S2, S3 and S6 are printed" (l.802).
- Under the declared readings, step 4 is never called printed. It is a live stop that happens not to fire on the record.

The rest of the plan agrees:
- The design block stamps the card with "the decisive table's" digest (l.322).
- Its decision table holds "(c)'s and (b')'s comparisons" (l.315).

This is also what the spec meant:
- final_spec.json stop_and_ask: "H, G, N and K for every deciding and guarding row in D. It excludes the printed-only parts (C3, the game range, (b0), the U-blind line, and C5 until it is measured)". Neither (c) nor no change is in that exclusion list.
- spec.cycles: "Step 4 ((c)) runs only on forward swing cycles. On the record on disk that is C2 alone ... so S3 cannot fire."
- spec.rule: "(c)'s C2 comparison is printed in the verdict line."
- So the spec also runs step 4's comparisons in C2 even though S3 cannot fire. The plan's wording reads the same way.

Even on the finding's own reading, the effect is small:
- Condition 1 depends only on the windows (the years on disk and house_maps.csv), never on a (c) or no-change figure. While it fails, no such figure can change the outcome. Once it can hold (record before 2016 sourced, choice 6), both readings compare these rows and include them.
- A moved (c) figure still fails by name through the pins (l.319: "for (c) REP seats and N. A moved pin ... fails by name").

The proposed fix ("N for (c) and no change in every swing cycle of D") would itself depart from the spec:
- It drops no change's H, G and K, which the spec includes as a guarding row.
- It drops (c)'s C4 figure.
- It names a (c) for C1, which does not exist.*
- [consistency] C5's truth gate drops the spec's 'and its footnoted vacancies' - *The words really are missing. The spec's truth field says: "C5's truth gate is generated the same way, against [HH-DIV]'s 120th row and its footnoted vacancies." The staged plan's line 305 (the design block's "The cycles" bullet) says only: "its truth gate then generated against `[HH-DIV]`'s 120th row." "The same way" was dropped as well. But the failure the finding describes ("does not say how a 120th vacancy is held") cannot happen, for four reasons:

1. How a seat is held is fixed inside the hashed fence for every cycle, C5 included. Line ~744: "The truth is the record's own district plurality (house_districts.csv's winner). Z_s is a state's undecided seats (NC-9 2018)." W_s, N and K score Z by interval. The design block's line 304 adds "on the Clerk's counts in every cycle". So a 120th vacancy where the Clerk prints a winner is that winner, and one with no votes printed is Z. The spec's phrase would not say how a vacancy is held either, so restoring it would not close the gap the finding describes.

2. In this catalog, a [HH-DIV] row already carries its footnoted vacancy:
   - `house_years.csv` has the columns `year,congress,seats_r,seats_d,seats_other,vacant`, and its header reads "vacant: the seat its footnote leaves unassigned".
   - `UsHouseDistricts.cs:2569` is `HouseRecord (..., string Vacant)`.
   - `house_districts.md` reading 9: "a footnote mark stands after the seats".
   - Plan line 292 already says "the districts give `[HH-DIV]` in every year but its two footnoted vacancies".
   So "against [HH-DIV]'s 120th row" already includes the vacancy.

3. The gate that already exists handles a vacancy in any row, not just 2018 and 2020. `GeneratedCatalogCheck.cs` 783-789:
   - `int r2 = winnersR - (vacantWinner == "R" ? 1 : 0) ...`
   - `bool vacancyRight = y.Vacant == "-" ? none == 0 : (vacantWinner != null && (none == 0 || (none == 1 && unwon == y.Vacant)))`
   This covers both the NY-22 shape (the Clerk names a winner) and the NC-9 shape (no winner) for any year.

4. The generator cannot let a 120th vacancy in undeclared. `us_house_prep.pl:201` fails with "the Nth Congress carries footnote M, not declared here" for any Congress outside its declared `%want`. Reading the 120th means widening the `115 .. 119` loop, which forces the footnote into `%vacant`, and from there into the `vacant` column.

So two faithful implementations end in the same place. The line is outside the fence (`<!-- R-US18 RULE BEGIN/END -->`, lines 699-860), so it carries no digest weight.

The one thing left over concerns the dropped "the same way", not the vacancy phrase. If choice 2 (2020's truth) is ruled toward `[HH-DIV]`'s 434, the amendment that writes it into the block has to say whether C5's vacancies follow. Restoring "and its footnoted vacancies" would not settle that.*
- [consistency] US-13 'Needs: US-11' is now ambiguous, since US-11's instrument waits on Elias - *The plan gives US-13's "Needs: US-11" only one reading, and the commit does not change it.

1. Line 295, "*Needs:* US-0; for its instrument, R-US18's six choices answered blind or the defaults confirmed (§791)", is a need of US-11's own instrument. US-11 is still finished only by its Done-when line, which the commit leaves unchanged (line 296): "each method's seat error per cycle in the card's GENERATED block; ... R-US18 asked". So US-11 is not done until its instrument has run, and the instrument waits on the six choices.

2. Line 356, "*Needs:* US-5, US-11.", is unchanged from 8f7b87fa (line 309 there). US-11 was already split before this commit: 8f7b87fa line 292 has "**Built, the data half: `COMPLETED.md` §790**". The commit did not create the question of which half US-13 needs.

3. The plan always says so when a need is only part of an item: line 351 "US-0 (the race half: US-3, US-4)", line 493 "US-0 (the read can run ahead; ...)", line 417 "US-10 (its rates already in Elias's hands)", line 505 "US-17 answered and stamped". A bare "US-11" therefore means the whole item up to its Done-when. Two faithful readers would both wait for it.

4. Nothing in US-13 supports a data-half-only reading, and its text shows it needs the count:
   - Lines 354-357: its record comes from "the saved `[HH-DIV]` divisions and NARA's results pages", not from US-11's district catalog.
   - Its instrument "prints the swing the model produces", and R-US20 (line 884) decides "if the model's midterm falls outside the record's range". That range is "the president's party's House seat change at every midterm since 1946".
   - Turning the model's 2026 swing into seats is US-11's instrument output. Line 318 lists it: "the game's range ... 5 Nov 2024 and 3 Nov 2026 ... REP seats at V from 40 to 60 % in one-point steps; the control point".
   - So US-13 waiting, through US-11, on Elias's six answers is the right consequence of the plan, not an ambiguity.

No two implementations of the plan would decide differently here, and no rule or condition changes. At most it is a note: a parenthetical on US-13's Needs line could make the wait through US-11 explicit.*
- [consistency] Pre-existing, outside this diff: the catalog header's cands rule contradicts the U rows - *The data point is correct. All 11 U rows carry cands 1 for the winner's party and 0 for the other (for example `2016,FL,24,...,0,1,0,0,D,U`), and the one N row, `2018,NC,9`, carries 0,0. The rest of the finding does not hold.

1. The catalog's own wording says "printed without votes" only for NC-9:
   - `Tools/us_house_prep.pl:486`: "# a mark on a district's number: only the race printed without votes, its footnote ordering a new election". The unprinted path that follows (lines 491-497) sets flag N and calls `next`, so `$x->{n}` is never set and cands is written 0,0 (lines 810-811).
   - The U path (lines 505-519) sets cands on purpose: `$out->{n} = { R => $c->{party} eq 'R' ? 1 : 0, D => ... }`, "its one name".
   - `house_districts.md:22` keeps the two cases apart: "a mark on a district's number only on the race printed without votes, its footnote ordering a new election; an unopposed seat alone, its footnote a no-opposition law".
   - Reading 5: NC-9 has "no votes printed"; an unopposed seat "goes to its one name, with no votes".
   - The header's flag N says "no votes printed"; flag U says "its name not printed on the ballot - no votes". The name is missing from the ballot, but the Clerk still prints it.
   - So the header's main clause, "how many of each the Clerk printed", gives 1 for a U race.

2. Reading 7 does not contradict the U rows. It says "Races where one major party stood alone are not flagged: `cands_r` and `cands_d` say so (... none in the race printed without votes)". "The race", definite and singular, is the record's name for NC-9 (0,0). The U rows' 1/0 values are exactly what that sentence says cands is for.

3. The failure scenario cannot happen. A contested test built on cands (cands_r>=1 && cands_d>=1) marks every U row uncontested and one-party for the winner's party, which is correct. U rows could only be mis-handled by keying U on cands = 0 instead of the flag, and the fence rules that out by naming the flag:
   - `USA_STAGE_PLAN.md` fence: "none if it has no winner and no votes (N); 1 or 0 by its winner's party if unopposed and printed without votes (U); where exactly one of final_r and final_d is zero ...".
   - The spec's method_a keys case (ii) on U and case (iii) on final, never on cands.
   - No code reads cands for meaning: `GeneratedCatalogCheck.cs:693-700` only re-reads the rows.
   - The commit does not touch the CSV.

4. One loose phrase remains in the header, which predates this commit and is not in this diff. The same paragraph says final is "0 where no votes were printed", which does cover the U rows. A reader of the header alone could therefore stretch the cands parenthetical to cover U rows as well. That is wording only and decides nothing in R-US18's rule.*
- [style-claims] The fence is not pure ASCII: the § on its first content line - *The facts in the finding are correct. The failure path and the claim that it cannot be undone are not.

CONFIRMED: docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:701 "Preregistered (§791)." holds the only non-ASCII bytes between the markers (699/860): one run, C2 A7. The digest over the LF-normalised bytes is f2120d1783ea05aa2f052750befd84dfdba24bc7ad034a649a03972cb104f8b5 for both the index blob and the CRLF working tree. Read as Latin-1 and re-encoded it is 73eacb85…, and as Encoding.ASCII it is 2ee7f611…. There is also a fourth value the finding missed: perl that decodes with :encoding(UTF-8) and does not re-encode gives 7fdfefb4… silently, because § is in the Latin-1 range and Digest::SHA does not croak.

REFUTED, the failure path. Each wrong value needs a hasher that misreads the file. None comes from a faithful reading:
- Plan line 322 says UsHouseCountCheck is built "on `UsStateSwingCheck`'s pattern". That pattern hashes UTF-8: Assets/Editor/UsStateSwingCheck.cs:320-322 `Digest(string text) => SHA256 … Encoding.UTF8.GetBytes(text)`, with docs read by `File.ReadAllText(path, Encoding.UTF8)` (lines 66, 92) and `Replace("\r\n", "\n")`.
- I ran that exact path in .NET: ReadAllText(UTF8), CRLF to LF, slice between the markers, UTF8.GetBytes, SHA256. It gives f2120d…, the same as perl's '<:raw' byte digest.
- The repo's other digest helpers all work on bytes and all give f2120d… here: ReviewLedgerCheck.Sha256NoCr (raw bytes, CR dropped), review_row.ps1 StateSha (a byte-preserving Latin-1 round trip), GeneratedCatalogCheck via ElectionsDataCatalogGenerator.Sha256Of(bytes), and us_house_prep.pl `open '<:raw'` plus sha256_hex.
- An Encoding.ASCII hasher, or a PowerShell 5.1 or Latin-1 misread, departs from the declared pattern. It is a bug, not a second faithful reading.

REFUTED, "only remedy is an amendment ruling":
- Plan line 323 lists "a rule digest not on the instrument's list" under "Data failures are defects, not asks - each fails the instrument until fixed".
- Line 697 ties an amendment ruling to a change in the block ("each naming Elias's ruling").
- If the hasher or the recorded digest is wrong while the block is unchanged, the fix goes in the tool or the record, checked against the §791 blob that git holds byte for byte. No ruling is needed.
- The instrument and its digest list are not built yet (Needs, line 295; line 864). Any mismatch would show at the first build, before anything is pinned.

NO RULE EFFECT: no step 1-7 reads the rule digest, so the § cannot change what the rule decides.

NO STYLE BREACH: 128 lines of the plan carry §, and lines 295, 697 and 864 use the same "(§791)" form. CLAUDE.md's only ASCII rule (line 89) covers keys and asset stems, not doc text.

WHAT IS LEFT (note): the definition at line 697, "(the lines between the markers, each ending LF)", does not name the byte encoding. That gap only matters for the one-time recording in §791 (the silent traps above).*
- [style-claims] The fence and design block were condensed by the session, which had seen the estimates; the plan credits them to the blind panel - *The finding's facts are right: lines 298 and 697 credit the rule and the design to the panel, and the session wrote the fenced text by condensing final_spec.json. But none of the four "departures" it cites is a choice the session made and credited to the panel. The plan also says outright that the session made the estimates. Line 697: "rough scratch estimates ... which the session had made first while mapping the item".

1. **S7, "read after 2024" (lines 809-811).** This is the spec's own scope, not the session's. The rule field reads "STOPS (S7) if a measured cycle has a winner outside REP and DEM (P1 fails)", and P1 sets the scope: "In 2016-2024 the truth gate holds every winner to REP or DEM. In a newly read cycle (C5), a winner outside them fails this premise and the rule STOPS (S7)". The truth field agrees: "A winner outside REP and DEM in 2016-2024 fails this gate" and "in C5 is not a data defect: it is P1 failing, and the rule stops (S7)". So does stop_and_ask's data-failure list: "a winner outside REP and DEM in 2016-2024 (caught by the gate)". The fence's parenthetical "(In 2016-2024 such a winner fails the truth gate, a data defect.)" merges these. The finding's own P1 quote supports the fence.

2. **Decisive digest (lines 841-843).** The spec reads "every figure steps 1-7 read ... W per redrawn state-cycle, and H, G, N and K for every deciding and guarding row in D. It excludes the printed-only parts (...)". The fence says "compare", "under F-old and F-prop", "every row a step compares in D" and "Nothing printed only is in it". The digest only decides when the bars fail on a moved table. The verdict comes from steps 1-7 whether or not the digest moves, so this wording cannot change what the rule decides and could not tune it to the estimates. At most there is a small difference in what the digest covers: no-change's or (c)'s C4 figures, which no step compares. That is a separate question about the digest, not about blindness.

3. **The six choices and the gate (lines 295 and 864).** All six choices come from the spec:
   - stop_and_ask sends five conventions "before the first run";
   - undecidable 10 adds the sixth: "Sourcing the Clerk's statistics from before 2016 ... That is Elias's decision, before the run".

   The gate is the session's addition, but nothing credits it to the panel:
   - line 295 is the Needs line, which comes before the Design attribution at line 298;
   - line 864 is in the asks, which line 697 excludes: "everything outside the markers - the ruling record, the asks, the ruling lines - is not part of it".

   The gate is also stricter than the spec's "A convention not ruled keeps its default", and it changes nothing the rule decides.

4. **Ask 1, "first".** This is P16's reason word for word: "the kind of election a 2024-start game meets first". It sits outside the fence. A separate wording point: the spec's cycles field says "next: 3 Nov 2026", which the design block uses. A 2024-start game meets 5 Nov 2024 first, so "first" is loose. That flaw is in the spec too, so it is not a departure.

**My own comparison.** I checked the fence against the spec's rule, method_a, method_b, method_c, measures, truth and stop_and_ask. Every term that bears on a decision matches:
- the terms and the half rule;
- (a)'s shares and F-old's quantile form;
- (b), (b0), (b U-blind) and (b'), including the Newton constants;
- (c)'s window and its empty-window case;
- the no-change carry;
- W, G, H, N and K;
- steps 1-7, including S5's "unless step 2 picks (b)", which equals the spec's "S5 bears on the verdict only when (a) is the step-2 method or step 2 stops";
- the tie-breaks, the stops' options, the ruling states and C5.

What was dropped is either a derived figure (moved out under the claim convention) or a clause with no effect on the record. Example: "A state with one seat at both elections is held". house_maps.csv has no single-seat row with lines_changed != 0.

**Verdict.** No choice that bears on a decision and was made by the non-blind session sits under the panel's credit, so there is no failing path. What is left is wording: "wrote it" would be more exact as "designed it; the fenced text is the session's condensation of its spec".*
- [style-claims] S7's 'a measured cycle read after 2024' literally covers every cycle - *The phrase can be parsed as the date of reading, but that parse changes no outcome. The claimed failing path, a registered S7 ask letting the bars pass, cannot happen.

1. The same step settles it inside the fence. G:/UNITY/Projects/PoliSim/docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:809-811 reads: "The rule STOPS (S7) if a measured cycle read after 2024 has a winner outside REP and DEM, printing that measurement. (In 2016-2024 such a winner fails the truth gate, a data defect.)"

2. Registering an ask clears only a stop. The fence's bar rules say "A stop fails the bars until its ask is registered." Line 323 makes the data failure separate: "Data failures are defects, not asks - each fails the instrument until fixed: the truth gate ...; a winner outside REP and DEM in 2016-2024 (the truth gate); ... GeneratedCatalogCheck's re-read failing." Nothing in the fence or the design lets an ask clear a data failure. So if S7 also fired on C1-C4, the instrument would still fail until the data was fixed. Once fixed, S7 no longer fires.

3. Existing code already fails such a winner before any instrument exists.
   - G:/UNITY/Projects/PoliSim/Assets/Editor/GeneratedCatalogCheck.cs:762-766: `lead` can only be "R", "D" or "-", so `lead != r.Winner` fails any contested O winner.
   - Lines 783-788 of the same file: `o2 != y.SeatsOther` is checked against house_years.csv, whose seats_other is 0 in every year 2016-2024.
   - This check runs in the bars: CheckSuite.cs:275, next to UsStateSwingCheck and UsNationalVoteCheck.
   - Tools/us_house_prep.pl:618 raises `problem("House $year: the districts give R $r D $d other $o, [HH-DIV] ...")`. Its line 770 then dies with "nothing written". Line 615 also requires NY-22 2020's winner to be R.

4. The catalog holds no read date, so the reading-date parse cannot be implemented from the data. The headers are:
   - house_years.csv: year,congress,seats_r,seats_d,seats_other,vacant
   - house_maps.csv: year,state,seats,lines_changed,source
   - house_districts.csv: year,...,winner,flags (winner is R, D, O or -)
   The only date an implementation can test is the election year. On disk, no year has an O winner.

5. The finding's "literal" reading is the panel's own text. final_spec.json's STEP 7 says "The rule STOPS (S7) if a measured cycle has a winner outside REP and DEM (P1 fails)", with no date limit. Its data failures separately list "a winner outside REP and DEM in 2016-2024 (caught by the gate)". The panel already let S7 overlap the gate, and the gate fails on its own. The plan's narrowing removes that overlap without changing any result.

Both parses give the same result in every case:
- On the record, S7 cannot fire for C1-C4.
- A 2016-2024 O winner fails the prep tool, GeneratedCatalogCheck and the truth gate until it is fixed, whether or not S7 also names it.
- C5's 2026 winners fall under S7 either way.
No input lets two faithful implementations disagree. What remains is wording only.*
- [style-claims] The fence header overstates, and the block never says DECLARED - *Checked against the staged plan (git show :docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md) and final_spec.json. The finding's facts are literally true, but none of them is a defect.

1. The header does not overstate anything new. Line 701 reads "R-US18'S RULE - US-11's measurement decides the House's seat method." That restates the R-US18 entry's own sentence at line 695, "The measurement decides; Code expects (a) where maps held." This diff leaves that sentence unchanged: it is a context line, and `git log -S` puts it at s777 cba1bd04. The panel used the same phrase in STEP 2: "'The measurement decides', but a switch away...". The plan's grain at line 138 says what the phrase means: "Nothing measured goes live unruled."
   - The block's own body then pins down what "decides" means, so an implementer cannot read it two ways:
     - line 815-816: "THE OUTCOME is "code recommends (a)" ... otherwise the rule does not decide";
     - line 822: "R-US18 is asked with the full table whatever the outcome";
     - line 850: "A ruling binds a decided verdict as it binds a stop";
     - line 853: "US-16's count asserts R-US18 RULED".
   - No reading of the title lets a decided verdict go live without a ruling.

2. "Preregistered" is new to the repo's own documents. A grep that leaves out raw/ finds it only in this file, at lines 298, 697 and 701. But it is the panel's own term: the spec's rule opens "R-US18'S RULE, PREREGISTERED.", and P17 is "Preregistration with an amendment path". The intro paragraph at line 697 defines it ("the preregistered one, then one per amendment"). CLAUDE.md has no vocabulary rule, so this breaks no style rule.

3. The DECLARED label is there, one level up. US-11's design heading at line 298 reads "*Design, DECLARED before the build (§791).*" and goes on: "The methods' arithmetic, the scoring and the decision are R-US18's preregistered block; this list holds everything else." So the share rule for U and one-party seats at lines 716-719 is DECLARED by reference. That matches the §788 precedent at line 213: "*Design, DECLARED before the build (§788):*" heads sub-items that carry no label of their own.
   - R-US14's and R-US15's rules (lines 657 and 664) carry no per-rule DECLARED tag either.
   - The R-US18 intro at line 697 also says "The rule, declared before any measurement (§791)."
   - The alternative is flagged at line 333: "unopposed seats against an imputed share - the record cannot choose".
   - The grain's "Premises named: DECLARED or ruled" (line 137) is therefore met.

4. "After this commit the wording can change only by amendment" is true (line 697). That explains why the reviewer flags it now, but it does not make a wording preference a defect.

None of the three points changes what the rule decides. None is an ambiguity an implementer could resolve two ways, and none breaks a written style rule.*
- [implementability] S3 is silent on a (c) with rho <= 0, which the design marks unplayable - *(1) The fence is not silent; it decides. STEP 4 (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md 785-789): "The rule STOPS (S3) only if all of these hold: - D has at least two forward swing cycles; - in every one, (c)'s |N| is strictly below the step-2 method's ...; - in every one, (c)'s |N| is strictly below no change's." There is no rho term. (c) is defined for any rho (745-752: "logit Shat(T) = logit S(B) + rho * [...]", "rho = sum(x * y) / sum(x^2)"), and the only rho failure named is "sum x^2 = 0 in a (c) window" (a data defect). So a faithful build of the fence has exactly one outcome for any rho.

The design block cedes the decision to the fence and keeps the mark as print-only. Line 303 puts "a rho at or below zero marked unplayable" inside "*(c), printed beside its verdict-line comparison:*". The block's intro says "the scoring and the decision are R-US18's preregistered block; this list holds everything else". The measures list ends "Only the figures the rule names decide; everything else informs Elias."

This is faithful to the panel. final_spec.json stop_and_ask has "PRINTED, NEVER A STOP: ... (c)'s other fits, and a rho_hat <= 0, marked unplayable". method_c has "PRINTED, NEVER DECIDING: ... A rho_hat <= 0 is printed and marked unplayable." The spec's STEP 4 has the same three conditions. The proposed fix ("S3 needs rho > 0") would therefore ADD a condition the panel's spec does not hold.

(2) The claim that a reconciled (c) would not be buildable is false. Fence 851-852: "A ruling must name an option that can be built: (a) with F-old or with F-prop; (b); (b'); or (c) reconciled to (a) or (b)." There is no rho condition. Also, the rho that would be played is the {C1, C2, C4} fit (line 303: "In play, if ever ruled, its window is C1, C2 and C4"), not a per-cycle forward fit.

(3) The scenario cannot occur. Computed from house_by_state.csv, house_districts.csv and house_maps.csv, every transition on disk has x*y > 0:
- 2016->2018: x<0, y<0
- 2018->2020: x>0, y>0
- 2020->2022: x>0, y>0
- 2022->2024: x<0, y<0

This holds on the own-line votes, on the deciding count (reading vi), and with 2020 at 212/434 (reading i). Readings (iii)-(v) only lower C1's redrawn-seat count (33 of 435), so no window changes. The windows are C2 = {C1}, C4 = {C1, C2} and C5 = {C1, C2, C4}; C3 is out with 429 seats redrawn. So every forward rho the rule can fit is strictly positive. At the C5 re-run, 2026 enters no forward window (a transition counts only if its later election precedes T), so C5 cannot produce a rho at or below zero. Choice 6, if ruled, is "written into the block by amendment" (the asked-blind paragraph after the fence), so the fence would change there anyway. In short, rho at or below zero can only appear in the printed fits (held-states-only, leave-one-out), which is what the mark is for.*
- [implementability] Step 5 restates step 2's conditions without 'of D' - *The working tree matches the index (no unstaged diff). Staged docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md, lines 792-794, step 5: "If (b') is fitted in every cycle of D, the rule STOPS (S6) if (b') beats the step-2 method by step 2's own switch conditions: H strictly lower in C1 and in C2, H not higher in C4, the sum of G strictly lower, and K not lower in any cycle."

1. Not a departure from the spec. The panel's final_spec.json rule field words step 5 the same way: "sum G strictly lower; K not lower in any cycle." The plan keeps that meaning.

2. Step 5's own sentence fixes the scope in two places. It opens with "fitted in every cycle of D", and D's cycles are the only ones where (b') is guaranteed to exist. The colon after "by step 2's own switch conditions:" makes the list a restatement of step 2's conditions, not new ones. Step 2, lines 777-778, scopes both explicitly: "the sum over D of G(b) < the sum over D of G(a)" and "K(b) >= K(a) in each cycle of D".

3. The fence's own terms rule C3 out, lines 705-706: "D = {C1, C2, C4} ... C3 is printed, never deciding." A C3 K or G term inside S6's trigger would let C3 decide whether S6 fires, and so decide the outcome. A faithful implementation cannot do that.

4. The fence's digest clause, lines 841-843, makes the C3 reading inconsistent: "The decisive digest is SHA-256 over every figure steps 1-7 compare ... H, G, N and K for every row a step compares in D. Nothing printed only is in it." If step 5 compared C3's K, that figure would have to be in the digest (it is compared) and out of it (C3 is printed only). The fence is consistent only if step 5 compares D's rows.

5. The surrounding plan says the same. US-11's design block, line 305: "C1 to C4 are measured; D = {C1, C2, C4} ... C3 ... is printed in R-US14's stress-case form". Line 315: "the decision table over D". The spec's cycles field: "in steps 2, 3 and 5, C4 can only veto a switch", and "PRINTED ONLY: C3 for every method".

6. The edge cases do not open a gap either. Under reading (ii), D = {C2, C4}, S6 is printed only (lines 801-802), so the phrase cannot move an outcome there. Under C5 (lines 855-858), "the rule is run again over D plus C5", which changes the cycle set for step 2 and step 5 alike. Leaving out "of D" makes no difference there.

The reviewer's scenario needs an implementer to read C3 into a stop that the fence says C3 never decides. No faithful reading gets there, so both implementations reach the same outcome.*
- [implementability] The spec's 'c_T asserted >= 0' was dropped - *The wording claim is accurate. The spec's method_b, line 10, reads: "c_T = n_T - U_R - U_D, asserted >= 0." The fence at plan line 731 reads: "c_B = m_B - U_R - U_D; c_T = n_T - U_R - U_D, n_T the state's seats at T". Neither line 302 ("(b), asserted") nor line 323 (the data failures) restates the assertion. Line 323 does copy the spec's own DATA FAILURES list (stop_and_ask) faithfully, and that list never contained c_T either.

The failing path, a negative c_T passing silently into P, cannot happen:
1. The bound is structural. U seats are decided seats, so U_R + U_D <= m_B <= n_B and c_T >= n_T - n_B. A negative c_T needs a state that loses seats between B and T and had more unopposed base seats than its seats at T.
2. The catalog rules it out on the record. I computed this from house_districts.csv and house_maps.csv for C1 to C5, with C5 using the 2026 seats. U rows by year: 2016=2, 2018=4 (all Florida), 2020=1, 2022=2, 2024=2, every one with an R or D winner. The only seat changes come in C3. The seven states that lost a seat there (CA, IL, MI, NY, OH, PA, WV) had no U rows in 2020. The smallest c_T in any state-cycle is 1, and that is in a one-seat state, where P is not used ("n_T = 1: REP if uhat > 0.5..."). Every state with two or more seats has c_T >= 2. C3 is "printed, never deciding" in any case.
3. It cannot happen in play. Line 324 says the count runs "at the apportionment in force (2 U.S.C. 2a; R-US13 (a) carries 2020's forward)", so n_T = n_B. Game counts leave no seat undecided, so c_T = c_B >= 0. A post-2030 apportionment is flagged at lines 325-350 as "C3's case, printed" and would need an amendment.
4. Even if it did occur, the plan's own text would catch it. With c_T < 0, U_R exceeds n_T - U_D, so line 735's min(n_T - U_D, max(U_R, ...)) returns n_T - U_D, which is below U_R. That breaks the clause at line 302 under "(b), asserted": "U seats stay their party's". If U_D > n_T, the output is negative, which line 323's "a method's output outside its bounds" catches.

The fence's digest is also not at stake. Line 697 says US-11's Design (outside the markers) "holds the methods' inputs, assertions, outputs", so an assertion can be added later without an amendment. The omission does not change any outcome, and no two faithful implementations can reach different results because of it.*

## What was done about the first pass

Ninety-four findings survived the skeptics, most of them the same few seen through five lenses; every one was acted on, and the block, the design and the asks were rewritten before the digest was taken. By theme:

- **C4 "can only veto" (2, 78, and in the design 69, 87).** The specification's arithmetic counts C4 in step 2's and step 5's sums, and its account says C4 never carries a switch; the skeptics showed a seat C4's near-zero swing moves can tip a sum. The session, which had seen the estimates, did not choose between the two: the arithmetic stays the default, S4 gains reading (vii) (the sums over the swing cycles alone), and the choice is asked blind (choice 7). The design's sentence restores "or a rounding".
- **No change at North Carolina in C2 (3, 18, 34, 77).** Carried onto n_T in every state by P, so every row sums to the apportionment and W stays an integer - the specification's own stated invariant.
- **The cycles named instead of their roles (5, 38, 66, 79).** Steps 2, 3 and 5 read "every swing cycle of D" and "the level cycle"; each cycle keeps its role in every reading and run.
- **The outcome undefined (8, 83) and the ruling record (17, 65, 86).** The outcome of a run defined once; RULED stores it; C5's re-ask fails the bars until registered, as the specification has it; C5's figures enter the decisive digest once measured; the mirror line names no digest and is checked.
- **The tie-break (1).** Names the held-state sum; equal whole-House sums fail (b)'s condition.
- **After S1 (6, 81) and S4's "fallback" (7, 80).** (b') listed under S1; S5 bears on the verdict only where the specification says.
- **"Run on the defaults" (9, 20, 39, 63, 82) and choices 5 and 6 (21, 40).** Defined: word to run on the defaults rules none; each choice's effect named; choice 6 rewrites the block's first year.
- **Choice 1's false reason (4, 22, 35, 55) and choice 2's figure (56, 88).** C1 is the kind of election a 2024-start game meets at its first midterm, not first; the Clerk's own 2020 table prints 212 and a vacancy, so "the Clerk's 213" was wrong as well as typed.
- **Provenance (16, 19, 23, 47, 57, 58).** "Declared before any measurement" became "before the instrument's first run"; "facts of record only" became what the brief held, its statements on no saved page listed in §791 (finding 16's certification date is on a saved page - the refuted finding said so - and stays).
- **Inputs (36), the digest's bytes (12, 24, 51, 91), V_dc (30, 76), V(E)'s source and US-5's pending basis (42), US-16's guard (37), in_force (67, 73), the window rule and the record's first year (21).** Each fixed in the block or the design.
- **Typed counts (29, 46, 59, 71, 88), derived claims (60, 61, 62, 69, 70), the redraw kinds (43, 62).** Removed or pointed at the record.
- **Assertions and printing dropped (10, 15, 27, 28, 31, 32, 44, 45, 49, 53, 84, 85, 93).** Restored: c_T ≥ 0, the reconciliation's bounds, the identities as exact as they can be, the delegations of record pinned, NC-9's call printed and not scored, every tie-break printed, step 1 over every redrawn state-cycle, C5's truth gate with its footnoted vacancies.
- **The panel's output only in scratch (54).** Committed verbatim as `Reviews/2026-10-06_s791_us11_blind_panel.md`.
- **The rest (11, 13, 14, 25, 26, 33, 41, 48, 50, 52, 64, 72, 74, 75, 89, 90, 92, 94).** Wording, scope and definitions: n_B, S7's scope, the held district without a share moved into the block, (c)'s movement, F-prop not anchored, the comparisons "as the operator is written", the "(asked blind)" marks.

## The second pass - confirmed (verbatim)

A second pass over the staged tree after the first pass's fixes, 50 agents: three lenses (were the first pass's findings fixed and did the fixes break anything; a fresh adversarial read of the rule as an implementer; provenance, claims and the record), every finding put to a refute-first skeptic.

### 1. RULED keeps a single 'outcome it was made on', but once C5 is measured the bars compare every run against it (fixes to 8/17/83)

- **Lens:** fix-check - **reviewer:** minor - **skeptic:** minor
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:860

**The scenario.** Lines 859-862 give RULED one stored outcome. Lines 874-877 then compare 'the generated outcome' with it, 'C5's runs included'. Line 885 keeps the run over D and adds a second run 'over D plus C5'. Example: R-US18 is RULED on (a) over D. In 2027 C5 is measured. The D run still gives (a); the D-plus-C5 run gives S1. Line 876 fails the bars and R-US18 is asked again. Elias rules (a) with F-old again and keeps D. Store S1 and the D run's (a) differs; store (a) and the C5 run's S1 differs. Under the literal text RULED can never pass, and the ask repeats until D is amended. Only a record that holds each run's outcome ends it, and the block does not provide one. The pass-1 text had the same gap implicitly; the fix made the single field explicit.

**The fix proposed.** Before the digest is fixed, line 860: 'RULED, with the ruling, its option, the outcome of each run it was made on (the run over D and, once C5 is measured, the run over D plus C5 with step 1's fallback over C5's redrawn states) and the decisive digest it was made on'. Line 874: 'while each run's generated outcome equals the one the ruling was made on'. Alternatively, say that a ruling made after C5 is measured must amend D.

**The skeptic's evidence.** The finding holds against the staged block (git show :docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md).

1. One stored outcome. Lines 859-861 say RULED holds "the ruling, its option, the outcome it was made on and the decisive digest it was made on". That is one outcome.

2. "Outcome" is defined per run. Line 831 reads "THE OUTCOME of a run is the step-2 verdict ... with the set of the other stops that fire". No combined outcome over several runs is defined anywhere.

3. Two runs, both checked against that one outcome.
   - Lines 885-887: once C5 is measured, "the rule is run again over D plus C5 ... Either giving an outcome other than the one ruled on ... asks R-US18 again". "Either" means the D run and the D+C5 run are each compared with the single stored outcome.
   - Lines 874-877 say the same: "the bars pass while the generated outcome equals the outcome the ruling was made on ... A changed outcome - C5's runs included, once measured - fails until R-US18 is asked again; it is never re-pinned".

4. D cannot absorb C5 without an amendment. Line 888: "D itself changes only by amendment." The digest (lines 863-868) covers "the same over D plus C5", so it spans both runs, but the outcome field does not.

Failing path:
- R-US18 is ruled on the D run's outcome (a).
- C5 is measured and the D+C5 run gives S1. It differs, so R-US18 is asked again. That part is intended.
- Elias picks an S1 option, "(a) as measured" (line 845), and does not amend D.
- If the record stores (a), the D+C5 run's S1 differs. If it stores S1, the D run's (a) differs.
- Either way the bars fail, R-US18 is asked again, and the ask repeats until D is amended.

The same thing happens if Elias's first ruling comes after C5 is already measured. Under the literal text, RULED can never be reached once the two runs give different outcomes.

Why minor and not a defect: an implementer could reasonably store one outcome per run and nothing breaks. Equally literally, they could store a single outcome and the loop above happens. That is the "resolvable two ways with different outcomes" case. The rule does not decide wrongly while D is the only run, and the problem only appears after C5 is measured, which the text already anticipates. It still has to be fixed before the hash is fixed at this commit, because afterwards only Elias's ruling can change it.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Line 860: "RULED, with the ruling, its option, the outcome of each run it was made on (the run over D and, once C5 is measured, the run over D plus C5, with step 1's fallback over C5's redrawn states where the ruled option counts them) and the decisive digest it was made on".

Line 874: "the bars pass while each run's generated outcome equals that run's outcome in the ruling".

Lines 886-887: "either run giving an outcome other than its own ruled one".

Alternative: state that a ruling made while the D run and the D+C5 run differ must amend D, for example to D plus C5. Then only one run decides again, and the single stored outcome is enough.

### 2. Finding 89 only partly fixed: STEP 7 prints under S7's two scorings, but W_s and N have no form for a seat won outside REP and DEM

- **Lens:** fix-check - **reviewer:** minor - **skeptic:** minor
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:771

**The scenario.** Lines 771-772 say W_s 'is the distance of REPhat_s from [R_s, R_s + Z_s]: W_s = (...)/2'. The two forms agree only when R_s + D_s + Z_s = n_T. Take C5 and a 3-seat state of record 1 REP, 1 DEM, 1 independent (Z_s = 0), with a row giving REP 1, DEM 2. The distance form gives 0, the formula gives 0.5, and S7's first scoring ('misplaced for every method alike', line 855) needs 1. Line 825 now requires figures under that scoring, which has no formula. Line 867 puts C5's H, G and N in the decisive digest without naming the scoring. The second scoring (the seat added to Z_s) is consistent. N (line 774) has the same gap nationally.

**The fix proposed.** Before the digest is fixed: 'W_s is the distance ...; where R_s + D_s + Z_s = n_T it equals (...)/2'. In STEP 7, add: 'under the first scoring each such seat adds one to W_s and to |N| for every row; the decisive digest holds the second scoring's figures' (or both, each named).

**The skeptic's evidence.** Staged docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md, inside the R-US18 fence:
- 768-772: "The truth is the record's own district plurality ... Z_s is a state's undecided seats (NC-9 2018). R_s and D_s are the record's decided seats by party ... W_s ... is the distance of REPhat_s from [R_s, R_s + Z_s]: W_s = (|REPhat_s - R_s| + |DEMhat_s - D_s| - Z_s) / 2."
- 718-719: "DEMhat_s = n_T - REPhat_s".
- 824-826: STEP 7 "printing that measurement under each of S7's first two scorings. (In 2016-2024 such a winner fails the truth gate, a data defect.)"
- 855: S7 option 1 "the seat scored as misplaced for every method alike; scored as undecided by interval".
- 863-868: the decisive digest holds "H, G, N and K ... once C5 is measured, the same over D plus C5", with no scoring named.

Trace: the two forms of W_s agree only when R_s + D_s + Z_s = n_T. Take REPhat = R + e. Then DEMhat - D = (n_T - R - D) - e. That equals Z - e only when there are no seats outside the three classes. Line 323's truth gate guarantees this for 2016-2024, so the D cycles are safe. In C5, a seat won by a third party or an independent is decided, but it is in none of R_s, D_s or Z_s.

The reviewer's example: a 3-seat state of record REP 1, DEM 1, other 1, with Z_s = 0 and a row giving REP 1, DEM 2. The distance form gives 0 and the formula gives (0 + 1 - 0)/2 = 0.5. Scoring 1 ("misplaced for every method alike") needs 1, and the block gives no form for it. Scoring 2 (the seat added to Z_s) makes R + D + Z = n_T, so both forms give 0 and agree.

Pass 1's finding 89 asked for three things:
- STEP 7 to print under both scorings. Done.
- W_s defined as the distance, with the halved formula said to hold only when R + D + Z = n. Not done: the block states both forms as one equality.
- K's class for a delegation with such a member. Not done: line 776 resolves only "undecided seats".

This cannot change the D-only run, so it is not a defect. Once C5 has such a winner, S7 fires and the run is an ask anyway. Still, two implementers could read the block differently: the C5 step-2 verdict feeds the outcome that RULED compares (lines 874-877), and the C5 figures enter the decisive digest. Two implementations could therefore give different figures, digests and possibly step-2 verdicts. That fits "minor": an ambiguity an implementer could resolve two ways with different outcomes. N (line 774) is well defined as written but does not count the seat under scoring 1, so the reviewer's "same gap" is weaker there. N still needs the scoring named.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** This edit is inside the fence, so it must land before the digest is fixed.

At 771-772, define W_s by one formula that covers both scorings: "O_s is the state's seats won outside REP and DEM (none in 2016-2024, by the truth gate). W_s, the state's seats misplaced, = (|REPhat_s - R_s| + |DEMhat_s - D_s| + O_s - Z_s) / 2: the distance of REPhat_s from [R_s, R_s + Z_s] when O_s = 0."

The formula always gives an integer, because |dR| + |dD| has the parity of O + Z. It reproduces S7's first scoring: in the example, (0 + 1 + 1 - 0)/2 = 1. Under the second scoring, O_s moves into Z_s and the formula reduces to the present one.

At 774, say N is computed with O added to Z under the second scoring, and is unchanged under the first.

At 775-776, give K's class for a delegation with such a member. For example: under the first scoring it never matches unless the row's class equals the record's class counted over REP and DEM alone; under the second, it is resolved as an undecided seat.

In STEP 7 or the digest bullet (863-868), name which scoring's C5 figures the decisive digest holds: the second, or both with each named.

### 3. 'two of its phrases' is a transcribed count in the plan, which finding 23 said not to put there (findings 16/23/57)

- **Lens:** fix-check - **reviewer:** minor - **skeptic:** minor
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:700

**The scenario.** The provenance fix reads '`COMPLETED.md` §791 says what the brief held (two of its phrases on no saved page among it)'. That is a derived count about the brief, which the claim convention sends to §791, and finding 23's fix said 'Put no count in the plan'. The same sentence still says the brief held 'facts of the record'. The parenthetical names only the off-page phrases, not the brief's 2018 'no REP 39', which rec791 records as 38 in the catalog.

**The fix proposed.** Outside the fence, so no digest change: '(`COMPLETED.md` §791 says what the brief held - its statements no saved page bears out, and a count taken from scratch, among them - what the panel saw and did, each correction, ...)'.

**The skeptic's evidence.** Staged docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:700 (outside the R-US18 markers, so the digest is unaffected): "`COMPLETED.md` §791 says what the brief held (two of its phrases on no saved page among it), what the panel saw and did, ...". CLAUDE.md:39-41 counts "a count" and "there are N of X" as DERIVED claims, says they may only be GENERATED, REFERENCED or DELETED, and says "Nobody transcribes, anywhere, in any file". Only COMPLETED.md is exempt. "Two of its phrases on no saved page" is a count about the brief, and it is also state-dependent: it becomes false once a page for PA 2018's "court-ordered" or GA-6's "mostly the 2022 13th" is saved. pass1_findings.md item 23's fix said in so many words: "Put no count in the plan." The session took item 57's wording ("Two of its phrases are on no saved page") over item 23's and item 16's ("point to the record"). DocumentClaimCheck's two clauses are narrow (Assets/Editor/DocumentClaimCheck.cs:33-36) and do not catch spelled-out counts, so the bar will not flag this. The count is accurate today: rec791 names exactly PA 2018 and GA-6 as on no page, NY-22 as on one, and the 2018 figure of 39 as taken from scratch against the catalog's 38. So this is a style-rule breach, not a false claim. Its secondary points do not hold. The sentence says "facts of the record - no estimate", not "only", so it is not overstated. "Among it" is non-exhaustive, so leaving out the 39/38 item is not false, and rec791 records that item.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Outside the fence (no digest change), at line 700 replace "(two of its phrases on no saved page among it)" with "(the statements in it that no saved page bears out, and a count taken from scratch, among it)". Or simply "(its statements that no saved page bears out among it)". Keep the list itself only in COMPLETED.md §791.

### 4. The panel file's session-written header is mojibake ('Â§791') - a new defect from the fix to finding 54

- **Lens:** fix-check - **reviewer:** minor - **skeptic:** minor
- **Where:** Reviews/2026-10-06_s791_us11_blind_panel.md:1

**The scenario.** Lines 1 and 5 hold the bytes C3 82 C2 A7, which render as 'Â§791' in the title and twice in the provenance paragraph. Cause: scratchpad/us11design/render_panel.pl has a literal '§' without `use utf8;`, so its two source bytes are encoded again through :encoding(UTF-8). The verbatim body (lines 516, 563, 584, 652, 702, 880) and the s790 review's header are correct. MojibakeCheck skips Reviews/ (MojibakeCheck.cs:42), so no bar catches it. The staged and working copies are identical.

**The fix proposed.** Add `use utf8;` to render_panel.pl (or write "\x{A7}"), re-render, re-stage, and confirm that `grep -c $'\xc3\x82'` on the file returns 0.

**The skeptic's evidence.** I couldn't refute this; it's real. The staged blob (`git show :Reviews/2026-10-06_s791_us11_blind_panel.md`) has the mojibake on two lines. Line 1 reads "# The blind design panel - Â§791, PS-6 US-11 ...". Line 5 reads "(`COMPLETED.md` Â§791) ... wrong; Â§791 and `Reviews/...rule_review.md`". `od -c` shows the bytes as 303 202 302 247 (C3 82 C2 A7), which is a § encoded twice. `grep -c $'\xc3\x82'` on the working copy returns 2, meaning two lines, three occurrences. Cause: render_panel.pl line 7 has `$o .= "# The blind design panel - §791, ..."` and line 9 has the provenance paragraph, both with a literal § but no `use utf8` anywhere in the script (grep for 'utf8' only finds the § lines). The two source bytes are taken as Latin-1 characters and encoded again on output. No bar catches it: MojibakeCheck.cs line 42 excludes `Reviews/`, "whose verbatim reports quote mojibake to name it". This is a breach of the repo's mojibake hygiene in the header that the session wrote itself. It does not touch the R-US18 rule block, its SHA, or the plan, so it changes no decision and is not a defect.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Add `use utf8;` to scratchpad/us11design/render_panel.pl, or write the § as "\x{A7}", and keep the output layer :encoding(UTF-8). Re-render the file and re-stage it. Then confirm that `git show :Reviews/2026-10-06_s791_us11_blind_panel.md | grep -c $'\xc3\x82'` returns 0, and that the verbatim body's § characters (for example lines 516 and 563) still come out as the two bytes C2 A7.

### 5. Decisive digest: 'where D has two forward swing cycles' conflicts with step 4's 'at least two' and with the same sentence's 'whatever an earlier condition of its step gives'

- **Lens:** fix-check - **reviewer:** note - **skeptic:** note
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:866

**The scenario.** Step 4 (line 799) needs 'at least two' forward swing cycles. Suppose choice 6 is answered 'sourced' and C5 is measured. The run over D plus C5 then has three (C1, C2, C5), and a literal 'two' leaves (c)'s and no change's N out of the digest. The sentence also opens with 'each computed whatever an earlier condition of its step gives'. Step 4's first condition is such a condition, so a reader could include (c)'s C2 N in the default digest, while the 'where' clause excludes it. The readings differ on whether a catalog correction that moves only (c)'s C2 N forces a re-registered ask under ASKED.

**The fix proposed.** Before the digest is fixed: 'where D has at least two forward swing cycles (this condition governs, unlike step 4's others), N of (c) and of no change in each'.

**The skeptic's evidence.** Staged docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md, inside the hashed block. Line 792 says "D = {C1, C2, C4}". Lines 790-792 make C1, C2 and C5 swing cycles and C4 the level cycle. Lines 843-845 say "A cycle with an empty window has no (c). A forward swing cycle is a swing cycle of D whose window is not empty."

Step 4 (line 798) reads "D has at least two forward swing cycles". The digest (lines 863-868) reads "each computed whatever an earlier condition of its step gives, ... where D has two forward swing cycles, N of (c) and of no change in each; once C5 is measured, the same over D plus C5."

Over D alone, "two" and "at least two" cannot differ. D has only two swing cycles, C1 and C2. Under the default for choice 6, C1's window is empty: the record's first transition is 2016->2018, and no transition's later election precedes 2018. So by default only C2 is forward, and the clause does not fire either way.

The two readings part only on the run over D plus C5, and only when choice 6 is answered "sourced". Then C1, C2 and C5 all have non-empty windows, so there are three forward swing cycles. "The same over D plus C5" leaves open two things:
- whether the condition becomes "D plus C5 has two";
- whether "two" means exactly two, which the contrast with step 4's explicit "at least two" invites.

That scenario needs choice 6's alternative, and line 893 says that choice "rewrites THE TERMS' first year" by amendment. So the block will be re-hashed at that point anyway, and the wording can be fixed then. This lowers the stakes.

The second half of the finding is weaker. "Whatever an earlier condition of its step gives" naturally means that figures are computed even where a step stopped early. The digest item's own explicit "where" clause is the specific text and governs the general phrase. Still, an implementer could read step 4's first condition as one of those "earlier conditions". On that reading (c)'s C2 N would enter the default digest, which changes whether a catalog correction that moves only that N forces a re-ask.

Both are wording imprecisions with a plausible alternate reading, not a contradiction in what the rule decides under its defaults. The note grade is right. If C5 under choice 6 is weighted, it could be argued up to minor.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** In the digest line: "...; where the run's deciding cycles (D, or D plus C5) have at least two forward swing cycles - this condition is the digest's own and is not lifted by 'whatever an earlier condition of its step gives' - N of (c) and of no change in each of them; once C5 is measured, the same over D plus C5."

### 6. Reading (vii)'s guarantee is stated unconditionally, but it lapses once choice 7 is ruled for its default (new, from decision 1)

- **Lens:** fix-check - **reviewer:** note - **skeptic:** note
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:836

**The scenario.** Line 894 makes (vii) printed only once choice 7 is answered either way. If Elias answers 'counted', C4's figures in the sums can again carry (b), or turn (a) into S1 or S1 into (a), with no ask. Lines 836-838 ('C4 carries no switch without an ask ... reading (vii) stops the rule') and line 305 ('S4's reading (vii) asks') would then be false. Line 894's recipe only marks the reading printed only. Line 305 has a caveat for choice 1 ('while S4's reading (ii) stands') but none for choice 7.

**The fix proposed.** Line 305: 'while choice 7 is unruled, where its figures ... (vii) asks'. Line 894: 'a ruling on choice 7 also rewrites THE OUTCOME's sentence on C4'. Optionally, line 836: '... while reading (vii) stands'.

**The skeptic's evidence.** Staged docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md (read via `git show :docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md`).

Inside the hashed block (markers at lines 702 and 890), lines 836-838 say: "C4 carries no switch without an ask: its own conditions only veto, and where its figures in the sums decide, reading (vii) stops the rule." Lines 820-822 define (vii) as "every sum over D in steps 2 and 5 taken over D's swing cycles alone." Line 823 says: "The rule STOPS (S4) if any reading changes the outcome."

Line 894 says: "A choice he answers - for its default or for its alternative - is ruled: it is written into the block by amendment, and its S4 reading becomes printed only (choices 1-4 and 7: readings ... (vii))." Lines 864 and 868 confirm that a printed-only reading no longer takes part in deciding. So if Elias rules choice 7 "counted" (its default, line 901), (vii) no longer fires S4. C4's figures in the step-2 and step-5 sums can then decide (b) or S1/(a) with no stop.

From that point the clause "reading (vii) stops the rule" (line 838) is false. So is line 305's "where its figures in step 2's and step 5's sums decide, S4's reading (vii) asks". The first clause, "no switch without an ask", is arguable: the blind choice 7 was itself an ask.

The inconsistency is visible now. Line 305 gives choice 1 the caveat "while S4's reading (ii) stands ... (a blind answer to choice 1, either way, makes (ii) printed only)" but gives choice 7 no such caveat. Line 894's recipe only marks the reading printed only; it does not say to rewrite THE OUTCOME's sentence about C4.

Why only a note: the statements are true at this commit, since no choice is ruled. Word to run on the defaults keeps (vii) live (line 894), so it lapses only on an explicit ruling of "counted". That ruling is itself an amendment of the block, made by Elias, and a careful amender would see the sentence. The rule's decision today does not change. This is wording that will go stale, not a defect in what the rule decides.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Make the guarantee conditional now, while the block's hash is still open:
- Line 838 (inside the block): "...and, while reading (vii) stands, where its figures in the sums decide, reading (vii) stops the rule."
- Line 305: "...and, while S4's reading (vii) stands, where its figures in step 2's and step 5's sums decide, (vii) asks (a blind answer to choice 7, either way, makes (vii) printed only)". This mirrors the existing caveat for choice 1 and reading (ii).
- Optionally, line 894: "a ruling on choice 7 also rewrites THE OUTCOME's sentence on C4."

### 7. THE TERMS now close the record at 2024, but C5 is measured without amendment (new, from the fix to finding 21)

- **Lens:** fix-check - **reviewer:** note - **skeptic:** note
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:708

**The scenario.** Line 708 now reads 'The record's House elections are 2016, 2018, 2020, 2022 and 2024'. C5 (lines 884-888) is measured and run without amendment, and its truth is 'the record's own district plurality' (line 768). But 2026 is not one of the record's elections, so C5's truth falls outside the defined record. After this commit the line can change only by Elias's ruling. No figure in D moves.

**The fix proposed.** Before the digest is fixed: '... 2016, 2018, 2020, 2022 and 2024, and 2026 once C5 is measured; its first transition is 2016->2018.'

**The skeptic's evidence.** The wording problem is real, but it cannot change any outcome. Staged docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md, inside the fence:

- Line 708 says "The record's House elections are 2016, 2018, 2020, 2022 and 2024; its first transition is 2016->2018." That is a closed list.
- Line 710 adds "C5 2024->2026 once measured".
- Line 768 says "The truth is the record's own district plurality (house_districts.csv's winner)."
- Line 769 makes "R_s and D_s ... the record's decided seats by party".
- Lines 884-885 say "C5 ... is BILLED until the Clerk's 2026 statistics are saved. Then it is measured".

So C5's later election, 2026, is outside the defined record, yet C5's truth and R_s/D_s are defined as "the record's". Read literally, the block leaves C5 without a truth. Nothing outside the fence settles this, and the fence becomes immutable at this commit, so the tension is real.

Why it cannot change an outcome:
1. The block says plainly, at lines 710 and 884-888, that C5 is measured and run. Line 768's parenthetical names the file, house_districts.csv's winner. An implementer has no reasonable reading under which C5 is unscored, and both readings send C5's truth to the same column.
2. The other place "the record" matters is (c)'s window (line 761): "every transition of the record whose later election precedes T". For C5, T = 2026. Whether or not 2026 is in the record, the 2024->2026 transition does not precede T, so the window is the same.
3. Step 7 already refers to "a measured cycle whose later election is after 2024" and keeps "2016-2024" for the truth gate. That reads 2026 as data added once measured.

The finding's own premise is right that no figure in D moves. So this is wording only (note), not minor: implementers cannot split into two readings with different outcomes. rec791.md still has the digest as the placeholder DIGEST791, so the line can still be fixed before the digest is computed.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Before the digest is computed, change line 708 to: "- The record's House elections are 2016, 2018, 2020, 2022 and 2024, and 2026 once C5 is measured; its first transition is 2016->2018." Choice 6 still rewrites only the first year. Recompute the digest after the edit.

### 8. Reading (ii) in the C5 run is undefined, and the new 'C2 is the only swing cycle' contradicts one reading of it (residual of findings 5/38/66/79)

- **Lens:** fix-check - **reviewer:** note - **skeptic:** minor
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:815

**The scenario.** The run 'over D plus C5' (line 885) re-runs step 6. Under (ii), D is either {C2, C4} (the item's cycles) or {C2, C4} plus C5. In the second case, line 815's 'C2 is the only swing cycle' is false, and the reason S2, S3 and S6 are printed ('each would rest on one swing cycle') no longer holds. The two readings can differ on whether S4 (ii) fires in the C5 run, and so on whether R-US18 is asked again.

**The fix proposed.** Before the digest is fixed, in the C5 paragraph: 'in that run reading (ii) is D = {C2, C4} plus C5, its swing cycles C2 and C5, and S2, S3 and S6 are compared'. Or say that (ii) is not re-run over D plus C5.

**The skeptic's evidence.** Staged docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md, inside the R-US18 fence. L815-816: "(ii) the item's own cycles, D = {C2, C4}: C2 is the only swing cycle; the step-2 verdict and S5 are compared; S2, S3 and S6 are printed, since each would rest on one swing cycle". L822: "The rule STOPS (S4) if any reading changes the outcome". L884-888: "C5 ... Then it is measured, a swing cycle; the rule is run again over D plus C5 ... Either giving an outcome other than the one ruled on ... asks R-US18 again". L866-868 (digest): "under every reading not made printed only ... once C5 is measured, the same over D plus C5". L888: "D itself changes only by amendment."

The fence never says how reading (ii) combines with the C5 run. (ii) assigns D = {C2, C4}. The C5 run is "over D plus C5". That leaves two readings: (A) under (ii) the C5 run uses {C2, C4}, which is the literal set (ii) names, so the run is identical to the pre-C5 one; or (B) it uses {C2, C4} plus C5, with swing cycles C2 and C5. Under (B), L815's "C2 is the only swing cycle" is false, and the stated reason for printing S2, S3 and S6 no longer holds, so a reader could also take those three as compared rather than printed. The two readings give different step-2 verdicts and S5 results for the (ii) row of the C5 run. They can therefore disagree on whether S4 (ii) fires, which decides whether R-US18 is asked again at L886-888. They also change the figures in the decisive digest (L866-868). The first pass raised this exact point: pass1 findings 5, 38, 66 and 79, where finding 38's fix named the C5-rerun cycle sets and 79's said "a reading or re-run that changes the cycle set keeps each cycle's role". The fix that went in was the role phrasing ("every swing cycle of D"), which settles the steps but not how (ii) combines with D plus C5. (ii) stays live unless Elias answers choice 1 (L135: word to run on the defaults rules none of them), so this path is reachable once C5 is measured. Severity: an implementer can resolve it two ways with different outcomes, and the block is not contradictory on every reading, so this is minor rather than defect. The reviewer's "note" grades it too low.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Inside the fence, before the digest is fixed, settle it in the C5 paragraph (L884-888). Either (B): "In the run over D plus C5, reading (ii) is {C2, C4} plus C5, its swing cycles C2 and C5; S2, S3 and S6 are compared in it." In that case L815-816 should say "C2 is then the only swing cycle (C2 and C5 in the run over D plus C5)". Or (A): "Reading (ii) is not run again over D plus C5; its pre-C5 result stands." Whichever is chosen, make the digest clause at L866-868 name the same set under (ii).

### 9. Line 339 says R-US18 'is asked again' when house_by_state.csv moves, but the block re-pins under RULED when the outcome is unchanged (fix to finding 42)

- **Lens:** fix-check - **reviewer:** note - **skeptic:** note
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:339

**The scenario.** Suppose a §788 ruling moves house_by_state.csv while R-US18 is RULED and the outcome is unchanged. Line 875 has it re-pinned with 'outcome unchanged'. Line 339 says R-US18 is asked again. The two disagree on whether Elias is asked; the block governs.

**The fix proposed.** '... moves V(E) and the decisive digest, which R-US18's block handles by its state (re-registered under ASKED; re-pinned or asked again under RULED)'.

**The skeptic's evidence.** The finding holds. Line 339 of the staged USA_STAGE_PLAN.md, in the list under "What the record cannot decide, flagged", reads: "US-5's House basis (§788, asked) - a ruling that moves `house_by_state.csv` moves V(E) and the decisive digest, and R-US18 is asked again;". It says this unconditionally. The R-US18 block (staged lines ~868-878) handles each state differently:
- "ASKED: ... A moved digest fails until the ask is registered again with the new table, and Elias is told." This roughly matches "asked again".
- "RULED: ... A moved digest with the same outcome fails until a commit re-pins it, naming the old digest, the new one and "outcome unchanged". A changed outcome ... fails until R-US18 is asked again; it is never re-pinned."
- "NONE holds only before it". Before the first run there is nothing to ask again.

So the scenario can happen. Under RULED, a §788 ruling that moves V(E) but leaves the outcome the same is re-pinned, and Elias is not asked. Line 339 says he is. The first pass's fix to finding 42 brought line 300 into line ("a correction of the catalog, re-pinned or asked again as R-US18's block says") but missed line 339, so the plan now uses both wordings for the same mechanism.

Grade: note, not minor. Line 339 sits in an informational "flagged" list outside the hashed block. It sets no check, and the hashed block decides each state explicitly. An implementer building the bars follows the block, so the wording cannot change what the rule decides. The text is still inaccurate as written, so it should be fixed.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Line 339: "US-5's House basis (§788, asked) - a ruling that moves `house_by_state.csv` moves V(E) and the decisive digest, which R-US18's block handles by its state (registered again while ASKED; re-pinned if the outcome is unchanged, otherwise asked again, while RULED);". This wording also matches the "as R-US18's block says" phrasing on line 300.
