- line 37510 cites scratchpad `us4_proto.pl` as the independent proof of nine pinned figures
- lines 31169 and 33585 cite other scratchpad-only material
Citing a one-off scratch derivation in a record is established practice, not a lapse.

(2) The rules it invokes don't cover these scripts.
- CLAUDE.md:73 (§576) covers "tooling a pass depends on". Nothing later depends on digest791.pl. The plan states the digest's method, and the instrument recomputes the digest and fails on a mismatch. The script's header line states its whole method (SHA-256 over the UTF-8 bytes of the lines strictly between the markers, CR removed), and it also checks that the index and working copy agree. facts791.pl's figures are vote shares, maps and delegations re-derivable from committed CSVs.
- CLAUDE.md:74 (§643) covers a design a later session depends on. That design is committed: the rule block, US-11's Design, and the panel's specification and brief.

The brief is committed verbatim at Reviews/2026-10-06_s791_us11_blind_panel.md:812 ("## Appendix - the brief (`facts.md`), verbatim"). So line 5 can point there at no cost.

Net: no rule decision, digest or fact is affected. This is citation wording only.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** In rec791.md line 5, cite the brief as "`facts.md`, committed verbatim as the appendix of `Reviews/2026-10-06_s791_us11_blind_panel.md`" rather than the scratch path. At lines 8 and 24, keep the scratch citations as earlier records do (§ at COMPLETED.md 31381, 37510), but mark each as a one-off and give its one-line method:
- digest: "SHA-256 over the raw UTF-8 bytes of the lines strictly between the markers, CR removed, read from the index blob and required to equal the working copy's"
- facts: "vote shares, maps and delegations summed from house_by_state.csv, house_districts.csv and house_maps.csv"

Committing them under Tools/ is optional, because no later pass depends on them. The instrument recomputes the digest. Drop the reviewer's claim that only one earlier record cites a scratchpad script.

### 41. The record credits CLAUDE.md with a 'design declared before its code' rule it does not contain

- **Lens:** provenance - **reviewer:** note - **skeptic:** note
- **Where:** C:/Users/elias/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-elias/c8a2f04f-adf9-462b-8470-14c260fdbdb5/scratchpad/rec791.md:3

**The scenario.** Line 3: "CLAUDE.md's design rule wants the design declared before its code". CLAUDE.md has 'Measure before you propose, and propose before you build' and §643 (a design is written to the repo the day it is made). The 'DECLARED' wording comes from the plan's grain ('Premises named: DECLARED or ruled'; 'Measured before live') and §788's practice.

**The fix proposed.** Cite the plan's grain and CLAUDE.md §643 ('propose before you build'), or quote the actual rule.

**The skeptic's evidence.** rec791.md line 3 says: "A rule written after the figures are in sight can be tuned to them; CLAUDE.md's design rule wants the design declared before its code." I searched G:/UNITY/Projects/PoliSim/CLAUDE.md for "design rule", "declared before", "before its code", "before any code" and "design.*before", and none of them turns up such a rule. The closest rule is line 108 under THE WORKING DISCIPLINE: "**Measure before you propose, and propose before you build.**" Line 74 (§643), "A DESIGN A LATER SESSION DEPENDS ON IS WRITTEN TO THE REPO, NEVER A SCRATCHPAD OR A CONVERSATION", is about where a design is written and when ("the day they are made"). It says nothing about the design coming before its code. The only "DECLARED ... BEFORE" rule in CLAUDE.md is line 60, the dry film's scope (§574), which has nothing to do with this. So the record's sentence is a loose paraphrase of line 108, presented as a named "design rule" with the word "declared", and CLAUDE.md has neither. Its meaning is roughly faithful: "propose before you build" does imply the design comes before the instrument. It changes no decision, so this is a wording note and not a defect. One correction to the finding's own fix: §643 should not be cited as the before-code rule, because it governs where a design lives and not when. Also, COMPLETED.md is exempt from the claim checks, so no bar catches this.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** In rec791.md line 3, replace "CLAUDE.md's design rule wants the design declared before its code" with words that quote the rule that exists, e.g. "CLAUDE.md's working discipline is 'measure before you propose, and propose before you build'". If the DECLARED wording is wanted, attribute it to the stage plan's own grain ("Premises named: DECLARED or ruled") and not to CLAUDE.md. Do not cite §643 for timing: it rules where a design is written, not that it comes before code.

### 42. The appendix is called verbatim, but the renderer demoted the brief's headings

- **Lens:** provenance - **reviewer:** note - **skeptic:** note
- **Where:** Reviews/2026-10-06_s791_us11_blind_panel.md:812

**The scenario.** render_panel.pl applies s/^#/##/mg to facts.md, so '# US-11 / R-US18 ...' becomes '## ...' and each '## n.' becomes '### n.'. It also adds the section headings, '**Pn.**' and '*Reason.*'. The content is otherwise exact: facts.md matches after demotion, and all 141 string fields of final_spec.json appear as exact substrings.

**The fix proposed.** Line 5 or the appendix heading: 'verbatim, its headings one level down'.

**The skeptic's evidence.** The finding holds, at note level. I traced it in render_panel.pl (in the scratchpad folder us11design). The appendix is built with `my $b = slurp($facts); $b =~ s/^#/##/mg; $o .= $b;`, so every heading in facts.md moves down one level. In the staged Reviews/2026-10-06_s791_us11_blind_panel.md, line 812 reads '## Appendix - the brief (`facts.md`), verbatim'. Line 814 then reads '## US-11 / R-US18 - the facts sheet ...', where facts.md line 1 is '# US-11 / R-US18 ...'. Lines 818, 838, 845 and 882 read '### 1.' to '### 4.', where facts.md lines 5, 25, 32 and 69 read '## 1.' to '## 4.'. Line 5 of the Reviews file says '**Everything below is the panel's output and its brief, verbatim**'. So the claim is not literally true of the heading markup.

Nothing else changes the content. facts.md has exactly five lines starting with '#', all of them headings, so the substitution touches nothing else. Neither facts.md nor any string field in final_spec.json contains a CR or three newlines in a row, so the closing `s/\n{3,}/\n\n/g` and the CRLF normalisation change nothing. The spec sections are printed unchanged under headings the script adds ('### Rationale', '**Pn.**', '*Reason.*', 'A1.'/'R1.'). Those are labels around the text, not changes to it.

This is a wording inaccuracy only. It does not affect the R-US18 rule block, its digest or any decision.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** In Reviews/2026-10-06_s791_us11_blind_panel.md line 5 (and in the matching string in render_panel.pl so a re-render stays the same), write '...its brief, verbatim (the brief's headings one level down, under section headings added here)'. Or change the appendix heading at line 812 to '## Appendix - the brief (`facts.md`), verbatim, its headings one level down'.

## The second pass - refuted by the skeptics

- [fix-check] The panel file names a review file that does not exist - *The facts check out: Reviews/2026-10-06_s791_us11_blind_panel.md line 5 says "§791 and `Reviews/2026-10-06_s791_us11_rule_review.md` list each correction". `git status` shows only that panel file and docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md as staged, and Reviews/ on disk holds s780-s790 plus s791_blind_panel. The staged plan diff has no Reviews/ reference.

It is still not a defect. It is the project's normal order of work. Every earlier commit (s786, s787, s788, s789, s790) adds its own review report, and that report is written from the review that runs on the staged change. For example, s790's is "A workflow review (`polisim-staged-review`) of the change that ...". The review running now is the one that file will report. It cannot exist until this pass ends, and line 5 names it ahead of time on purpose. The draft record rec791.md also has the `REVIEW791` placeholder (line 31) where the review pointer goes, next to `BARS791`, so the file is already planned in the commit sequence. CLAUDE.md line 62 sets the same order: the review runs, then "its report is under `Reviews/`", then the row.

The dangling reference only appears if the session commits without finishing its own sequence. That is the finding's conditional "If the commit lands without it", not anything wrong in the staged text. The reference does not change what R-US18 decides and has no effect on the rule block's SHA-256, because the panel file sits outside the plan's markers. At most it is a reminder for the commit checklist.*
- [fence-fresh] 'N' names both the no-winner district and the national seat error inside the hashed block - *The overlap is in the text, but no reading of the rule can come out differently because of it. Staged USA_STAGE_PLAN.md line 727: "A base district's share d: none if it has no winner and no votes (N); 1 or 0 by its winner's party if unopposed and printed without votes (U)". The letters in brackets are the data's own flag codes, not variables. ElectionsData/usa/house_districts.csv header line 16 reads "#   N  no winner of record - no votes printed, the election not certified" and line 18 reads "#   U  unopposed, its name not printed on the ballot - no votes". house_districts.md line 34 counts "flags C 1 F 31 N 1 S 5 U 4" for 2018. So '(N)' points at the catalog's flag N (NC-9 2018). The block mentions that flag once, at line 727, and nothing in the block uses it again. Every later N (774 "N = the signed distance of REP's national seats...", 794, 800, 802, 839, 866, 867) is a seat-level |N| or a figure listed with H, G and K, and none of them can be read as a district flag. The flag U, by contrast, is used again as a name (742 "U_R and U_D are the base's U seats"), which shows the bracketed letters are deliberately the catalog's flag names. Nothing the rule decides, no figure in the decisive digest, and no stop changes under either reading. At most this is cosmetic. If the block is touched before the digest is fixed anyway, '(flag N)' / '(flag U)' would say outright that these are the CSV's flags.*
- [provenance] Panel header points to a review file that does not exist - *The pointer is real but it is not dangling. It is a forward reference to the review report that this review produces. Reviews/2026-10-06_s791_us11_blind_panel.md:5 says "§791 and `Reviews/2026-10-06_s791_us11_rule_review.md` list each correction". It is true that no such file exists yet. Only blind_panel.md and USA_STAGE_PLAN.md are staged, and USA_STAGE_PLAN.md does not name the file.

The repo's convention shows where the file comes from. Every recent commit carries exactly one Reviews/ file, and that file is the staged-review workflow's report, written after the review and committed with it. Per-commit counts of Reviews/ files: 8f7b87fa 1, 14ee39b6 1, 40583b01 1, 43169a7a 1, 947921ab 1, 1b8ba4ed 1, 7d71fcc4 1, b183a332 1. The s790 file opens "A workflow review (`polisim-staged-review`) of the change ...". CLAUDE.md:62 also requires the review report to be under Reviews/ before the bar runs.

The record draft shows the same thing. scratchpad/rec791.md still has the placeholder line `REVIEW791` (line 31), next to `BARS791`. That marks the review report as owed: it is written and staged after this pass, before the commit. So "if the commit goes in as staged" describes a mid-review snapshot, not the commit.

The one real risk is the name. Neither rec791 nor anything else fixes the file name, so the report could land under a different one, for example ..._s791_us11_blind_rule.md, and line 5 would then point nowhere. This does not touch the R-US18 block, its SHA-256 (the block lies outside blind_panel.md), or any decision of the rule.*
- [provenance] 'The Clerk's 213 of 435' is pinned on the first draft, but it was the brief's phrase and the panel adopted it - *The phrase does come from the brief: facts.md:73 reads "2020's NY-22: the Clerk's 213 of 435 or [HH-DIV]'s 212 of 434". The panel file also uses it, at line 348 ("213 REP / 222 DEM of 435, by the Clerk"), line 525 ("The 2020 truth is the Clerk's 213 of 435") and line 144 ("2020 by the Clerk"). But the finding's two premises do not hold.

(1) The record does not say the first draft originated the phrase. rec791.md:22 says only that "the first draft's 'the Clerk's 213 of 435' was wrong besides (the Clerk's own table ... prints 212 and a vacancy ...; 213 is the catalog's district plurality)". That is true: pass1 findings 56 and 88 show the draft's choice 2 had this wording, and the session replaced it (staged plan, choice 2: "The district plurality on the Clerk's counts ... or the division as published ... [HH-DIV]'s 212 of 434, which the Clerk's own table of political divisions ... prints too").

(2) The phrase is not wrong in the brief or in the panel's spec, because both define it in scope. facts.md:44 sets "The Clerk's own district plurality - each candidate's lines summed ... gives ... 2020 213/222 (NY-22 to Tenney)". The panel's truth section, which holds line 348, opens with "Every cycle is scored against the record's own district plurality on the Clerk's final counts". In both places "the Clerk's 213" is shorthand for that defined quantity. In the first draft the shorthand stood alone in an ask set against [HH-DIV]'s published 212, so there it read as the Clerk's published figure. That is why the record judges it wrong there.

rec791.md:5's list has a narrower subject than the finding says. It covers brief facts "not on a saved page" (PA 2018 court-ordered, GA-6, the 39/38 count) and the T-1 = 2016 claim. It is not a list of everything wrong in the brief. The 213 is on the record (house_districts.csv's winner) and the brief defines it, so it does not belong there. Adding it, as the finding's fix proposes, would overclaim that the brief was wrong.

What is left is wording only. A reader of the committed panel file meets "the Clerk's 213 of 435" at line 525, and a short note that the shorthand came from the brief's definition would help them. The record makes no false claim, and nothing in the rule or plan changes.*
- [provenance] A figure is typed inside the digest-locked block (212/434) and in choice 2 - *I could not make the failure happen. USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:813-814 says "(i) 2020's truth as published: [HH-DIV]'s division, REP 212 of 434 decided, NY-22 undecided and scored by interval; (c)'s S(2020) = 212/434". The number is tied to its source, [HH-DIV]'s published 2020 division, which is a fixed historical record. It is not a fact about the code, the catalog or the environment.

1. The claim convention (CLAUDE.md:37-41) does not catch it. A DERIVED claim there is "a fact about the code or the environment", and its test is "the code can change freely and no document becomes wrong". Changing or re-reading code cannot make [HH-DIV]'s published 2020 row wrong. §419 ("Figures generated, never typed", plan:136) covers figures the code and catalog use, such as tables typed into C#. The plan already types sourced [HH-DIV] figures in the same way, for example the Done-when at line 296 ("REP 222 / DEM 213; REP 220 / DEM 215") and line 97 ("538 electors, 270 to elect").

2. The block agrees with the catalog: ElectionsData/usa/house_years.csv:7 reads `2020,117,212,222,0,NY-22`, which is REP 212, DEM 222 and NY-22 vacant, so 212 + 222 = 434 decided. The 434 is plain arithmetic on the sourced row: 435 seats less the one footnoted vacancy.

3. The finding's scenario was that "the 2020 row is ever re-read differently". That can only happen if the catalog misreads a fixed publication, which is a data defect. If it did, the figure in the block would act as a cross-check against the source the block names, and the rule would still be decidable. The rule would still score [HH-DIV]'s division, so what it decides does not change.

4. The first pass weighed this exact point and kept the figure on purpose (pass1_findings.md:139: "Leave the fence's 'REP 212 of 434 ... (c)'s S(2020) = 212/434' ... figures of record cited to [HH-DIV], they agree with house_years.csv"; #56 and #88 removed only the wrong 213 and kept the sourced 212). Choice 2 at line 896 is outside the markers and cites the same source.

The suggested rewording means the same thing and would only move the preregistered text away from the panel's spec. It is optional, not owed.*

## What was done about the second pass

Forty-two findings survived; every one was acted on - thirteen edits to the block, nine to the design, the panel file re-rendered and the record rewritten.

- **The ruling record held one outcome (1, 15).** Once C5 is measured the rule makes two runs, and one stored outcome could never match both, so a ruling could never hold. The record now keeps the outcome of each run, ASKED the outcomes asked (19), and each run is compared with its own. NONE fails every run until its ask is registered (27).
- **A seat won outside the two parties (2, 20).** W_s gains O_s - S7's first scoring - where the formula went half-integer; |N| counts it too; S7's second scoring moves it into Z_s.
- **Reading (ii) in the C5 run (8, 21)** is {C2, C4, C5}; S2, S3 and S6 are compared where its D has two swing cycles. The C5 run reads every D as D plus C5. The record's elections include 2026 once C5 is measured (7). The digest's step-4 clause reads "at least two" (5, 25) and ends "nothing else is in it" (17).
- **S5 suppressed when (b) is picked (16).** S5 now fires beside S2, so S2's option "the other" cannot install (a) with its fallback unasked.
- **The buildable options (18, 22).** (b') folds into (b) declared again by amendment, which must say what it gives where the curve is not fitted and whether its parameters are refit; (c) is reconciled to (a) with F-old, (a) with F-prop or (b), and the design's reconciliation covers an F-prop state.
- **C4's guarantee (6, 26)** holds while reading (vii) stands, over step 2's and step 5's sums. The comparisons sentence is scoped to steps 1-5 (28).
- **The panel file (4, 24, 32, 30, 31, 34, 42, 29).** Its header was double-encoded (the renderer lacked `use utf8`) and miscounted the attacks: the critics raised twenty-two (twelve and ten, counted in the run's journal) and the reviser accepted all of them, four only in part - the "rejected" entries are legs of those four fixes. Re-rendered with both corrected, "verbatim" narrowed to what the file holds, the brief's demoted headings said; this file is the review it points to.
- **The design and asks (3, 9, 10, 11, 12, 13, 23, 35, 37, 38).** No count in the plan's provenance; the US-5 basis line as the block handles it; the corrections listed in §791, not R-US18's entry; the panel quoted exactly ("C4 can only veto"); `in_force` against `lines_changed` a data failure, not a sentence; the cycles' years given; Pennsylvania 2018 named as a DECLARED redraw, its instrument BILLED.
- **The record (14, 33, 36, 39, 40, 41).** The wait's reach (US-11's close and US-13); the corrections split into departures and restorations; the identities qualified; the specification's signal for S5 named; the one-party counts' basis (NC-9 apart); what the review read in the panel's transcripts; methods in place of scratch-script paths; CLAUDE.md quoted for what it says.

## The third pass - confirmed (verbatim)

A third, narrow pass over the second pass's thirteen edits to the block, 7 agents: one lens, every finding put to a refute-first skeptic.

### 1. A ruling made before C5 is measured leaves the D+C5 run with no outcome of its own, but the RULED bar and the C5 paragraph compare against exactly that

- **Lens:** edits - **reviewer:** defect - **skeptic:** defect
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:885

**The scenario.** Edits 7 and 10 changed the comparison from 'the one ruled on' to 'that run's outcome in the ruling' (885) and 'its own in the ruling' (900). The RULED record (868-870) holds 'the outcome of each run it was made on'. A ruling made before C5 is measured was made only on the run over D. That is the expected path, since the Clerk's 2026 statistics are due in 2027 (line 305). Example: RULED (a) with F-old in 2026, recorded outcome '(a)' for the D run. C5 is read in 2027 and the run over D plus C5 gives '(a)' or '(a), S3'. Nothing in the block defines that run's outcome 'in the ruling'. Build A compares it with the D run's ruled outcome: '(a)' passes, the digest has moved, so the result is a re-pin, and '(a), S3' re-asks. Build B treats a missing outcome as changed and re-asks on every C5 measurement. Build C compares nothing: it re-pins, and even '(a), S3' goes green. Each build needs this term and the block leaves it undefined.

**The fix proposed.** Inside the fence, at 885 and 900, add: 'a run the ruling was not made on - the run over D plus C5 under a ruling made before C5 was measured - is compared with the ruling's outcome of the run over D'. Or, if the intent is to re-ask whenever C5 is first measured, say so: '- or, where the ruling was made before C5 was measured, R-US18 is asked again with the new table'.

**The skeptic's evidence.** The finding holds. Every line it cites is in the staged block of docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md, and nothing inside the fence closes the gap.

1. What the record holds (lines 868-870): RULED keeps "the outcome of each run it was made on (the run over D and, once C5 is measured, the run over D plus C5 with step 1 over C5's redrawn states)". A ruling made before C5 is measured was made on the run over D only, so the record holds no outcome for the run over D plus C5.

2. The scenario is the expected path, not an edge case:
   - C5 stays BILLED until the Clerk's 2026 statistics are saved, "due 2027" (line 305; also lines 463 and 1002).
   - US-16 fails until R-US18 is RULED (lines 894-895), so a ruling before C5 exists is what the plan works toward.
   - Once C5 is measured, the decisive digest gains "once C5 is measured, the same over D plus C5" (lines 879-880), so the digest moves too.

3. Both comparisons need the undefined term:
   - The RULED bar (line 885): "the bars pass while each run's generated outcome equals that run's outcome in the ruling. A moved digest with the same outcomes fails until a commit re-pins it ... A changed outcome fails until R-US18 is asked again."
   - The C5 paragraph (lines 899-901): "Either run giving an outcome other than its own in the ruling ... asks R-US18 again."
   For the new run there is no "outcome in the ruling", so neither "same outcomes" (which allows a re-pin) nor "changed outcome" (which forces a new ask) is defined.

4. Faithful builds diverge:
   - Treat the missing outcome as unequal: the bars fail and R-US18 is asked again on every first C5 measurement.
   - Compare against the D run's outcome: a re-pin, or a new ask only if the D+C5 run differs.
   - Treat it as nothing to compare: a re-pin, and even "(a), S3" goes green.

5. A promised state may never be reached. Under the second and third builds the RULED record never gains the outcome of the run over D plus C5, which line 869 says it holds once C5 is measured. A re-pin carries only the old digest, the new one and "outcome unchanged". A new ruling follows only an ask. An ask follows only a "changed outcome".

6. Nothing else in the block settles it. "R-US18 is asked with the full table whatever the outcome" (line 852) and "NONE: every run fails the bars until its ask is registered" (line 881) apply to NONE and to the first run, not to a run that first appears under RULED.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Inside the fence, add one sentence to the RULED bullet (line 885) and mirror it in the C5 paragraph (line 900), which should cite it.

Re-ask form, recommended. It is consistent with "asked with the full table whatever the outcome", and it is the only form that ever writes the D+C5 outcome into the record:

"A run the ruling was not made on - the run over D plus C5, under a ruling made before C5 was measured - has no outcome in it: its first run fails the bars until R-US18 is asked again with the new table, and the new ruling records both runs."

Alternative, if the intent is that a ruling made before C5 carries over:

"A run the ruling was not made on is compared with the ruling's outcome of the run over D, and a commit that re-pins it records that run's outcome beside the D run's."

Either sentence must also say how the RULED record comes to hold the D+C5 run's outcome, which line 869 says it holds once C5 is measured.

### 2. Step 1 over C5's redrawn states is checked against the ruled fallback, not a recorded result, so a ruled S5 option the C5 states disfavour can never hold

- **Lens:** edits - **reviewer:** defect - **skeptic:** defect
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:900

**The scenario.** The RULED record (869-870) stores, per run, 'the run over D plus C5 with step 1 over C5's redrawn states'. The C5 paragraph (900-901) does not compare that step with the record. It compares it with 'the ruled one' ('for step 1, a fallback other than the ruled one'). Example: C5 is measured. In the run over D plus C5, F-prop's sum is strictly lower, so S5 fires, and F-prop is also strictly lower over C5's redrawn states alone. Elias rules S5's option 'F-old' (860), which is buildable by 889. Step 1 over C5's states now gives a fallback other than the ruled F-old, so R-US18 is asked again. Re-ruling F-old reproduces the same comparison and asks again, every time. The mirror case behaves the same way: F-prop is ruled and C5's states keep F-old. RULED with that option is therefore unreachable without an amendment, although the block offers the option and calls it buildable. Separately, 869-870 stores step 1 over C5 unconditionally, while 899-900 runs it only 'where the ruled option counts them by (a)'s fallback'.

**The fix proposed.** At 900-901: 'for step 1 over C5's redrawn states, a fallback other than the one recorded with the ruling (the ruled one, where the ruling was made before C5 was measured)'. At 869-870, record step 1 over C5's result only 'where the ruled option counts them by (a)'s fallback', matching 899-900.

**The skeptic's evidence.** The block gives two different tests for step 1 over C5's redrawn states, and they conflict.

1. The RULED record and the bars clause compare each run with the result recorded in the ruling. The RULED record (THE ASK AND THE BARS) stores "the outcome of each run it was made on (the run over D and, once C5 is measured, the run over D plus C5 with step 1 over C5's redrawn states)". The bars then say: "RULED: the bars pass while each run's generated outcome equals that run's outcome in the ruling."

2. The C5 paragraph compares step 1 with the ruled option instead: "Either run giving an outcome other than its own in the ruling - for step 1, a fallback other than the ruled one - asks R-US18 again, and the bars fail until the ask is registered."

So for step 1 over C5, the step 1 result stored in the RULED record is never what gets checked. The check is against the ruled option.

Failing path (the mirror case is the clean one):
- Step 1 can only return "F-old is kept if its sum is not above F-prop's, ties included" or S5. "The rule never switches the fallback by itself", so step 1 never returns F-prop.
- Elias rules S5's option "F-prop, persisting in every state it counts". The block calls this buildable: "(a) with F-old or with F-prop".
- After C5 is measured, suppose C5's redrawn states give F-old a sum not above F-prop's (a tie is enough). Step 1 over them then gives F-old, which is "a fallback other than the ruled one", so R-US18 is asked again.
- Elias re-rules F-prop on the same data. The ruling records this step 1 result, but the C5 paragraph still compares with "the ruled one" (F-prop), so it is asked again, every time.
- RULED with F-prop is therefore not stable while C5's states keep F-old, short of "amend the rule as ruled". The RULED bars clause, which compares with the recorded outcome, would pass in the same state. That is the contradiction.

The finding's own example (F-old ruled while C5's states fire S5) depends on reading S5 as "a fallback other than F-old". That reading is weaker, but the F-prop case does not need it.

Two limits on the finding:
- An implementer could read the C5 paragraph as a one-time check when C5 is first measured, which would weaken the loop. Its wording ("the bars fail until the ask is registered") reads as a standing check.
- The second point is real but minor. The RULED record stores step 1 over C5 without condition, while the C5 paragraph runs it only "where the ruled option counts them by (a)'s fallback". For a ruling of (b) or (c) reconciled to (b), it is undefined what gets recorded.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** In the C5 paragraph, replace "for step 1, a fallback other than the ruled one" with "for step 1 over C5's redrawn states, a result other than the one recorded in the ruling (or, where the ruling was made before C5 was measured, a fallback other than the ruled one)". In the RULED record, change the parenthesis to "(the run over D and, once C5 is measured, the run over D plus C5, with step 1 over C5's redrawn states where the ruled option counts them by (a)'s fallback)", so it matches the C5 paragraph.

### 3. Reading (ii) prints S2 where its D has one swing cycle, but S5's exception depends on whether S2 fires

- **Lens:** edits - **reviewer:** minor - **skeptic:** minor
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:787

**The scenario.** Edit 4 makes S5 depend on S2: S5 is suppressed only when 'step 2 picks (b) and S2 does not fire' (787-788). Edit 5 makes S2 'printed' in (ii) over {C2, C4} (822-823), and 'the step-2 verdict and S5 are compared'. Example: the main run over {C1, C2, C4} picks (b). S2 does not fire, because (b)'s |N| exceeds (a)'s in C2 but not in C1. F-prop is strictly lower, so S5 is suppressed and the run decides (b). Reading (ii) also picks (b), F-prop is strictly lower over (ii)'s redrawn state-cycles, and S2's test over C2 alone holds. Build A counts the printed S2 as firing, so S5 fires in (ii), (ii) gives '(b), S5' against '(b)', and S4(ii) stops the rule. Build B treats a printed S2 as not firing, so S5 stays suppressed, the outcomes match and the rule decides (b). The two builds disagree on whether the rule decides.

**The fix proposed.** In (ii) at 822-823, add: 'where S2 is printed, step 1's exception reads it as not firing' (or 'as evaluated'), whichever is intended.

**The skeptic's evidence.** The finding holds. I read the fenced block in docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md (lines 702-904).

- **Step 1 (S5):** "If F-prop's sum is strictly lower, the rule STOPS (S5) - unless step 2 picks (b) and S2 does not fire, (b) having no fallback". So whether S5 fires depends on whether S2 fires in the same run.
- **Step 3 (S2):** "the rule STOPS (S2) if that method's |N| is strictly larger than the other's in every swing cycle of D." This test is computed inside each reading's run, because step 6 says "Steps 1-5 are run again under each alternative".
- **Reading (ii):** "D less C1 - {C2, C4} ... the step-2 verdict and S5 are compared; S2, S3 and S6 are compared only where this D has at least two swing cycles, and otherwise printed". With D = {C2, C4}, C4 is the level cycle, so C2 is the only swing cycle. S2 is therefore "printed", but S5 is "compared".
- **The outcome paragraph:** the outcome is "the step-2 verdict ... with the set of the other stops that fire", and "S4 compares outcomes less S4 and S7".

**Where the text is silent.** "Printed" decides whether S2 is part of (ii)'s compared outcome. Nothing in the block says whether a printed S2 still counts as firing for step 1's exception.

**The failing path is reachable:**
1. The main run over {C1, C2, C4} picks (b).
2. In that run, (b)'s |N| is larger in C2 but not in C1, so S2 does not fire.
3. F-prop is strictly lower, but the exception applies, so S5 does not fire. The main outcome is "(b)".
4. Reading (ii) picks (b), and F-prop is strictly lower over C2's and C4's redrawn state-cycles.
5. S2's test holds over C2 alone. Read literally ("the rule STOPS (S2)"), S2 fires, so the exception no longer applies and S5 fires. S5 is compared, so (ii) gives "(b), S5" against "(b)", and S4 stops the rule.
6. A build that treats a printed S2 as an untrusted, non-firing stop ("since each would rest on one") leaves S5 suppressed. The outcomes match, and the rule decides (b).

So two faithful implementations disagree on whether the rule decides. That is a genuine ambiguity, but the term itself is defined and nothing in the block contradicts it, so it is minor, not a defect. The literal reading has an odd side effect: an S2 that rests on one cycle, which (ii) says to print only, still reaches the compared outcome through S5.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** In reading (ii), after "and otherwise printed, since each would rest on one", add one clause that settles it. Either "; a printed S2 counts as not firing in step 1's exception", or "; step 1's exception reads S2 as its test gives, printed or not". The first matches the stated rationale that S2 resting on one cycle is not trusted.

### 4. K's class is undefined for a state with a seat won outside REP and DEM; edit 2 added O_s to W_s and |N| only

- **Lens:** edits - **reviewer:** minor - **skeptic:** minor
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:779

**The scenario.** K (779-780) matches the record's class (REP majority, DEM majority, tied) 'under some resolution of the state's undecided seats'. Under S7's first scoring, the formula default, O_s is not undecided. Example: in C5, a 3-seat state's record is R 1, D 1, O 1, and a row gives REP 2, DEM 1. Build A classes the record by REP against DEM, so it is tied and K does not match. Build B classes it by a majority of the seats, so it fits no class and never matches. Under the second scoring the seat is resolvable and REP's resolution matches. K feeds step 2's condition K(b) >= K(a) in every cycle of D plus C5, step 5's switch conditions, and the decisive digest's K figures. So the step-2 verdict recorded with an S7 ruling, and the digest, can differ between builds. W_s and |N| are the same for every method up to a constant, so K is the only figure in which the two scorings can part.

**The fix proposed.** Inside the fence at 779-780, add: 'with O_s > 0, the class counts REP against DEM seats alone under S7's first scoring; under the second, O_s's seats are resolved as undecided ones.'

**The skeptic's evidence.** The finding is real, but its example is wrong. In its 3-seat case the record is R1 D1 O1 and a row gives REP 2 DEM 1. Build A (REP against DEM) calls the record tied, and Build B (majority of all seats) gives it no class. A row always fills all n_T seats (line 719: DEMhat_s = n_T - REPhat_s), and a 3-seat row cannot be tied, so neither build matches. The two builds agree in that case.

A case that does split them: a 4-seat state whose record is R2 D1 O1. Build A calls it a REP majority (2 > 1). Build B gives it no class (2 of 4 is not a majority, and R != D). Say (a) gives R3 D1 and (b) gives R2 D2. Under Build A, (a) matches and (b) does not. Under Build B, neither matches. So step 2's fourth condition, K(b) >= K(a) in every cycle of D (line 797), fails under A and holds under B. Build C counts O_s as undecided seats, which is what S7's second scoring does (lines 774-775).

Text: lines 779-780 say "K = the delegations, of 50, whose class (REP majority, DEM majority, tied) equals the record's under some resolution of the state's undecided seats." W_s says O_s > 0 "is S7's first scoring" (774), and |N| counts O "under S7's first scoring" (777-778). No such line covers K. "Undecided seats" is Z_s, and Z_s leaves O_s out (768-770) until the second scoring moves O_s into Z_s.

Reachability: O_s > 0 can only happen in C5, because 2016-2024 fail the truth gate (770, 833). C5 is measured into the run over D plus C5, where "every D of this block reads D plus C5" (898-899). S7 fires there (831), so that run does not decide. Its outcome still holds the step-2 verdict (838), and the RULED bar compares that outcome with the ruled one (885-887, 900-901). K is also one of the decisive digest's figures (875-878). So two faithful builds can record a different step-2 verdict and a different digest. That makes it minor, not a defect, since the rule can still reach every state it promises.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Inside the fence, after line 780, add: "With O_s > 0, under S7's first scoring a class compares REP's seats with DEM's alone; S7's second scoring resolves O_s's seats as undecided ones." Also replace the finding's example with a 4-seat state whose record is R2 D1 O1, where (a) gives R3 D1 and (b) gives R2 D2. The original 3-seat example does not split the builds.

### 5. |N| under S7's first scoring counts the third-party seat twice when a method gives it to REP, unlike W_s's first scoring

- **Lens:** edits - **reviewer:** minor - **skeptic:** minor
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:777

**The scenario.** W_s's first scoring is (|dR| + |dD| + O_s - Z_s)/2. That equals the second scoring's distance from [R_s, R_s + Z_s + O_s] plus O_s, the same for every method, as S7's option 'misplaced for every method alike' (863) says. N's first scoring is 'the signed distance from [R, R + Z]', and '|N| also counts the seats in O'. Read literally, that is |dist| + O. Example: national R 215, D 219, O 1, Z 0. A method giving the independent's seat to REP has N = +1 and |N| = 2. A method giving it to DEM has N = 0 and |N| = 1. W_s scores both as 1. In C5, a swing cycle of D plus C5, step 3's S2 and step 4's S3 compare |N|, so they favour the DEM-leaning method. The digest holds signed N, which does not carry the O at all.

**The fix proposed.** At 777-778: 'under S7's first scoring |N| is the distance of REP's national seats from [R, R + Z + O], plus O' (the second scoring's |N| plus O, as for W_s). Say which form the digest holds.

**The skeptic's evidence.** The terms in the fenced block define W_s = (|REPhat_s - R_s| + |DEMhat_s - D_s| + O_s - Z_s) / 2, and every row sets DEMhat_s = n_T - REPhat_s. Take Z = 0 and REPhat = R + a, so that DEMhat = D + O - a. Then W_s = (|a| + |O - a| + O)/2, which is O for every a in [0, O]. Outside that range it is O plus the distance from [R, R+O]. So W_s is the distance from [R_s, R_s+Z_s+O_s] plus O_s. That is the same for both parties, which matches S7's option "the seat scored as misplaced for every method alike" and "S7's second moves O_s into Z_s".

N is defined differently: "N = the signed distance of REP's national seats from [R, R + Z], positive = REP over; under S7's first scoring |N| also counts the seats in O." The interval is [R, R+Z] and leaves O out, so the third-party seat counts as if DEM had won it. Take the finding's case: R 215, D 219, O 1, Z 0. A method that gives the seat to REP has 216 seats, so N = +1 and |N| = 2. A method that gives it to DEM has 215 seats, so N = 0 and |N| = 1. Adding the constant O does not remove this difference. The difference is real, because no method can give the seat to anyone but REP or DEM: DEMhat = n_T - REPhat.

It can actually be reached. S7 applies only when a cycle after 2024 has a third-party winner, which in practice means C5. Steps 3 and 4 compare |N| in every swing cycle of D, and in the C5 run D reads D plus C5. S2 and S3 are part of THE OUTCOME, which RULED compares run by run. So the asymmetry can change whether S2 or S3 fires and favours the method that leans DEM. The case is narrow: S7 fires whenever this happens, so that run never decides on its own. But the reported S2/S3 set, and any ruling that picks S7's first scoring ("misplaced for every method alike"), would rest on an N that does not score the seat alike. This contradicts the wording of the S7 option, not the comparison in a reachable decided state. It is graded minor rather than defect because the run is already stopped by S7.

The digest holds signed N measured against [R, R+Z], which carries the same lean. Since O is a constant from the data, this does not make the digest ambiguous, but the digest holds the asymmetric form.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** Define N against the interval that W_s uses: "N = the signed distance of REP's national seats from [R, R + Z + O], positive = REP over; under S7's first scoring |N| is that distance plus O, under its second it is that distance alone (O moved into Z)." Where O = 0 this is the same as the current text, so 2016-2024 does not change. Then say that the digest holds this signed N, so |N| under either scoring follows from it and O.

### 6. Prose above the fence still speaks of a single outcome checked after an amendment

- **Lens:** edits - **reviewer:** note - **skeptic:** note
- **Where:** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:700

**The scenario.** Line 700 (outside the fence) says 'the ruling record is checked against the new outcome'. After edit 7 the record holds an outcome per run, under both ASKED and RULED (867-870, 882-886).

**The fix proposed.** '... the ruling record is checked against each run's new outcome.'

**The skeptic's evidence.** Line 700 is outside the fence (BEGIN marker at 702, END at 904). It ends: "After an amendment the instrument runs again and the ruling record is checked against the new outcome." It uses the singular, but the block now keeps one outcome per run. ASKED holds "the outcome of each run asked" (867-868) and passes while "each run's outcome equal[s] the registered ones" (880-881). RULED holds "the outcome of each run it was made on (the run over D and, once C5 is measured, the run over D plus C5 ...)" (868-870) and passes while "each run's generated outcome equals that run's outcome in the ruling" (883-884). The C5 paragraph says "Either run giving an outcome other than its own in the ruling" (899-900). So once C5 is measured there are two runs and two outcomes, and the prose's "the new outcome" no longer matches the block's per-run wording. Nothing fails because of this. The prose sits outside the fence and has no force, the block's bars govern, and "the new outcome" could be read as a collective noun, so an implementer would still do the same thing. It is only a wording drift left behind by edit 7, with no contradiction and no unreachable state.

**The skeptic's corrected fix.** At line 700, outside the fence, change "the ruling record is checked against the new outcome." to "the ruling record is checked against each run's new outcome." The fenced block's digest is unaffected.

## The third pass - refuted by the skeptics


## What was done about the third pass

Six findings survived, none refuted; all six were acted on with the reviewers' own wording, and the block's digest was taken after them.

- **A run the ruling was not made on (1).** A ruling made before C5 is measured holds no outcome for the run over D plus C5; that run now fails the bars until R-US18 is asked again with the new table, and the new ruling records every run - the only form under which the record ever comes to hold that run's outcome.
- **Step 1 over C5's redrawn states (2)** is compared with the result recorded in the ruling, not with the ruled fallback (which step 1 can never return as F-prop), and the record stores it only where the ruled option counts them by (a)'s fallback.
- **Reading (ii)'s printed S2 (3)** counts as not firing in step 1's exception.
- **A seat won outside the two parties in K and N (4, 5):** a class compares REP's seats with DEM's alone under S7's first scoring; N is measured from [R, R + Z + O], and |N| adds O under the first scoring only, so the seat is counted once.
- **The prose above the block (6)** checks each run's new outcome.

The block was then fixed: 209 lines, its digest preregistered in `COMPLETED.md` §791.
